# Distribution of the diagonal mass in criterion 210

162 already separated distinct pairs and the diagonal in another sum over primes. The additional information here is a joint scaling law for the specific weight of 210. Let D_N be the p=q part of E_N. In each summand mark the coordinates (u,y) with y=u log p/√N. The diagonal measure converges weakly, without RH, to

    1_{u≥3,y≥0} du/u⁴ · y exp(−y²/2)dy/(2π).

In particular D_N→1/(162π). The marginal in y, once normalized, describes the diagonal fraction contributed by the primes up to exp(Y√N/u): it tends to 1−exp(−Y²/2) for each Y≥0.

**Envelope.** By scaling c_N(t;u)=u c_N(ut;1). For k=0,1 and j=0,2 one has ||∂^j(τ^k H₁^N)||₁≪N^((j−1)/2) for N≥4. In neighbourhoods of ±1, log H₁(±1+h)=−h²+O(h³), its derivative is O(|h|) and its second derivative is bounded. Integrating the Gaussian bounds after differentiating gives the result. On the complement H₁≤r<1 and the product formula leaves a factor r^(N−4) times an integrable function independent of N, with prefactor O(N²), using the rational decay at infinity. Integrating by parts twice gives

    |c_N(t;u)|+|c'_N(t;u)|/u ≪ (u/√N)(1+u|t|/√N)^−2.

**Transfer of primes.** At fixed u test against ψ(y) smooth, bounded with bounded derivative, initially with compact support or ψ=1. Write the uncut sum as an integral against dθ(x) with weight x⁻¹ t |c_N(t;u)|² ψ(ut/√N), t=log x. On replacing dθ by dx the error by parts is dominated by the integral in dt of |θ(e^t)/e^t−1| multiplied by

    Cψ [(1+t+tu/√N)|c_N|²+2t|c_N c'_N|].

For fixed u the second factor has integral O_u(1) and tends uniformly to zero on compacts. PNT gives error o_u(1). The cutoff of 210 has log X_N proportional to N for fixed u. The same integration by parts in its tail, with θ(x)≪x, gives o_u(1) by the envelope, including the boundary term t|c_N|². To dominate in u one uses the positive uncut sum and ψ=1: the envelope and integration with θ(x)≪x give O(1+u) (integrals of |c_N|², t|c_N|² and t|c_N c'_N| respectively O(u/√N), O(1), O(u)). This is integrable with du/u⁴.

**Profile.** At each peak, writing τ=±1+w/√N the profile H₁^N converges in L¹(dw) to exp(−w²) after first restricting to the neighbourhood of the peak, by the Gaussian bound; outside both neighbourhoods the rescaled mass decays exponentially. Transforming one obtains, uniformly in y, an additive error o(1) in

    √N c_N(√N y;1)=exp(−y²/4)cos(√N y)/√π+o(1).

The continuous integral in t is ∫ y N |c_N(√N y;1)|² ψ(y)dy. On compacts use the profile and the weak average 1/2 of cos²; in the tail Cy(1+y)^−4 dominates. One obtains the stated marginal for each u. Domination in u gives the joint law first for continuous product tests of compact support (by smooth approximation), and then for all bounded continuous tests by convergence of the total mass. The integral in u equals 1/81.

**Open obligation.** For C_N=E_N−D_N, 210 implies that a uniform upper bound along a subsequence would suffice. Under RH one requires C_N→−1/(162π). The proved law localizes only the p=q mass; it does not authorize discarding the primes with log p≫√N/u in the distinct pairs. In particular 162 and falsifier 167 prevent interpreting the diagonal separation by itself as a new mechanism for the sign.

**Falsifier for the truncation suggested by the diagonal.** In fact, for every fixed Y>0, cutting the sum A_N at L=Y√N/u (in log p) destroys even the local control at verified rates. Call B_N that short sum. For fixed u, integrating against dθ(x) the weight x^(−1/2)c_N(log x;u), PNT gives

    (√N/u)e^(−L/2) B_N(u)
      = exp(−Y²/4)/√π · Re[exp(iY√N)/(1/2+iu)] + o_u(1).

Indeed the boundary error on replacing dθ by dx, in this normalization, is O_u(|θ(e^L)e^(−L)−1|). In the integral of the error by parts, the envelope for c_N and c'_N gives a majorant C_u exp(−v/2) in v=L−log x≥0, multiplied by |θ(x)/x−1| which tends to zero for fixed v. For the integral against dx, the preceding Fourier profile at t=L−v has phase Y√N−uv and amplitude tending to exp(−Y²/4)/√π in the same normalization after removing exp(−v/2); integrating this complex exponential gives the denominator.

Taking N the integer nearest to (2πm/Y)², the phase tends to zero modulo 2π and the limiting real coefficient is positive for EVERY fixed u. On [3,4] the full A_N tends to zero by 209 and the verified absence of zeros at those ordinates. By contrast B_N diverges there at least like c_u exp(Y√N/(2u))/√N along that subsequence. Thus the tail A_N−B_N cancels a term exponential in √N even at verified rates, although its fraction of diagonal mass tends only to exp(−Y²/2), arbitrarily small by choosing Y large. Fatou applied to |B_N|² on [3,4] also gives divergence of the truncated average along that subsequence. The argument rules out inferring boundedness of the short average from diagonal concentration. The growth exhibited is subexponential in N; it does not contradict a root-one rate bound for the short sum.

### A positive band in the ratio of the primes creates exponential growth

Fix L>0, w_L(v)=(1−|v|/L)_+ and a_p=(log p)c_N(log p;u)/√p with the original cutoff X_N(u) of 210. On 3≤u≤3.1 replace |Σ a_p|² by
    Q_{N,L}(u)=Σ_{p,q≤X_N} a_p a_q w_L(log(p/q)).
The diagonal stays exactly the same, the weight is positive semidefinite and only admits pairs with e^(−L)<p/q<e^L. Nevertheless there exist r>1 and c_L>0 such that for large N
    ∫₃^3.1 Q_{N,L}(u)du/u⁴ ≥ c_L r^(2N)/N.
The original energy on this same interval tends to zero by 209–210. Compared with 162–165, the full longitudinal cutoff of 210 is kept here: the artificial growth comes from attenuating the pairs by their ratio.

Indeed Q is the integral of |Σ a_p exp(iT log p)|² against the density (L/2π)sinc²(LT/2), with sinc(x)=sin(x)/x. In particular it dominates the same integral of the square of the sum with cosine. For u in the preceding interval and T close to T₀(u)=√(u²−1/4), that real sum is
    Re[H_u(T+i/2)^N]+O(N+1)
uniformly. To see this use the explicit formula of 208 with the even test [H_u(τ−T)^N+H_u(τ+T)^N]/2, as in the translation of 228. Its two polar terms add up to 2 Re[H_u(T+i/2)^N]. On the real line the archimedean term costs O(1) by uniform integrability of H_u(τ±T)log(2+|τ|). For the zeros, |γ±T|>10 in a sufficiently small fixed neighbourhood, since |γ|>14. The modulus formula of 208 gives there |H_u(γ±T+iδ)|≤r₀<1 uniformly for |δ|≤1/2 (for example use |H_u(z)|≤4u²|z|²/(|z|²−u²)²). The sum of its first absolute powers is bounded by the unit count and the tail O(|γ|^(−2)). The cutoff at X_N costs O(1) by 208 since the cosine has modulus ≤1. Removing prime powers costs O(N+1): for squares use sup|c_N|=O(1) and Σ_{p≤√X_N}(log p)/p≪log(2X_N)=O(N+1), and for higher powers the series converges. Dividing by the factor two of the prime formula gives the claim.

For z=T₀+i/2 one has |z|=u, u²+z²=2T₀ z and therefore H_u(z)=u²/T₀²>1, real positive. On |T−T₀|≤η/N, a local branch of log H has uniformly bounded derivative: choosing η small the phase of the power stays in [−π/4,π/4] and its modulus is at least a constant times (u²/T₀²)^N. The density sinc² has only isolated zeros and T₀ varies strictly with u. There is therefore a closed subinterval of positive length where the density near T₀ has a positive floor. Integrating over that strip of width 2η/N proves the exponential floor with r=min_{[3,3.1]}u²/(u²−1/4)>1. The computation identifies the polar term that reappears. A proof by ratio bands that uses the uncentred prime datum must preserve the compensation with the discarded pairs.

### Polar repair and detection with only distinct prime pairs of ratio close to one

Let B=sup_ρ |Reρ−1/2|. Choose any 0<l_N≤1 with log(1/l_N)=o(N), for example l_N=exp(−N/log(N+2)). Fix a real, even, smooth function φ with compact support in (−1/2,1/2) and integral one. Use g_N(t)=exp(−t²/(4N))/√(4πN) from 228, and define
    d_N(t,T)=(1/4−∂_t²)[g_N(t)cos(Tt)],
    s_p(T)=(log p)/√p · d_N(log p,T),     p≤exp(2N),
    w_N(T)=l_N |φ̂(l_N T)|²,     φ̂(v)=∫φ(t)exp(−ivt)dt,
    C_N=Σ_{p≠q} ∫ s_p(T)s_q(T) w_N(T)dT.
The sum uses simple primes and predetermined coefficients. Then
    lim log(1+|C_N|)/(2N)=B².
In fact if B>0 the balance C_N is positive for all large N. Only pairs with |log(p/q)|<l_N have nonzero coefficient. An upper ceiling exp(o(N)) along an unbounded subsequence would suffice. This criterion does not prove such a ceiling.

Exact support. The differential gives (g_N/4−g_N''+T²g_N)cos(Tt)+2Tg_N' sin(Tt). After forming a product, the integrals are combinations of derivatives up to order four of the rescaled autocorrelation of φ, evaluated at log(p/q) and log(pq). All vanish outside (−l_N,l_N); log(pq)≥log 4>l_N. Removing the diagonal costs at most exp(o(N)): on t≤2N the coefficients g_N,g_N',g_N'' cost O(N^(−1/2)), and Σ_{p≤exp(2N)}(log p)²/p≪N² by Chebyshev. Integrating (1+T²)² against w_N loses O(1+l_N^(−4)).

Proof of the exponent. The even spectral test is the average of exp(−N(τ±T)²) multiplied by τ²+1/4. It vanishes at ±i/2 for each T. In the explicit formula of 228 the two polar terms disappear before squaring and without subtracting a continuous density. By symmetry, the zero side is equivalent to
    b_N(T)=Σ_ρ (z²+1/4) exp(−N(z−T)²),    z=Imρ−i(Reρ−1/2).
The difference b_N(T)+2Σ s_p(T) is O(poly(N)(1+|T|)^C) for a fixed C, uniformly. Indeed the archimedean integral is bounded by Gaussian integration against (1+|τ|)²log(2+|τ|). Removing the squares costs O(poly(N)(1+T²)) by the sum of (log p)/p up to exp N, and the higher powers converge absolutely for the weight p^(−k/2). For the tail of all powers at t>2N, the derivatives cost the Gaussian weight times O((1+T²)(1+t/N)²); group into unit intervals and use ψ(x)≪x and exp(t/2−t²/(4N))≤exp(−(t−2N)/2). Thus the error has L²(w_N) norm at most exp(o(N)).

By counting zeros in unit intervals, |b_N(T)|≪(1+|T|)^C exp(NB²) for N≥1. The fixed-order moments of w_N are exp(o(N)), giving the ceiling, even when B=0. For the floor with B>0, fix 0<b<B and follow the parabola argument of 228: on a small compact interval a single pair (γ,|δ|) maximizes δ²−(γ−T)² with value >b² and a fixed gap to the rest. Such an interval exists because on a compact set only finitely many parabolas can compete and distinct ones cross at most once. The two conjugate terms of the dominant pair have amplitude 2m |(γ−iδ)²+1/4|, strictly positive, and phase linear in T with slope of modulus 2N|δ|. Its cosine has modulus ≥1/2 on a fixed fraction of the interval for large N. If l_N→0 then w_N(T)≳l_N there by φ̂(0)=1. For general sequences, extract from any subsequence one with l_N→l∈[0,1]; when l>0 the interval can be narrowed avoiding the isolated zeros of φ̂(lT). In both cases the cost in mass is exp(−o(N)). The remaining sum keeps the exponential gap by zero counting and the Gaussian tail. The energy of the complete prime sum dominates exp(2Nb²−o(N)) by the triangle inequality in L². The diagonal is subexponential, so the floor persists in the distinct pairs. Let b→B.

Comparison: 228 centred the Gaussian and used the Cauchy weight, without compact support in ratios; 230–231 already detected the deviation on local prime intervals, in 231 by fitting a line. Here the polar cancellation is differential and gives a single sum over distinct pairs in a ratio band that can shrink like exp(−o(N)). The preceding triangular example still applies to the uncorrected weight.

### Size of both signs and effective cancellation in the ratio band

Take l=1/N in the polar repair. For each distinct ordered pair first integrate its coefficient in T; call P_N and Q_N the sum of the positive coefficients and of the magnitudes of the negative ones, respectively. Then C_N=P_N−Q_N. With
    J(v)=∫ y⁴ |φ̂(y)|² cos(yv)dy,
    c_φ=√(2π)/(8π) · ∫max(J(v),0)dv >0,
one obtains unconditionally
    P_N ∼ Q_N ∼ c_φ N^(5/2) exp(N/2).
Moreover, |P_N−Q_N|/(P_N+Q_N) ≪ exp(−c√N) for some c>0. These are statements about the true primes, after integrating the kernel of each pair.

Proof of the masses. Write t=log p, s=log q, v=(t−s)/l. On 0≤s,t≤2N, the ratios g'_N/g_N and g''_N/g_N are uniformly bounded. The differential formula of 211 and the trigonometric products give for the integrated kernel, without the factors (log p)(log q)/√(pq),
    g_N(t)g_N(s)[l^(−4)J(v)/2+O(l^(−3))]  if |v|≤1,
and zero outside. Indeed only the product of the T²cos terms in both variables has degree four. The channels in t+s are zero by support. J is smooth with compact support in (−1,1), with J(0)>0 and ∫J=0 by inversion at frequency zero. So its positive and negative parts have equal, strictly positive integral. The positive-part function is Lipschitz with constant one, also when applying the formula to the negative of the kernel.

Justification of the replacement by the density. Divide t,s into cells of length N^(−4) on [N/2,3N/2], adjusting the endpoints into comparable cells. In each cell [h,h+Δ] the PNT with classical remainder gives Σ log p/√p=(1+o(1))∫e^(t/2)dt with uniform relative error O(Δ+exp(−c√N)/Δ), by partial integration or by bounding 1/√p by its endpoints. In each rectangle the g vary by a factor 1+O(Δ) and J_± by O(Δ/l). In those errors only the cells with |t−s|≤l+2Δ intervene. The continuous mass with weight g_N(t)g_N(s)e^((t+s)/2) over that band is O(l exp(N/2)/√N) by completing the square. So both replacements and the O(l^(−3)) of the kernel cost o(l^(−3) exp(N/2)/√N) in the final mass. Outside the central interval group into unit cells and use θ(x)≪x: only neighbours at distance O(1) intervene, and t−t²/(2N)=N/2−(t−N)²/(2N) gives a saving exp(−cN) even counting the pairs of whole cells. The diagonal costs only a power of N as in the preceding section.

In the remaining integral change s=t−lv. For |v|≤1 the factors in s can be evaluated at t with relative error O(l) on the central interval. The integral in t of exp(t)g_N(t)² is asymptotic to exp(N/2)√(2πN)/(4πN) by the Gaussian centred at N. The Jacobian l and the factor l^(−4)/2 give the announced constant. The computation also localizes the masses of both signs at log p=N+O(√N) in the sense of Gaussian concentration (take a growing multiple of √N to capture a fraction tending to one).

For the signed saving, work before squaring with Σ s_p(T). Replace dθ(x) by dx and bound the difference by integration by parts with the PNT remainder. The cost is ≤poly(N)(1+|T|)^C exp(N/4−c₁√N): the derivative of the weight on passing to t is dominated, after incorporating the remainder, by poly(N)(1+|T|)^C exp(t/2−t²/(4N)−c₂√t) on [log 2,2N], and the same bounds hold at the endpoints. The Gaussian exponent has maximum N/4 at N and loses an amount proportional to N outside [N/2,3N/2]. On the other hand the continuous integral ∫exp(t/2)(1/4−∂²)[g_N(t)cos(Tt)]dt reduces to endpoints only by two integrations by parts; at both it is O(poly(N)(1+|T|)^C), even at 2N where exp(N)g_N(2N)=O(N^(−1/2)). Squaring, integrating against w_N, whose fixed moments grow polynomially, and removing the diagonal, also polynomial, gives the relative saving after absorbing polynomial factors. In particular the exponent of the regularized fraction log((1+|P_N−Q_N|)/(P_N+Q_N))/N tends to 2B²−1/2 by the preceding section. For the sign a balance exp(o(N)) is missing; the equality of the main terms and the proved saving do not establish it.

### Three distance bands with fixed sign via a finite difference that kills the poles

Fix d=1/N, l=d/10 and in the preceding construction choose φ even, nonnegative, smooth, of integral one and compact support in (−1/2,1/2). Instead of differentiating twice use the multiplier
    m_d(τ)=2[cosh(d/2)−cos(dτ)]/d².
It vanishes exactly at ±i/2. Write b₀=2cosh(d/2), b₋₁=b₁=−1 and R(v)=∫|φ̂(y)|²cos(yv)dy. By autocorrelation R≥0 and it is supported in (−1,1). For j=0,1,2 form three nonnegative sums G_j(N) over distinct ordered primes p,q≤exp(2N), setting t=log p, s=log q. The weight of each pair in G_j is
    (log p)(log q)/(2d⁴√(pq)) ·
    Σ_{a,b∈{−1,0,1}, |a−b|=j} |b_a b_b| g_N(t+ad)g_N(s+bd) R((t−s+(a−b)d)/l).
The letter b of the inner index is an integer; b_a and b_b are the preceding coefficients. For all large N, G_j uses only ||log(p/q)|−jd|<l. The three bands are disjoint. With B from 228,
    lim log(1+|G₀−G₁+G₂|)/(2N)=B².
If B>0 the balance is eventually positive. Hence it would suffice to prove G₀+G₂≤G₁+exp(o(N)) along an unbounded sequence. The previous filter of 211 had weights whose sign depended on the integrated profile; here each admitted distance determines exactly the sign of the pair, without approximation of derivatives and without counting primes in the gaps between bands.

Proof. The substitute for d_N(t,T) is d^(−2)Σ_a b_a g_N(t+ad)cos(T(t+ad)). Integrate products against l|φ̂(lT)|². The half corresponding to t+s+(a+b)d vanishes since log 4−2d>l for large N. The difference half gives exactly the formula above with sign (−1)^j. Subtracting the diagonal one obtains G₀−G₁+G₂. Its cost is polynomial by d^(−1)=N and the bounds of the preceding section. The same explicit-formula argument applies: on the line and in the strip |Im τ|≤1/2 one has |m_d(τ)|≪N², the archimedean and power contributions are polynomial, and the three shifts of size d do not affect the tail bound at t>2N (for t>2N the exponent t/2−(t±d)²/(4N) starts at O(1) and decreases with slope ≤−1/3). For the spectral floor, m_d(z)→z²+1/4 uniformly on compacts and m_d respects conjugation. On the dominant parabola of 211 the amplitude stays separated from zero, and the phase shift constant in T does not change the fraction of the interval with cosine of modulus ≥1/2. So the limit is preserved, with the same argument for B=0.

Size of the three groups. If H_N=l d^(−4) exp(N/2)√(2π)/(8π√N) · ∫R(v)dv, then
    (G₀,G₁,G₂)/H_N → (6,8,2).
In the rectangles of the preceding proof, replace the profile J_± by the smooth profile R translated to the five centres (b−a)d. In the central band of the Gaussian integral, shifting s and the arguments of g_N by O(d) changes the smooth factors by 1+O(d), uniformly in t≤2N; the arguments of R are kept unchanged by setting s=t+(a−b)d−lv. The same N^(−4) mesh, the same tail ceilings and the same polynomial removal of the diagonal serve for each of the nine positive readings. Each reading contributes asymptotically |b_a b_b|H_N and the sums of those coefficients by j tend to 6,8,2. The equality 6+2=8 only cancels the main terms. The additive inequality at subexponential precision among the three groups remains open. The antecedent 163–165 already treats the aggregated near and far sign in another detector; the feature of this filter is a partition into three explicit positive bands that reproduces exactly the full exponent.

### One difference, two bands, all primes in [X,2X]

Set t=log X, h=1/log(2+t), a=log(3/2), and freeze h when integrating in v. Use the positive density W of 240 (convolution of boxes, integral one and support in [0,1/10]), W_h(y)=W(y/h)/h. Write
    S(u)=Σ_p (log p) W_h(log p−u),
    F(v)=S(t+v+h)−exp(h)S(t+v),    0≤v≤a,
    E(X)=X^(−1)∫₀ᵃ F(v)²dv.
All primes lie in [X,2X] for X sufficiently large. On expanding and removing p=q there remain exactly one positive sum P (distance |log(p/q)|≤h/10) minus another positive sum Q (distance ||log(p/q)|−h|≤h/10), with the weights of the expansion and factor X^(−1). Both use only distinct pairs. The diagonal is O(t/h) by ∫W_h²=O(1/h), the PNT and Σ_{X≤p≤2X}log² p=O(Xt). Thus
    lim log(1+|P−Q|)/log X = 2B.
If B>0, P−Q is eventually positive. A one-sided ceiling P≤Q+X^o(1) along an unbounded sequence would suffice. 241 already used the first difference for two counts and 242 for sparse samples. The strict separation of the two bands in the same dyadic interval uses the narrow kernel and the integral of its square.

Spectral justification. The continuous integral of the count at rate u is exp(u)H(h), where H(s)=∫W(y)exp(sy)dy. Its first difference vanishes exactly. The formula of 239–240 applied to the scaled kernel gives for F the sum over zeros with coefficients −mult(ρ)H(hρ)(exp(hρ)−exp h) and exponentials exp(ρ(t+v)), with error O(exp(t/2)poly(t)). Indeed the explicit formula over all powers uses the same pole and zeros with H(hs); the trivial zeros cost O(1) by the nonnegative support of W and large t, and removing powers ≥2 costs O(exp(t/2)poly(t)/h) by counting integers. The multipliers satisfy |H(hρ)|≪exp(−c h|Imρ|/log²(3+h|Imρ|)) at large heights in the scaled variable; for bounded scaled heights O(1) suffices. The sum of moduli of multipliers counted with multiplicity is polynomial in t by the unit count. It follows that E(X)≤exp(2Bt+o(t)).

For the lower bound with B>0 fix a zero ρ₀ of abscissa β₀>1/2, and σ between 1/2 and β₀. In the isolation filter of 240 use fixed support [0,a] and G=t(log t)^5: the tail above G after normalization to β₀ costs o(exp(−t)) since hG/log²G≫t. The other zeros with Reρ≥σ up to G are t^(q+o(1)), q=3(1−σ)/(2−σ)<1 by the density bound used in 240. Kill them with the differences of that memo, with steps a/[10(G+1)(n+1)] for n zeros, and convolve with a uniform of length a/4. The complex density f has mass one, support [0,a] and L² norm ≤exp(o(t)); no factors for polynomials are needed. In ∫f(v)exp(−ρ₀(t+v))F(v)dv the term of ρ₀ survives, of modulus asymptotic to mult(ρ₀)h|ρ₀−1|, while the zeros of low abscissa and the arithmetic error cost exp(−(β₀−σ)t+o(t)). By Cauchy–Schwarz E(X)≥exp((2β₀−1)t−o(t)). Passing to the supremum and removing the polynomial diagonal proves the limit. Under B=0 the ceiling and the regularization by 1 suffice.

### Direct arithmetic attack: the quadrature over the primes already meets the bound

Let p⁻,p⁺ be the primes adjacent to p and b_p=(p⁺−p⁻)/2. For u in the two preceding ranges one has uniformly
    Σ_p b_p W_h(log p−u) = exp(u)H(h)+O_ε(X^(−1/8+ε)).
In particular the whole first difference with the geometric weights b_p costs O_ε(X^(−1/8+ε)). Proof: apply the trapezoidal rule on each interval between primes to f(x)=W_h(log x−u), extended by zero. On the union where f or its derivatives intervene, |f''|≪X^(−2)h^(−3). The relevant gaps have left endpoint ≤2X and (for large X) are contained in [X/2,3X] by PNT. The error is ≤C sup|f''|Σg³. The second-moment bound for gaps of 232 gives Σg³≤(Σg²)^(3/2)≪_ε X^(15/8+ε). This proves the estimate after absorbing the powers of log t. Compared with the regression of 232, here the approximation is uniform in the centre u and directly uses the same primes in both summands of F.

Hence the root-mean-square bound for the first difference with the signed weights r_p=log p−b_p suffices. Passing to moduli before summing loses a power: for X sufficiently large
    ∫₀ᵃ Σ_p |r_p| |W_h(log p−t−v−h)−exp(h)W_h(log p−t−v)|dv ≫ X/log X.
Indeed the two supports are disjoint in v for each prime. In [t+a/4,t+3a/4] there is a subinterval of fixed absolute length (independent of t) at distance ≥c>0 from the integers; it suffices to remove the neighbourhoods of radius a/32 of the integers. For its primes b_p is an integer because p⁻ and p⁺ are odd. Thus |r_p|≥c and the unshifted term has integral of W_h equal to one. By PNT there are ≫X/log X primes in that subinterval, uniformly in its endpoints since it lies in a single octave and has fixed logarithmic length. A root bound in L²(dv) on the absolute majorant contradicts this floor by Cauchy–Schwarz. The remaining problem is a signed cancellation in the difference, not the precision of integration between the primes.

### The detector admits uniformly small residues at each prime, even next to large gaps

The trapezoid residues log p−b_p can have size comparable to that of a gap. To eliminate this dependence without suppressing any prime, one can choose another positive quadrature on exactly the same nodes. One obtains weights r_p with
    sup_{X≤p≤2X}|r_p|/log p=o(1),
and replacing log p by r_p in F(v) costs only O(X^(1/3)h^(−3)), uniformly in v. The r_p/log p take a single value per arithmetic half-cell, common to all windows of the detector. Hence the L² norm normalized by √X changes by o(1).

Construction. Divide [X,2X] into n=⌈X^(1/3)⌉ equal cells of length H=X/n; assign the endpoints to a single cell. In each cell J split into two halves and compute the masses μ_i=Σ log p and the barycentres m_i=Σ p log p/μ_i, i=1,2. Let z be the midpoint of J. The replacement masses are
    A₁=H(m₂−z)/(m₂−m₁),   A₂=H(z−m₁)/(m₂−m₁).
Assign to each prime of half i the weight γ_i log p with γ_i=A_i/μ_i. Both masses are positive, add up to H and have first moment H z. The PNT in short intervals implies uniformly μ_i∼H/2 and m_i=z±H/4+o(H), with minus sign in the first half. Indeed, on any fixed fraction of the cell the length is comparable to X^(2/3), within the range of the theorem; subdividing into a fixed number of equal parts and then letting that number grow approximates the first moment in rescaled coordinates. Thus γ_i=1+o(1) uniformly. Primary source for the short PNT, with more than enough margin: Guth and Maynard, https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20552 .

For f(x)=W_h(log x−u), subtract on each cell its affine Taylor polynomial at z. The two positive masses, discrete and continuous, integrate that line exactly. The remainder per cell costs O(H³ sup|f''|), and on summing it is O(XH² sup|f''|)=O(X^(1/3)h^(−3)). The functions of the two counts are supported in [X,2X] and the continuous integral of the weighted difference is zero by 107. Taking r_p=(1−γ_i)log p gives the statement. If a cell starts at a prime the endpoint convention does not affect the equivalents. The computation requires masses and first moments of the true primes, without lengths of individual gaps. Unlike the trapezoid of 121, the geometric model keeps each weight close to the arithmetic weight and still reproduces the continuum below root precision.

Scope. The short PNT controls qualitatively the maximum of |1−γ_i|. Summed in modulus it only gives o(X) here for the reading: on the logarithmic support of length O(h) the prime mass costs O(Xh) by short PNT and the weight O(1/h). It remains to prove sufficient cancellation among the signed errors of the half-cells to reach X^(1/2+o(1)) in L² along some unbounded sequence. The construction does not claim that the sum of the original residues restricted to the large gaps is negligible.

Additional antecedents: 219 and 226 already use positive adjustments that preserve moments. What is specific here is the uniform relative control per half-cell and the sub-root error for this difference.

### Cancellation at root scale between two disjoint readings by rank blocks

Let F(v) be the difference of 107. In each octave X=2^j one can form two readings F₁,F₂ without a single common prime, with X^(1/3+o(1)) primes each. For the following stratified choice, almost surely in all sufficiently high octaves one has ∥F_i−F∥₂/√X=o(1) for i=1,2. So there is already sub-root control on the difference between the two readings. The cross product keeps the exponent 2B without subtraction of the diagonal:
    lim log(1+|X^(−1)∫₀ᵃ F₁(v)F₂(v)dv|)/log X=2B   almost surely.
The probability here is only over the indices chosen within lists of true primes. The same limit holds deterministically for the median of the cross products, taking first the median of the signed product and then the modulus. Both the random version and the median still require an arithmetic bound on the common component.

Construction and estimate. Fix k=⌊X^(2/3)/(log X)^4⌋ and split the ordered primes of [X,2X] into successive blocks of k, except the last one of k'≤k. In each block of size m≥2 draw an ordered pair of distinct indices, uniformly and independently across blocks. In reading i replace that block by the prime chosen at position i, with weight m log p instead of log p. Omit any final block of size one, whose uniform error costs O((log X)/h). All other contributions are unbiased. By the short PNT used in 131, any interval of length 4k log X starting in [X,2X] contains at least k primes for large X, even if it crosses 2X. In particular the blocks, including the incomplete final one, have diameter O(k log X). There are O(X/(k log X)) blocks by PNT.

As a function of x∈[X,2X], the per-prime reading
    (log x)[W_h(log x−t−v−h)−exp(h)W_h(log x−t−v)]
has derivative O((log X)/(Xh²)), uniform in v. The variance of the contribution of one block to a reading is at most m² times the square of the oscillation on that block. Summing by independence across blocks and integrating over the fixed interval of v one gets
    E ∥F_i−F∥₂² /X ≪ k³(log X)³/(X²h⁴) + (log X)²/(Xh²)
                        ≪ (log X)^(−8)
for large X. The dependence between i=1 and i=2 within the same block does not affect the marginal bounds. Markov at threshold ∥F_i−F∥₂/√X>(log X)^(−1) gives probability O((log X)^(−6)), summable over octaves. Borel–Cantelli gives the simultaneous statement without needing independence across octaves.

For the normalized product, if A=∥F∥₂/√X and both normalized errors are ≤η, Cauchy–Schwarz places it between A²−2ηA−η² and A²+2ηA+η². The probability of this strip tends to one also octave by octave, so it eventually contains any median with η=1/log X. If B>0 then A=X^(B+o(1)); under RH, A≤X^o(1). This proves the limit and preserves the one-sided criterion. The expansion of the product has exactly the same two bands of 107, since the positive factors m alter no support or sign. Unlike deleting the diagonal of a self-form with amplified weights, in this product a term p=q never appears. The true order by blocks is preserved; primality is not modelled randomly. The compression is of the reading: knowing the ranks or generating the selected primes still has an arithmetic cost.

### A signed balance of the prime readings at nearby scales, decided at relative precision

Let F_X(v;k)=Σ_p (log p) f(pk), where f(y)=W_h(log y−t−v−h)−exp(h)W_h(log y−t−v), t=log X and h is that of 107, frozen for all k. In particular F_X(v;1)=F(v). Take K=(log X)^4 and a Gevrey-2 function w in the ordinary variable, equal to one up to 1 and zero from 2, with 0≤w≤1 (for example integrate a rescaled box density of 239). Uniformly for 0≤v≤a one has
    Σ_{k≤2K} (−1)^(k−1)w(k/K)F_X(v;k)
      = (η(1/2)/4 + o(1)) h √X exp(v/2),
    η(s)=Σ_{k≥1}(−1)^(k−1)k^(−s)  (Re s>0).
The sign of this combination is positive for X sufficiently large. Compared with the factorial recurrence of index 36172, the smooth cutoff uses only a polylogarithmic factor between scales and the main term of the prime squares is kept for this test.

With all powers, first form the sum without w. The identity Σ_{d|m}Λ(d)=log m gives exactly Σ_m [log m−2·1_{2|m}log(m/2)] f(m), with the convention of applying the second term only on even numbers. It is an alternating reading of (log m)f(m) plus 2log 2 times the reading over even numbers of f. The first term is O_D(X^(−D)) for each fixed D by summation of differences of sufficiently high order, since the support has length O(X) and the derivative of order j costs O_j(X^(−j) polylog X). The second reading differs from (log 2)∫f(y)dy by O_D(X^(−D)) by Poisson on the mesh 2, with the same derivatives. The integral is exactly zero by 107.

To introduce w with error O_D(X^(−D)), fix n and set Y=X/n. The support of f(nk) requires k comparable to Y, with absolute constants. If (1−w(k/K)) intervenes then Y≳K. The derivative of order j of the product in k costs at most h^(−1)(C/(Yh))^j(j!)² on the whole support. Indeed the box construction of 240 gives |W^(j)|≤C^(j+1)(j!)² by the product of the inverses of its first widths and the sup norm of the next one. Composition with log(k/Y) preserves this ceiling for k/Y in a positive compact: in the formal Taylor formula of order j, the coefficient of degree j in the i-th power of the incremental series of the logarithm costs C^j binom(j−1,i−1). Multiplying by j! |W^(i)|/(i! h^i) and summing gives the bound; the derivatives of w intervene only where Y≍K. Applying j differences to the alternating sum gains 2^(−j) and costs in modulus O((Y+j) sup|derivative of order j|) by the integral formula for differences. Taking j=⌊c√(Yh)⌋ with c small gives O((Y/h)exp(−c'√(Yh))). Since √(Kh)/log X→∞, summing over n≪X/K with Λ(n)≤log(2X) costs O_D(X^(−D)). The truncated sum over all powers is therefore negligible.

It remains to subtract the prime powers. For the square, at each k≤2K the classical PNT applied at scale √(X/k) and Abel give for Σ_p(log p)f(kp²) its integral in dp with error O(√(X/k) polylog X exp(−c√log X)), uniformly in v. The integral is
    (1/2)√(X/k) exp(v/2) H(h/2)[exp(h/2)−exp(h)].
The weighted sum of k^(−1/2) tends to η(1/2)>0 by alternating summation and variation of the cutoff; H(h/2)→1. The powers ≥3 cost O(X^(1/3) polylog X) even summing all k of the cutoff by moduli. Changing the sign of the squares term proves the equivalent.

Limitation for the closure. A mode X^ρ collected at scales X/k gets multiplied by Σ(−1)^(k−1)w(k/K)k^(−ρ). For each fixed nontrivial zero, this multiplier is O_ρ(exp(−c√K)), because η(ρ)=(1−2^(1−ρ))ζ(ρ)=0. In detail, apply j differences to (1−w(y/K))y^(−ρ) and bound the sum by its integrated derivative of order j (or by dyadic pieces). In the transition it costs O_ρ(K^(1−Reρ)(C_ρ/K)^j(j!)²); for y≥2K the derivatives of the monomial give the same integrated ceiling for j≥2. It is justified in the tail by alternating convergence and finite shifts; choose j proportional to √K. Hence the sign decided in this combination is compatible with a large exterior mode in F_X(v;1). The obligation on the individual reading remains open.

### Classification and cost of repairing the recurrence with another periodic filter

Let q be fixed, c(k) real of period q, even chosen anew in each octave, and D_c(n)=Σ_{d|n}Λ(d)c(n/d). Measure Δ(c)=max |c(i)−c(j)| over pairs with the same greatest common divisor with q. For X sufficiently large as a function of q:
    inf_{A_r,B_r} Σ_{X<n≤2X} |D_c(n)−A_{n mod q}log n−B_{n mod q}| ≥ c_q X Δ(c).
The affine backgrounds can be fitted by class IN that octave. If Δ=0, the minimal error is exactly zero. The result only restricts methods that pay in moduli the remainder of that substitution.

For Δ=0, by finite inversion on divisors c(k)=Σ_{d|q,d|k}α_d. Thus D_c(n)=Σ_{d|q,d|n}α_d log(n/d); the Dirichlet multiplier is ζ(s)Σ_{d|q}α_d d^(−s). This includes the alternating filter of 164. For Δ>0 first subtract a filter of that type taking one representative value per gcd class. Its convolution can be absorbed into the background, leaving ∥c∥∞≤Δ and preserving a pair m₁,m₂≤q with difference Δ. There are invertible classes a₁,a₂ modulo q such that m₁a₁≡m₂a₂≡r: on dividing by the gcd g, the resulting classes are units modulo q/g and can be lifted to units modulo q by the Chinese remainder theorem. For a prime p≡a_i, p∤m_i,
    D_c(m_i p)=c(m_i)log(m_i p)+d_i,
    d_i=Σ_{d|m_i}Λ(d)c((m_i/d)a_i)−c(m_i)log m_i.
This uses that the only divisors with nonzero Λ are p and the prime powers dividing m_i. In particular |d_i|≤2Δ log(2q). The two lines in the variable log n are ≥Δ log X/2 apart over the whole octave, for X large uniformly in the weights. Against any line fitted at r, at each point at least one differs by ≥Δ log X/4. The set corresponding to each line is a union of at most two intervals in log n. One of the four has length ≥(log 2)/4. On that interval the PNT in the fixed progression a_i modulo q provides ≫_q X/log X values m_i p, uniformly in the endpoints (physical length comparable to X). Summing them proves the bound, without assuming anything about the size of the fitted slopes.

In particular, for a fixed nontrivial zero ρ, the continued multiplier C(s)=Σ c(k) k^(−s) satisfies |C(ρ)|≪_{q,ρ} Δ(c). Indeed remove the preceding gcd part and then the mean of what remains; both parts are multiples of ζ in the indicated sense and vanish at ρ. The remaining sequence has sup norm ≤2Δ and prefixes of modulus ≤2qΔ, so Abel gives the bound in Re s>0. If an absolute substitution error ≤X^(1/2+o(1)) is required, then Δ≤X^(−1/2+o(1)). A fixed mode X^ρ with Reρ<1 leaves, after multiplying by C(ρ), size o(√X). Hence neither allowing X-dependent weights nor fitting slope and intercept per class resolves the conflict, as long as q is fixed and the error is controlled in this way.

The statement characterizes the substitution at the level of coefficients, without concluding that its signed remainder against the test of 107 has that cost. A possible source of arithmetic information would have to use cancellation in that remainder, or leave the classified class. The bound for the individual reading has not been obtained.

### Signed remainder with a single negative class and removal of all its small divisors

Set q=2+⌊log(2+log X)⌋, K=q(log X)^4 and c(k)=1_{k≡1 mod q}−1/q. On the octave [X,2X] form entirely arithmetic weights
    R(n)=(Λ*c)(n)−c(n)log n
        =Σ_{d|n}Λ(d)[1_{n/d≡1 mod q}−1_{n≡1 mod q}].
Each summand is nonpositive at n≡1 and nonnegative elsewhere. The sign here is that of the coefficient before applying the window difference f of 164. Let J(v)=Σ_n R(n)f(n). Then
    lim log(1+X^(−1)∫₀ᵃ|J(v)|²dv)/log X=2B.
Unlike the alternating balance of 164, each fixed exterior zero is retained. Moreover, in each of the two parts (inside and outside class 1) one can remove ALL d≤X/K with error O_D(X^(−D)) in the signed reading against f, for each fixed D, uniformly in v. For d>X/K the powers of order ≥2 cost in total X^(1/2+o(1)) even by moduli, since k=n/d≤2K and the weighted count of powers ≤2X is O(√X polylog X). Keeping only the primes p>X/K, each n≤2X has at most one for large X. So at root precision one can use only n=kp, k≤2K, with the same explicit signs. The incidence k=1 by itself keeps exactly the primes outside class 1.

To remove the small divisors, fix d and write n=dk. With or without the class restriction on n, the restricted bracketed coefficient is a function of k periodic modulo q and bounded by one. Its mean multiplied by ∫f(dy)dy is exactly zero by 107. The discrete error is estimated by Poisson in each class of k, with Y=X/d≥K. The derivative of order j of f(dy) costs h^(−1)(C/(Yh))^j(j!)² on a support of length O(Y), as in 164. After integrating by parts at each nonzero frequency m/q and summing the classes one obtains O((Y/h)exp(−c√(Yh/q))) choosing j of order √(Yh/q) and summing |m|^(−j). Since √(Kh/q)≫log X, these errors multiplied by Λ(d) can be summed for d≤X/K. This step uses the cancellation between the two windows; it does not estimate each n in moduli.

For the exponent, the reading of c(n)log n costs O_D(X^(−D)) by the same argument at scale X: the zero frequency disappears by the zero mean of c. In the reading of Λ*c introduce w(k/K) of 164 with error O_D(X^(−D)): the product with 1−w has Y≳K and now the mean of c is zero, without needing the integral of the product to vanish. For each k≤2K apply the explicit formula of 107 to the count of powers at X/k, keeping h and q frozen. Its continuous pole vanishes. One obtains the same expansion as there with extra multiplier L_X(ρ)=Σ c(k)w(k/K)k^(−ρ), ceiling O(K) in the strip and aggregated errors ≤X^(1/2+o(1)). For each fixed zero of abscissa β, the prefixes of c have modulus ≤1; Abel gives L_X(ρ)=C_q(ρ)+O_ρ(K^(−β)) where C_q(s)=Σc(k)k^(−s) in Re s>0. At a zero of ζ one has
    C_q(ρ)=1+q^(−ρ)[ζ(ρ,1+1/q)−ζ(ρ)],
    |C_q(ρ)−1|≤|ρ| ζ(β+1)q^(−1−β).
Indeed write the series of the integers 1 modulo q from its second term and continue; the difference of the two zetas in brackets is the absolutely convergent series of (m+1/q)^(−s)−m^(−s). Thus L_X(ρ)→1 without any hypothesis on zeros shared between L-functions. In the isolation argument of 107 the ceiling O(K) is absorbed into exp(o(t)) and the multiplier of the chosen zero tends to one; the same lower and upper bounds hold. Under B=0 the regularized ceiling suffices.

This overcomes the loss of sensitivity of 164 at the price of keeping the signed remainder of 182, instead of paying it in moduli. It remains to estimate the window difference on those products kp with p large. The sign of R per class does not decide that of J.

### Pointwise transfer through the two neighbouring classes of the multiples

In the notation of 164 fix the integer q=⌈X^(2/9)⌉ and I(k)=1_{k≡1 or −1 mod q}. Set R_q=Λ*I−I(n)log n. Uniformly in v one has the direct arithmetic comparison
    Σ R_q(n)f(n) = Σ Λ(n)f(n) + O(X^(1/3)h^(−3)).
This is proved without evaluating the filter at the zeros. In particular, if C_q is the reading restricted to the COMPOSITES and A_q the prime reading Σ_{p≡±1(q)}(log p)f(p), then
    C_q−A_q = −(1/4+o(1)) h√X exp(v/2).
This sign is unconditional. In C_q the coefficients of n≡±1 are nonpositive and those of the other classes nonnegative, before applying f. The equivalent decides a pointwise difference between the composite sector and two prime progressions; it does not decide the complete prime reading.

Proof. For 3≤q≤√X isolate k=1 in Λ*I. The remaining cofactors are the pairs qm−1,qm+1 for m≥1. After adding and subtracting twice the term at qm, the difference between the two readings of the first equality is
    Σ_{m,d≥1}Λ(d)[f(d(qm−1))+f(d(qm+1))−2f(dqm)]
      +2Σ_m(log m)f(qm)−Σ_{n≡±1(q)}(log n)f(n).
The first term costs O(X q^(−3)h^(−3)): in each second difference use d² sup|f''|≪d² X^(−2)h^(−3), and only d≤CX/(qm) can contribute. Chebyshev gives Σ_{d≤Y}Λ(d)d²≪Y³ and the series Σm^(−3) converges. In the other terms, Poisson on the three progressions replaces the sums by their integrals with error O_D(X^(−D)) for each fixed D, uniformly in q≤√X: the integrations by parts of order j give one power of q/X per derivative with polylogarithmic losses for fixed j. The combined integral is −(2log q/q)∫f=0. This gives the transfer, even with error o(h√X) for q≥X^(1/6+ε).

For p prime, R_q(p)=(1−I(p))log p. Subtracting this part leaves exactly C_q−A_q equal to the reading of the powers of exponent ≥2 plus the transfer error. For the squares, the argument of 164 with k=1 gives (√X/2)exp(v/2)H(h/2)(exp(h/2)−exp h) plus o(h√X), by the ordinary PNT; the powers of exponent ≥3 cost O(X^(1/3)polylog X). This proves the constant and its sign. The improvement over 197 is to distinguish point by point the bias of the squares against the two classes, at a power modulus, while also keeping the exponent of the detector. For the global sign a root ceiling for the total prime count is still missing, or an independent comparison implying it.

### Stable inversion of the second difference and a one-sided bound with positive weights

Fix f(z)=W(log z) for z>0 with W from 240. It is nonnegative, smooth and supported in [1,exp(1/10)]. H(s)=∫f(z)z^(s−1)dz is the function of 240, nonzero in Re s>0. For large y take q=max(3,⌈y^(1/4)⌉). This time the isolated cofactor 1 of 215 is NOT included. Form the single nonnegative sum
    A(y)=Σ_{m,n≥1}Λ(n)[f((m−1/q)n/y)+f((m+1/q)n/y)].
These are the two neighbouring classes of the multiples of q, at scale qy, with m≥1. The following one-sided ceiling, required for all sufficiently large y and all ε>0, is equivalent to the global sign:
    A(y) ≤ 2y[H(1)log y+H'(1)] + 2ζ(3)yH(1)/q² + O_ε(y^ε).
The second explicit correction is of order √y. The criterion does not assume the inequality holds for all y from a single finite computation.

To see the difference with respect to the annihilator of 164, set g(z)=z²f''(z), L(y)=ΣΛ(n)g(n/y) and U(y)=q²[A(y)−2Σ_k(log k)f(k/y)]. The centre is evaluated by Σ_{n|k}Λ(n)=log k. Symmetric Taylor with remainder of order four gives
    U(y)=Σ_{m≥1}m^(−2)L(y/m)+O(y/q²).
Indeed the second derivative contributes (n/y)²f''(mn/y) and the fourth costs O(n⁴/(q²y⁴)). Only n≤Cy/m can intervene, and Σ_{n≤Cy/m}Λ(n)n⁴≪(y/m)^5 by Chebyshev. Summing m^(−5) gives the error O(√y). By Poisson, 2Σ(log k)f(k/y)=2y[H(1)log y+H'(1)]+O_D(y^(−D)) for every fixed D. Since ∫g=2H(1), on centring L by 2yH(1) precisely the term 2ζ(3)yH(1) appears for U.

The operator remaining in the second difference has multiplier ζ(s+2), with no zeros in the strip, and inverse with coefficients μ(m)/m². In the norm sup_y |L(y)|/√y the scales m≥2 cost at most Σ_{m≥2}m^(−5/2)=ζ(5/2)−1<0.35. For the uncentred version the inversion is locally finite by support; in y≥1 the Taylor errors on inverting cost O(√y+1), also using the ceiling O(1) at bounded scales. In the centred version the series of the linear term converges absolutely. So the loss of precision when solving for the reading of one scale is controlled.

To justify that the one-sided ceiling suffices, write U_c=U−2ζ(3)yH(1). The Mellin transform in y of U_c over y≥1 is, initially in Re s>1,
    ζ(s+2)s(s+1)H(s)(−ζ'(s)/ζ(s))−2ζ(3)H(1)/(s−1) + E(s),
with E holomorphic in Re s>1/2. This follows from the Taylor identity, the support at small scales and two integrations by parts in the Mellin transform of g. The singularity at 1 is removable, and there are no real singularities in s>1/2. Each zero with Reρ>1/2 leaves a genuine simple pole even if it is multiple. If the proposed ceiling holds, for σ>1/2 one has U_c(y)≤C_σ y^σ. The Mellin transform of C_σ y^σ−U_c(y), nonnegative for large y, cannot have a real abscissa of convergence >σ: by Landau's theorem that abscissa would be singular, and the preceding continuation is regular on the real interval (σ,∞). Hence there can be no pole of U_c in Re s>σ. Let σ↓1/2. Conversely under RH, ψ(x)−x=O(√x log²(2x)) gives L(y)−2yH(1)=O(√y log²(2y)) for y≥1 by Abel. The series in m and the Taylor error keep that ceiling for U_c; dividing by q² proves the stated ceiling.

Independent control achieved. The classical PNT in the same Abel argument gives O(y exp(−c√log y)) for centred L. In the series separate m≤√y from the rest, using the trivial ceiling O(y/m) in the tail (also at small scales after centring). For A(y) this yields, after the two explicit terms, an error O(√y exp(−c'√log y)) for large y. This certifies the second correction at relative precision, without reaching the required remainder y^ε. The contraction is a property of the inversion step; it is not a contraction of the error A(y) relative to the main term.

Precision on the antecedent and the converse: 069 already used Landau's one-sided argument with a nonvanishing dilation multiplier. What is particular here is the neighbouring positive sum with an evaluable integer centre and Taylor remainder at root level. Under RH the converse can be improved to O(1) for the error of A: in the smoothed explicit formula of L, the coefficients ρ(ρ+1)H(ρ) are absolutely summable by the decay of 240 and the unit count, so L(y)−2yH(1)=O(√y) for large y (the trivial terms are bounded by support and smoothness). The series in m keeps that ceiling and q²≥√y.

### Convex weight and a floor only over primes in disjoint teeth

Take f(z)=(1−z)_+ in the second difference, now at fixed modulus 6. Set P(x)=ΣΛ(n)f(n/x), L(x)=Σ(log n)f(n/x) and b(v)=(1−|v|)_+. Convexity gives the exact identity
    P(x)+R(x)=L(x)−L(x/2)−L(x/3)−L(x/6),
    R(x)=Σ_n Λ(n)(n/x) Σ_{m≥1} b(x/n−6m) ≥0.
Indeed the 6m±1 run through the integers coprime to 6 except 1. Subtract the centre twice, use inclusion–exclusion and the divisor identity of 230. For each n the second difference of the piecewise-linear weight only sees the break at 1 and equals (n/x)b(x/n−6m). Each n intervenes in at most one tooth. In 195:7557 and 7931 tents and the three-quarter power scale already appear; here the neighbouring contribution is positive term by term and the detector becomes a floor on the mass of the teeth.

By sum–integral comparison with variation O(log(2x)), the factorial side equals c₆ x/2+O(log(2x)), with c₆=(log 2)/2+(log 3)/3+(log 6)/6>1. Let R_pr be the same R using only simple primes. It already suffices to prove FOR ALL large x
    R_pr(x) ≥ (c₆−1)x/2 − O_ε(x^(1/2+ε))   for every ε>0.
Indeed this one-sided bound implies P(x)≤x/2+O_ε(x^(1/2+ε)). The Mellin transform of P has factor (−ζ'/ζ)(s)/[s(s+1)]; the term x/2 removes the pole at 1. Landau as in 230 excludes the poles to the right of 1/2. Conversely under RH the integrated explicit formula gives P(x)=x/2+O(√x) (the series of multiplicities times 1/|ρ(ρ+1)| converges); removing powers from R costs O(√x log²(2x)) by counting. The computation does not deduce the required floor from R_pr≥0 alone.

Part of the floor that is indeed reached at root scale. With M=⌊x^(1/4)⌋, the teeth m>M of R_pr contribute
    (1+o(1)) x/(432 M²) = (1/432+o(1))√x.
More generally the first equivalent holds for x^α with M=⌊x^α⌋ and any fixed α in (0,1/4]. For M<m≤TM, T>1 fixed, in each tooth x/p=6m+v with −1≤v≤1. The length in p of any part of fixed length in v is comparable to x/m², and p≍x/m. Even at α=1/4 one has x/m²≍p^(2/3) with constants depending on T. By the short PNT of 131, uniformly in these m, the weighted prime mass of the tooth is (1+o(1)) times
    x∫_{−1}^1 b(v)(6m+v)^(−3)dv ∼ x/(216m³).
For uniformity split [−1,1] into a fixed number of subintervals, use the extremes of the weight and then refine the partition. For m>TM the support requires p≤x/(6TM−1) and each prime contributes at most (p/x)log p. Chebyshev bounds the tail by O(x/(TM)²). Summing the equivalents, then sending T to infinity, gives the constant. In particular removing without compensation all the teeth m>x^α with α<1/4 discards a positive mass of exponent 1−2α>1/2. The actual contribution is known to precision o(√x) over the whole critical tail; the deficit relative to the density in the inner teeth remains open.

### Cancellation between two scales: removal of all primes up to x^(4/5)

Let c=(c₆−1)/2 and let R_pr be that of 252. Use in R_pr(x) and in R_pr(x/2) THE SAME cutoff on the prime, p>x^(4/5), denoting those parts by R₊(x;x) and R₊(x/2;x). Unconditionally
    R_pr(x)−R_pr(x/2)/2 = R₊(x;x)−R₊(x/2;x)/2 + O_ε(x^(1/2+ε)).
Hence it suffices to prove for all large x the floor
    R₊(x;x) ≥ R₊(x/2;x)/2 + 3cx/4 − O_ε(x^(1/2+ε)).
Both sums are nonnegative and use only primes in (x^(4/5),x/5]. To recover the original floor, set D(x)=R_pr(x)−cx and iterate D(x)≥D(x/2)/2−O_ε(x^(1/2+ε)) towards smaller scales. The errors form a geometric series. Under RH D(x)=O_ε(x^(1/2+ε)) by 252, so the criterion remains equivalent.

Proof of the removal. For p≤x^(4/5) the arguments are large and the period-6 tent can be completed with all integers m. Let T(v)=Σ_{m∈ℤ}b(v−6m). The joint weight is (p/x)[T(x/p)−T(x/(2p))]. The mean 1/6 disappears exactly by the choice of the factor 1/2. For p≤x^(3/4) Chebyshev suffices. On a dyadic block P<p≤2P in the rest, possibly trimmed, the Fourier series of T has coefficients O((1+|j|)^(−2)). The factor 1/2 in the argument only changes constants in the phase scale F₀≍x/P. Sums with Λ can be used, since removing higher powers in these blocks of the reading costs ≪P^(3/2)polylog(P)/x, below root.

We detail the saving over the proof in 195:7941. At each frequency j≥1 apply Vaughan with U=V=P^(1/3), as in the type I/II decomposition of 195:7848. In type I the short variable is ≤P^(2/3), the long one has scale length L≥cP^(1/3). The second derivative in the long variable has modulus comparable to jF₀/L². The relative bound, after summing the short variable, is
    ≪_ε P^ε [P^(−1/3)√(jF₀)+(jF₀)^(−1/2)].
The divisor or logarithm coefficients in the short variable and the partial summation of a logarithm in the long one are absorbed into P^ε. The bounds are uniform in the truncation endpoint of the product.

In type II orient the two variables so that M≥L, ML≍P, L≳P^(1/3). Apply Cauchy in the long variable, open the square in the short one. For two integers of the short variable at distance r≥1 the difference phase is a constant times 1/m in the long one, with monotone derivative of modulus comparable to jF₀ r/(LM). If jF₀≤c₀√P for c₀ sufficiently small, that modulus stays below 1/2: the FIRST derivative bound gives O(ML/(jF₀ r)) on the common product interval. Summing r and the diagonal one obtains the relative bound
    ≪_ε P^ε [L^(−1)+(log(2L))/(jF₀)]^(1/2).
For j greater than c₀√P/F₀ use the trivial ceiling. This separation is valid because √P/F₀ grows like a power over the whole band. After summing with j^(−2), the total relative cost of both types is
    ≪_ε P^ε [P^(−1/3)√F₀+F₀^(−1/2)+P^(−1/6)+F₀/√P].
The last term is the whole harmonic tail. Abel incorporates p/x at cost O(P/x), so the relative bound must be multiplied by P²/x. The limiting term gives P^(5/2)/x^(3/2)≤√x for P≤x^(4/5); the other three give respectively P^(7/6)/√x, P^(11/6)/x and √P, all below root with margin. The dyadic sum enters into ε. This proves the estimate for the complete signed contribution up to the cutoff. The same error is not claimed for R_pr(x) separately: there the arithmetic mean (1/(6x))Σ p log p remains. The floor between the sums of larger primes is still pending.

### Symmetric distribution over teeth and scale of the continuous tail

Write B_s(x)=Σ_p (p/x)log(p)b(x/p−s), b(u)=(1−|u|)_+. Exactly, for the preceding difference WITHOUT truncating the primes,
    R_pr(x)−R_pr(x/2)/2 = B_6/2 + Σ_{k≥1} H_k,
    2H_k=B_{12k−6}+B_{12k+6}−B_{12k−1}−B_{12k+1}.
Indeed b(u/2)=b(u)+(b(u−1)+b(u+1))/2. The even centres cancel; splitting each odd centre in halves between two neighbours leaves half an initial copy. The arithmetic sum is finite. The identity persists with any common cutoff on p.

In the continuous reading without cutoff, B_s is replaced by x F(s), F(s)=∫_{−1}^1 b(v)(s+v)^(−3)dv (s≥6). For a=12k, exactly
    2H_k^{cont}/x=∫_1^6∫_{−t}^t F''(a+u) du dt.
Since F''(s)=12∫b(v)(s+v)^(−5)dv, each cell is positive and H_k^{cont}∼210x/(12k)^5. Hence Σ_{k>K}H_k^{cont}∼(105/(2·12^5))x K^(−4) as K→∞; in particular the whole continuous tail costs O(√x) from K=⌈x^(1/8)⌉. The equivalents refer to the complete continuum, without introducing into F a discontinuity from a cutoff in p. This second-order cancellation in the continuous masses does not give convexity of the prime masses: for the global inequality an aggregated lower bound is required for the differences between prime and continuous readings, including the initial half-cell. The PNT for each fixed k does not provide its error at root level.

Independent control of the sign parts. The refinement identity leaves positive tents centred at 12k−6 and negative ones at 12k±1 (weight 1/2), with no overlap of interiors between signs. Even with p>x^(4/5), the negative mass in absolute value is (C₋+o(1))x, C₋=(1/2)Σ_{k≥1}[F(12k−1)+F(12k+1)]>0. For each fixed centre use PNT by partial summation; for k>K the total mass is O(x/K²) by Chebyshev and the support p≪x/K. Two integrations by parts give F(s)=1/[s(s²−1)]. Already the centre 11 contributes x/2640+o(x). So compensation between distinct primes is needed even in the preserved band.

### Uniform prime sign of the initial cells and scope of their centred floors

There exist d>0 and X such that for x≥X all prime cells H_k with 12k≤exp(d√log x) are strictly positive, simultaneously. In that range no support touches p≤x^(4/5) for large x. By the classical PNT with error θ(y)−y=O(y exp(−c√log y)), Abel gives
    |B_s−xF(s)| ≪ (x/s²)exp(−c₁√log x)
uniformly for 6≤s≤2 exp(d√log x). Indeed the weight (y/x)b(x/y−s) is piecewise linear, of height 1/s and variation 2/s; on its support y≍x/s and log y≥(log x)/2. The continuous margin of the cell is ≫x/k⁵ by the formula for F'' of 289. The relative error is therefore ≪k³exp(−c₁√log x)=o(1) choosing 3d<c₁. This gives the uncentred sign on a growing number of cells.

By contrast, requiring the CENTRED floor H_k≥H_k^{cont}−O_{k,ε}(x^(1/2+ε)) for each k of any fixed unbounded set already implies RH, even allowing constants without uniformity in k. Proof: for z fixed in the open strip let F_z(a)=∫ b(v)(a+v)^(−z−2)dv and let J_k(z) be the half-difference of the four values F_z at the centres of 289. By double integration of F_z'' as there,
    J_k(z) ∼ (35/2)(z+2)(z+3)(12k)^(−z−4)
as k→∞ for each fixed z. In particular every fixed exterior zero ρ is visible in each sufficiently distant cell. The Mellin transform in x with weight x^(−z−1) of the prime reading extended to powers is (−ζ'/ζ)(z)J_k(z) for Re z>1 by the substitution v=x/n−a. Adding powers costs O_k(√x log²x). Subtracting xJ_k(1) on [1,∞) removes the pole at 1. If there were a pole at ρ with Reρ>1/2, choose k in the set with J_k(ρ)≠0 and 1/2<σ<Reρ. The assumed floor allows adding Cx^σ and obtaining an eventually nonnegative function whose Mellin transform has a pole at ρ but is regular on the whole real line to the right of σ: contradiction with Landau's theorem on the real abscissa of convergence. So requiring a centred estimate per cell at infinitely many scattered indices would still require the complete target. The preceding uncentred sign is obtained without that hypothesis.

### Finite prefix that retains the signal of each zero at bounded height

Let M_K(z)=F_z(6)/2+Σ_{k≤K}J_k(z) with the preceding definitions. The complete multiplier is
    M_∞(z)=(1−2^(−z−1))[(1−2^(−z)−3^(−z)−6^(−z))ζ(z)−1]/[z(z+1)].
Indeed z(z+1)F_z(a)=(a−1)^(−z)−2a^(−z)+(a+1)^(−z); sum over a=6m for Re z>1, apply the scale difference and continue. Therefore at every exterior zero ρ=β+it the modulus of M_∞ is ≫(1+|t|)^(−2) uniformly.

For K≥C(1+|t|), the tail satisfies |M_∞−M_K|≪(1+|t|)K^(−β−3), uniformly in 1/2≤β≤1. Indeed the representation via F_z'' of 302 gives integrals over shifts |u|≤7 of the product (z+2)(z+3) times Σ_{k>K}(12k+u)^(−z−4), with bounded weights independent of z,K. On N<k≤2N the phase −t log(12k+u)/(2π) has monotone derivative of modulus comparable to |t|/N and less than 1/2. By first derivative and Abel it costs O(N^(−β−3)/|t|) if |t|≥1; otherwise use the absolute sum. Sum dyadically. Thus K=⌈A(1+T)⌉ with A absolute and sufficiently large keeps at least half the modulus of the complete signal at all exterior zeros of height ≤T simultaneously.

For β≥1/2+δ, δ>0 fixed, even K=⌈A_δ(1+T)^(1/(1+2δ))⌉ suffices. If K<C(1+|t|), split the tail into K<k≤C(1+|t|) and the part already treated. For each centre now use the exact second difference z(z+1)F_z above. Each endpoint sum on a block N<k≤2N (trimming allowed) is O(N^(−β)(√|t|+N/√|t|)) by second derivative, since |phase''|≍|t|/N², and Abel. The first contribution summed is O(√|t| K^(−β)); the second is O_δ(|t|^(−δ)log(2+|t|)) using β≥1/2+δ, for 0<δ<1/2. This bounds the error after multiplying by |z(z+1)|. The first term is made small by choosing A_δ large, and the second tends to zero. Bounded heights are absorbed by increasing A_δ. One thus obtains a centred floor sufficient to exclude exterior zeros in the rectangle considered: the root-level inequality for the single prefix M_K at all large x would suffice, by Landau as in 302. This quantification refers to the Mellin signal; it does not establish that arithmetic floor for the prefix.

### Two moments at a single break of the prefix

Fix K and call S(x) the prime prefix of 312, m=M_K(1)>0. If w_a is the total coefficient of B_a in the prefix, set c_d=w_{d-1}−2w_d+w_{d+1} (zero outside the support), D=12K+7. By 289,
    V(x)=xS(x)−mx²=Σ_{d,p} c_d log(p)(x−dp)_+ −mx².
It is a strictly concave parabola between breaks. For n>D² there can be a single pair (d,p) at a break n=dp: if the primes were distinct each would divide the opposite cofactor ≤D while p>D. At an interior minimum one must have c_d>0 and
    0≤A(n)−2mn≤c_d log p,     A(n)=Σ_e c_e θ(n/e).
The first inequality uses the right derivative; the second the left one. Moreover, with B(n)=Σ_e e c_e Σ_{q≤n/e, q prime} q log q, one has there
    V(n)=mn²−B(n)+O_K(n log n).
To exclude an exterior zero the slope of the barrier must be included. Given a visible zero ρ and 1/2<σ<Reρ, Landau applied in both orientations as in 302 gives oscillations of both signs of S(x)−mx relative to x^σ with arbitrary constants (adding powers costs O_K(√x log²x)). There are therefore, arbitrarily far out, two positive points of V enclosing one with V(x)<−x^(1+σ). The cofactors with positive jump are exactly 12j+5,12j+7 (0≤j≤K) and 12k±1 (1≤k≤K), by the stencil of c_d.

Set W(x)=V(x)+x^(1+σ). On every interval without a positive jump W is strictly concave for large x, since σ<1. Minimizing W between the two preceding positive points gives a negative minimum at a unique break with
    0≤A(n)−2mn+(1+σ)n^σ≤c_d log p.
There V(n)<−n^(1+σ). In that strip the condition W(n)≥0 is exactly equivalent to
    B(n)≤nA(n)−mn²+n^(1+σ).
If this inequality is proved at all sufficiently large selected breaks, for each 1/2<σ<1, every visible zero is excluded. The criterion uses two explicit sums at points with a strip of width O_K(log n) for the first count. Its antecedent 195 (minima at primes) uses another functional. There is no estimate here of the arithmetic second moment at those contacts.

### What can be hidden from all local counts of the contact

Sensitivity test of criterion 322. Fix 1/2<α<1, a large scale n and two disjoint compact intervals I<J in (1/6,1/5), of positive length. To the measure Λ add total mass n^α on nJ distributed proportionally to log p among the primes of nJ, and subtract the same mass by the same rule on nI. The opposite perturbation is also allowed. Only simple primes are altered and their weights remain positive: by PNT their relative variation is O(n^(α−1)). The original jump at every dp=n is preserved, since n/p∈(5,6) on the altered support.

There exists η>0 depending only on I,J such that for all |x/n−1|<η and every integer e≥1 the sums of weights up to x/e stay exactly the same. Indeed 5<x/p<6 for the altered primes, so both bands enter fully for e≤5 and neither for e≥6. This preserves A(x) on that whole neighbourhood. It also preserves there, simultaneously for every integer r≥1, the complete factorial datum Σ_h Λ(h)⌊x/(rh)⌋=log(⌊x/r⌋!): on the two bands ⌊x/(rp)⌋=⌊5/r⌋. Hence the test probes even the family of local factorial constraints, much more than the value of A at one point.

By contrast the shift of B on the whole neighbourhood equals 5c₅ n^α(mean_J p−mean_I p) ≍ n^(1+α), with the chosen sign; the means are weighted by log p. Here only e=5 has nonzero coefficient among e≤5, and c₅>0. The two models thus differ by more than n^(1+σ) for any σ<α while satisfying exactly the same cited data. It is an obstruction to reconstructing B at root precision from those observations and from positivity. It is not a counterexample for the real weights, nor does it show that one of the models crosses the ceiling at a true contact. To see where the model breaks, evaluate the factorial at a scale y between nI and nJ: the subtracted masses enter with multiplicity one (the ratio of endpoints is <6/5), the added ones do not yet enter, and the factorial datum changes by exactly −n^α. This sensitivity distinguishes the information available away from the points x/r.

For A and B the construction works simultaneously in a single measure over scales n_j with ratio sufficiently large depending on K,I,J: each earlier block enters all cutoffs e≤D and its first moment in B carries the factor Σ_e e c_e=0 by the finite second difference. Later ones do not enter. The accumulated perturbation of θ(y) is O(y^α) at every y because each complete block has zero mass. This simultaneous extension concerns A,B; the floor factors of the factorials at later scales can distinguish old blocks.

### Factorial average across the tooth and contamination by the second multiple

Take K≥1 and the unnormalized average I=∫_{n/6}^{n/5} log(⌊y⌋!)dy. In the first tooth p∈(n/6,n/5), its coefficient per unit of Λ(p) is exactly n/5−p. Hence, at fixed mass, I measures the first moment of that band with minus sign. For q in (n/11,n/10), the first copy q enters the whole average and the second 2q only from 2q onwards, with no third copy. The coefficient is (n/5−n/6)+(n/5−2q). This allows probing the exact average, without discretization error.

In each of the two bands choose two separated interior subintervals of length proportional to n. A transport of mass of order n^α between them, as in 338, changes the position moment by an amount U of order n^(1+α). In the second band perform another transport, in the opposite direction, calibrating its real mass so that the change of moment is exactly −U/2. The weights remain positive for α<1. Both total masses stay fixed and NOW I ALSO stays fixed, since its change is −U−2(−U/2)=0. All the old local cutoffs x/e and factorials x/r stay fixed for |x/n−1| sufficiently small: the ratios in each pair of subintervals stay in (5,6) or in (10,11). By contrast Σ_{e≤5} e c_e=5 and Σ_{e≤10} e c_e=−5: in the second case the complete stencil 5,6,7 sums to zero in first moment and 10c₁₀=−5 is added. The change of B is thus 15U/2. Incorporating the continuous factorial average of the whole first tooth still requires controlling the second multiple. Identifiability counterexample in positive weights; it does not modify the value of the real prime weights.

Even M arbitrary factorial samples y≤Cn (with C fixed), together with all their integer divisions, leave quantifiable freedom if the average is not imposed. In (n/6,n/5) the points y/j for positive integer j introduce only O_C(M) cuts among all of them: j≤6C. Within each resulting interval ALL the floor coefficients are constant, as are the corresponding counting indicators. Treat primes at the cut points as untouchable. There remain ≫n/log n primes if M=o(n/log n), by PNT. In a box of h primes move one unit from each of the first ⌊h/4⌋ to one of the last ⌊h/4⌋. For h≥4 each distance is ≥h/2 by integer spacing. By Cauchy the total increase of the moment is ≫n²/[(M+1)log² n] (subtract O(n/log n) for small boxes). The relative weights change by O(1/log n). The local cutoffs of 338 are preserved using only a fixed compact subband. So for M≤n^(1/2−δ) the ambiguity exceeds n^(3/2+ε) for 0<ε<δ. The bound measures resolution in weight models, not the complexity of every possible proof.

### Exact removal of both ends via compensated factorial ramps

Fix K≥1 and the stencil c of 322. The reading with powers instead of primes differs by O_K(x^(3/2)log²x) in xS by counting on the fixed support; this is admissible for each σ>1/2. Let H(u)=Σ_e c_e(u−e)_+ of compact support and F_d(x)=Σ_m log(m)(x−dm)_+. The divisor identity gives exactly F_d(x)=Σ_q Λ(q)q Σ_{j≥1}(x/q−dj)_+. For an integer J>D, take λ_d=(c*μ)(d) up to J. The slope jumps coincide by λ*1=c, so subtracting Σλ_d F_d removes ALL the contributions with q≥x/J. The Möbius inversion step is classical and already appears in the corpus. Here the prefix is completed to remove at the same time the polynomial part of the other end.

Impose Σλ_d=Σλ_d/d=0 by adding a common coefficient on J<d≤2J and another on 3J<d≤4J. Both are O_K(1) uniformly: the initial ordinary balance is O_K(J) and the harmonic one is O_K(1) since |Σ_{m≤Y} μ(m)/m|≤2 by Σ μ(m)⌊Y/m⌋=1 for Y≥1. After dividing the first equation by J the determinant tends to log(4/3)−log 2≠0. For u>4J, use
    Σ_{j≥1}(u−dj)_+=u²/(2d)−u/2+(d/2){u/d}(1−{u/d}).
In the residual E(u)=H(u)−Σ_d λ_d Σ_j(u−dj)_+ only Bernoulli terms remain and |E(u)|≪_K J². By Chebyshev, Σ_{q≤P} Λ(q)q |E(x/q)|≪_K J²P² if P≤x/(4J). With P=x^(3/4)/J and large x one is left, uniformly for D<J≤x^(3/4), with
    xS(x)=Σ_{d≤4J} λ_d F_d(x) + Σ_{x^(3/4)/J<q<x/J} Λ(q)q E(x/q) + O_K(x^(3/2)log²x).
The factorials are sums of logarithms of integers evaluable without information on zeros. In particular J=⌊√x⌋ moves the unknown interval to prime powers between approximately x^(1/4) and √x, at root precision after dividing by x. It remains to compare the signed residual sum PLUS the factorial datum with mx²; moving the band does not supply that comparison.

Cost of this exact removal by integer dilation ramps: the jumps force λ_d=(c*μ)(d) for d<J. Already λ_{5r}=−c₅ for each prime r>D, 5r<J. More generally λ_{5m}=c₅μ(m) for m squarefree without prime factors ≤D: the only divisors of 5m up to D are 1,5 and c₁=0. Such m have positive density for fixed D by finite sieving up to L>D and removal of squares of larger primes, of upper density ≤Σ_{j>L}j^(−2), with finite product tending to a positive constant. This forces ≫_K J nonzero coefficients and Σ_{d<J}|λ_d|/√d ≍_K √J (the upper bound by boundedness of λ). The cost refers to exact coincidence of kernels, not to approximate methods or cancellation in the factorial sum.

### Complete centring and compression by smoothness

In 356 the factorial cost is removed by centring. With Y=x/d, uniform Stirling for v≥2 gives log(⌊v⌋!)=v log v−v+(1/2−{v})log v+(log(2π))/2+O(1/v). The primitive of the mean-zero sawtooth is bounded. Integrating from 0 to Y and multiplying by d one obtains
    F_d=x²log(x/d)/(2d)−3x²/(4d)+(x/2)log(2π)+O(d log(2Y)).
The compensations of 356 leave only −(x²/2)Σ λ_d(log d)/d with error O_K(J²log x). That main term is exactly the continuous integral of R(t)=Σ_d λ_d Σ_j(x−djt)_+: for Re s>1 its Mellin transform in t is x^(s+1)ζ(s)Σ λ_d d^(−s)/[s(s+1)]. By 356, R(t)=O_K(J²t) near 0 and has finite upper support, so one can pass to s=1. Since ∫t H(x/t)dt=mx², one is left with
    xS(x)−mx²=Σ_{q<x/J}Λ(q)qE(x/q)−∫₀^(x/J)tE(x/t)dt+O_K(J² log x+x^(3/2)log²x).
With J=⌊x^(3/4)⌋ only a centred discrepancy below (1+o(1))x^(1/4) remains, with no pending factorial estimate.

For any fixed δ in (0,1) there is even a detector of all exterior zeros with centred discrepancy only at t≤C x^δ. We detail the change of detector. In multiplicative convolution (measure dy/y) take f as the power of order N+2 of the indicator of [1,2]. Its Mellin transform is ((2^s−1)/s)^(N+2), with no zeros in Re s>0; f has support [1,C] with C=2^(N+2), and in log y it is a compact spline of class C^N. Define U(x)=Σ_q Λ(q) f(q/x). A floor U(x)≥x f̂(1)−O_ε(x^(1/2+ε)) would exclude every exterior zero by Landau: the Mellin transform in x uses f̂(s) multiplying −ζ'/ζ; the restriction x≥1 only subtracts an entire function by support away from zero.

Take J=⌊x^(1−δ)⌋ and λ_d=μ(d) up to J, completing J<d≤2J with a bounded constant that makes Σλ_d/d=0 (by the harmonic bound of 356). Let
    G_x(t)=f(t/x)−Σ_d λ_d Σ_{j≥1}f(djt/x).
By Möbius, G_x(t)=0 for t>Cx/J: all indices dj that enter are ≤J and (λ*1)(h)=1_{h=1} there. The smoothed factorial identity Σ_qΛ(q)Σ_j f(djq/x)=Σ_m log m f(dm/x) is exact. For Y=x/d, Poisson or N integrations by parts at the nonzero frequencies give
    Σ_m log m f(m/Y)=Y[log Y·f̂(1)+∫ f(u)log u du]+O_N(Y^(1−N)log(2Y)).
Compact support in (0,∞) holds both for f and for f log. In the weighted sum there remains −x f̂(1)Σλ_d log(d)/d, with error O_N(x^(1−δN)log x). The continuous integral of the same factorial copies has that EXACT value: for Re s>1 its Mellin transform in t is x^s f̂(s)ζ(s)Σλ_d d^(−s); one passes to 1 because Poisson in j cancels the term t^(−1) and the rest is integrable at zero for fixed x,J. Therefore
    U(x)−xf̂(1)=Σ_{q≤Cx/J}Λ(q)G_x(q)−∫₀^(Cx/J)G_x(t)dt+O_N(x^(1−δN)log x).
Choose N≥2 with δN>1/2. The precision is then better than root in the transfer error, with no hypothesis on zeros. The weights G_x depend on μ up to J: this operation reduces the range of the outer sum over prime powers, but introduces long arithmetic data into its weights. It does not by itself provide control of its discrepancy. Difference from 204: here the LARGE powers are removed and a one-sided detector of each zero is kept with a single centred residual small in support, not necessarily in norm.

### The two parts of the compressed weight exceed root within their effective band

A single smooth test f can be fixed for all orders of argument 368. In log y take the density of the sum of independent uniform variables on [0,j^(−2)] for j≥1 and set f(y) equal to that density at log y. Support [1,C], C=exp(Σ j^(−2)). The Fourier transform of the density decays faster than any power using the first k factors for each k; by inversion it is C∞. Its Mellin transform is the product of (exp(s/j²)−1)/(s/j²), nonzero in Re s>0 because each factor is and the sum of their deviations from 1 converges for fixed s. This choice admits all the estimates of 368.

Write g_J(v)=G_x(xv) for the same λ of 368. It has upper support b=C/J, and for v≤1/(2J) one has |g_J(v)|≪_k J(Jv)^k for every k≥1 by Poisson in the sum over j and the harmonic compensation. Its entire Mellin transform is f̂(s)(1−ζ(s)Σλ_d d^(−s)); in particular at every zero of ζ it coincides exactly with f̂(s). Take a fixed critical height ρ=1/2+iγ where ζ(ρ)=0, and the trivial zero −2. Fix η>0 and a=J^(−1−η). For s=ρ and s=−2 the integrals over (0,a) are O_A(J^(−A)) for any A by choosing k according to η,A (integrate the preceding bound). On [a,b] one gets
    ∫|g_J| dv ≥ (|f̂(ρ)|/2)√a,
    ∫g_J(v)v^(−3)dv=f̂(−2)+o(1).
In the second identity the integral is over [a,b]. Let P,N be the integrals of the positive and negative parts on that interval. The larger one is ≫√a. To compensate it in the second identity, the smaller must be ≥a³(b^(−3) max(P,N)−O(1)). For 0<η<1, b^(−3)√a→∞, whence
    min(P,N) ≫_η J^(−1/2−7η/2).
For J=⌊x^(1−δ)⌋ and any fixed δ in (0,1), take η>0 with 7η<δ/(1−δ) and η<1. In units t=xv BOTH continuous masses of G_x on [xa,xb] exceed x^(1/2+c) for some c=c(δ)>0 and all sufficiently large x (in the ≫ sense). The removed lower tail is already negligible to any power for the cited moments. So reduction 368 does not allow discarding a part for absolute smallness, even in the effective band and with a positive test f. It gives no lower bound for those two masses over the prime powers, nor does it prevent a cancellation in the centred discrepancy.

For fixed J>C, both signs appear arbitrarily close to 0. Indeed all the moments at s=−2n coincide with f̂(−2n), bounded for n≥1. If g_J vanished on a neighbourhood of 0, in u=v^(−2) these would be moments of two finite measures of disjoint compact support (use the weight v^(−3)dv to start at n=1); equality of all moments and polynomial approximation would force equality of the measures, impossible. If it had fixed sign near 0, a subinterval of nonzero mass strictly closer to 0 than the support of the opposite sign and than v=1 would exponentially dominate the negative moments of growing order. This contradicts their boundedness.

### Unconditional sampling of both signs by the real primes

With the test f of 386, J=⌊x^(1−δ)⌋ and δ fixed in (0,1), define L=log(2J), a=1/(JL⁴), b=C/J. For each sign, uniformly for all large x,
    Σ_{xa≤p≤xb} (log p) [g_J(p/x)]_±
      = (1+O_δ(exp(−cδ√log x))) x ∫_a^b [g_J(v)]_± dv
      ≫_δ x J^(−1/2)L^(−14).
Here [z]_−=max(−z,0). The same statement holds including the prime powers with Λ. The error is relative to each mass; the absolute root error in the centred difference does not follow.

Uniformity details. In any fixed strip the Mellin transform of f is O(exp(−c√|t|)) for large |t|: in the product of 386 the factors with j≤c₀√|t| have modulus ≤1/2 choosing c₀ small, and the product of the bounds of the remaining ones is ≤exp(O(Σj^(−2))). By inversion, the density in log y has k-th derivatives bounded by A^(k+1)(k!)², absorbing polynomial factors of the integral. The same holds in y: apply y^(−k)D(D−1)…(D−k+1), D=d/d log y, on [1,C]; the sum of absolute coefficients weighted by A^j(j!)² grows from k to k+1 by a factor ≤k+A(k+1)². Integrations by parts optimized at k≈c√|ξ| give |Fourier(f)(ξ)|≪exp(−c√|ξ|) also in the additive variable. In the Poisson argument of 386 this gives |g_J(v)|≪J exp(−c/√(Jv)) for 0<v≤1/(2J), absorbing the prefactors of each mesh. Hence the tails (0,a) of the fixed moments used in 386 are O_A(J^(−A)) for every A. Repeating that argument with this a, the positive and the negative mass in dv are each ≫J^(−1/2)L^(−14); moreover their ratio in either direction is O(L^12), by the moment −2 and b/a=CL⁴.

To transfer to the primes, let h(u)=exp(u)g_J(exp u). Its Fourier transform is, up to the sign of t, the Mellin transform of g_J at 1+it. By 386, in a fixed strip around that line and |t|≥2 it is bounded by J^B exp(−c√|t|) with B fixed (ζ grows at most polynomially and the finite sum has bounded coefficients). Cauchy gives the same bound with other constants for the first derivative in t. Cutting smoothly in frequency |t|≤2L⁴ with plateau |t|≤L⁴, the derivative in u of the tail has L¹ norm O_A(J^(−A)): use Cauchy–Schwarz with weight (1+u²)^(−1), and Plancherel on the tail multiplied by t and its derivative in t. The low part is a convolution of h with a kernel whose derivative has L¹ norm O(L⁴). Thus ∥h'∥₁≪ L⁴∥h∥₁+O_A(J^(−A)); likewise ∥h∥∞≤∥h'∥₁. The variation of each sign part is at most ∥h'∥₁.

On [xa,xb] the lower endpoint eventually exceeds x^(δ/2). Apply Abel with θ(y)−y≪y exp(−c√log y) to the weight [h(log(y/x))]_±/y. Endpoints and derivative cost O_δ(exp(−c'√log x)(∥h∥∞+∥h'∥₁+∥h∥₁)). The same holds for ψ. Multiplying by x one obtains the announced comparison: ∥h∥₁=∫|g_J|dv and, by the preceding ratio and tail, it costs at most O(L^12) times the smaller mass. The logarithms are absorbed by decreasing cδ. This is a pointwise estimate at scale x for each part; the compensation between their two errors at the absolute level required in 368 remains open.

### Exact exponent of the relative deficit at the short scale

Set Θ=sup Reρ over the nontrivial zeros and keep f from 386. For integer J→∞, N_J=∫₀^∞|g_J(v)|dv satisfies
    lim log N_J / log J = Θ−1.
Floor: for each fixed zero of abscissa β, by 386–399, |f̂(ρ)|≤a^(β−1) N_J+o(1) with a=1/(J log⁴(2J)). Pass first to the lower limit and then to the supremum over β. Ceiling: use M(t)=Σ_{n≤t}μ(n)≪_ε t^(Θ+ε) (the classical link used in 195:5572; for Θ=1 the trivial one suffices). If Θ<1, the total sum Σ μ(d)/d=0 and Abel on the tail give Σ_{d≤J}μ(d)/d≪J^(Θ−1+ε) taking ε small. For Θ=1 the constant ceiling of 356 holds. The compensation coefficient on J<d≤2J satisfies the same ceiling. With I=∫f and K(w)=Σ_j f(jw)−I/w (j≥1), K and K' are bounded on [0,2C] by Poisson, applicable also when differentiating the sum. On v≤C/J<1 one has g_J(v)=−Σ λ_d K(dv). The variation of K(dv) over 1≤d≤J is O(1) uniformly in v. Abel and the compensation give |g_J(v)|≪J^(Θ+ε). Integrate over its support of length C/J. This proves the limit without assuming that some zero attains Θ.

Consequence for the sign. Fix 0<δ<1, J=⌊x^(1−δ)⌋, Y=x/J and define the relative one-sided deficit on the prime powers by
    r(x)=[x∫g_J(v)dv−Σ_q Λ(q)g_J(q/x)]_+ /(x N_J).
Then, with log 0=−∞,
    limsup log r(x)/log Y = Θ−1.
Indeed the numerator differs from [x f̂(1)−U(x)]_+ by O_A(x^(−A)) by 368 for each A, since f is fixed and C∞. This last quantity has upper exponent Θ: the ceiling follows from the smoothed explicit formula (the sum of |f̂(ρ)| over zeros with multiplicity converges by 399; the line can be shifted to Re s between −2 and 0). For the floor, any eventual ceiling x^σ with 0<σ<Reρ contradicts Landau on centring the Mellin transform of U at 1 and adding a multiple of x^σ: there is a nonreal pole at ρ and no real pole to its right on the half-line (σ,∞). So there are deficits arbitrarily far out at those exponents. The denominator is x^(1+(1−δ)(Θ−1)+o(1)) by the proved limit and log Y∼δ log x. Subtracting exponents proves the identity even if Θ=1. The effective norm of 399 replaces N_J with error superpolynomial in J.

In particular a uniform improvement r(x)≪_ε Y^(−κ+ε) for each ε>0 is equivalent to Θ≤1−κ for 0<κ≤1/2. A first fixed power of saving would rule out a complete vertical strip of possible zeros; reaching the exponent 1/2 would close RH. The subexponential relative saving of 399 by itself is compatible with Θ=1. The exponent identity measures the necessary threshold but does not prove any κ>0.

### Effective bound for the signed error and required height of a witness
