Keep δ fixed, N_J,Y,a from 399–413. Let R be the modulus of the centred discrepancy of the prime powers in 413 divided by xN_J (both directions). For T=A(log x)², A absolute and sufficiently large depending on f, and B(T)=max_{|Imρ|≤T} Reρ,
    R ≪_δ T log(2T) (xa)^(B(T)−1) + O_δ(x^(−3)).
The deduction improves the relative error of the signed balance of 399; it still uses a known zero-free region, without extending it. Indeed the explicit formula for the uncompressed observable is
    U(x)−x f̂(1)=−Σ_ρ x^ρ f̂(ρ)−Σ_{j=1}^3 x^(−2j) f̂(−2j)+O_f(x^(−7)).
Shifting the line to −7 gives the three trivial residues at −2,−4,−6, of total order O_f(x^(−2)). For the statement divide those residues by xN_J; the first gives only O(x^(−5/2+o(1))) with the trivial lower ceiling of N_J, so it cannot be absorbed directly into O(x^(−3)). It is however absorbed into the first term since B(T)≥1/2, xa≤x and T≥1. The nontrivial tail above T is O(x exp(−c√T)) by 399 and zero counting; choose A for error O(x^(−6)). The transfer step 413 also costs O_δ(x^(−6)). For the low zeros, uniformly in their ordinates,
    |f̂(ρ)|=|∫ g_J(v)v^(ρ−1)dv| ≤ a^(β−1)N_J+O_D(J^(−D)).
This holds because the lower tail of 399 admits the same absolute majorant for 0≤β≤1. Since N_J≫J^(−1/2)/log^14(2J), choose D according to δ. Summing and normalizing one obtains the bound.

In particular, by the Vinogradov–Korobov region already audited in 371, one obtains unconditionally, with h=log log x,
    R ≪_δ exp(−c log Y/[h^(2/3)(log h)^(1/3)]).
Indeed log(xa)=log Y−4 log log(2J)∼log Y and the polylogarithmic prefactor is absorbed. Another consequence: if r(x)≥Y^(−κ) for fixed 0<κ<1/2 at a large scale, there is a zero with |Imρ|≤A(log x)² and β≥1−κ−O_δ(log log x/log x). For such a deficit it can be chosen with Re(x^ρ f̂(ρ))>0, retaining only the real parts of that sign in the sum that explains it; the trivial residues are negligible relative to the threshold. The cost in height and the bound are uniform in x; neither a fixed power saving nor the existence of that deficit is proved.

### Two equal masses and local obligation after discounting a trend

Fix δ∈(0,1) and an integer m≥0. In 368 keep λ_d=μ(d) for d≤J, but now choose m+2 constants on the disjoint blocks e^j J<d≤e^(j+1)J (j=0,…,m+1) so that L(s)=Σλ_d d^(−s) satisfies
    L(s)=1/ζ(s)+O_J((s−1)^(m+2)).
This requires the derivatives 0,…,m+1 at 1. Multiply first by J^(s−1): the moment matrix of the blocks in degree k has entries Σ_d (−log(d/J))^k/d converging to ∫_j^(j+1)(−u)^k du. This matrix is invertible: integrating the monomials over unit intervals gives polynomials in j of degree k with leading coefficient (−1)^k, and the nodes are distinct. The right-hand sides cost O_m(log^(m+2)(2J)) by absolute summation. If Θ<1 they are O_{m,ε}(J^(Θ−1+ε)) by Abel on the tail d>J of μ(d)/d with the weights (log(d/J))^k. So the correction coefficients admit the ceilings needed in 368–413, with harmless logarithmic powers when Θ=1.

The new residual has f̂(s)(1−ζ(s)L(s)) as Mellin transform. The simple pole at 1 consumes one order, so
    ∫g_J(v)(log v)^k dv=0   (0≤k≤m).
All the identities at the zeros remain exact. The upper support b=C/J does not increase: the added indices are larger than J. The tail and derivative arguments of 399 are kept with constants depending on m: the largest dilation is e^(m+2)J and the additional cost in the coefficients is polylogarithmic. The transfer of 368 still costs O_A(x^(−A)) for each fixed A. In particular in the continuum the two sign masses are exactly N_J/2. For the prime powers it now suffices to have the floor directly on their DIFFERENCE, ΣΛ(q)g_J(q/x)≥−O_ε(x^(1/2+ε)), with no main term to subtract. That floor has not been proved.

Testable consequence on the ordinary counts. Set a=1/(Jlog⁴(2J)), Y=x/J and
    D_m(x)=inf_{deg P≤m} sup_{xa≤t≤xb} |ψ(t)/t−P(log(t/Y))|.
One has limsup log D_m(x)/log Y=Θ−1. For the ceiling use P=1 and the classical bound ψ(t)−t≪_ε t^(Θ+ε) (if Θ=1 Chebyshev suffices). For the floor, the relative prime discrepancy R of the corrected weight satisfies
    R ≪_{m,δ} log⁴(2J) D_m(x)+O_A(x^(−A)).
Indeed ∫g_J(t/x)d[tP(log(t/Y))]=0 by the moments, including the derivative of the polynomial. On [xa,xb] integrate by parts the difference with ψ(t): the endpoints and the integral cost O(x D_m(x) log⁴(2J) N_J) by the variation control of h of 399 and t|d(g_J(t/x))/dt|≤|h'(log(t/x))|/(t/x)+|g_J(t/x)|. The integral of this last majorant in dt is O(x(∥h'∥₁+N_J)). For the complement take nearly optimal polynomials bounded uniformly on the interval, since P=0 gives a constant ceiling. Their coefficients are bounded according to m by interpolation at m+1 fixed points in log(t/Y) within the interval for large x. The tail t<xa of the polynomial and of its derivative, and the sum with Λ on the same tail, are superpolynomially small by 399; the weight is zero above xb. The proof of 413 is preserved with these corrections and gives exponent Θ−1 for the one-sided relative deficit, which does not exceed R. The floor for D_m follows. This localizes a difficulty in the shape of ψ(t)/t within the short band: not even fitting a different polynomial at each scale removes the exponent of its worst fluctuations. No new power bound for D_m is deduced.

### Strict separation of all higher powers

In 441 a single weight can be chosen so that the deficit keeps its full exponent even when using ONLY simple primes, and so that the signed total of the powers of order ≥2 has strictly smaller exponent. Let Θ=sup Reρ, J=⌊x^(1−δ)⌋, Y=x/J, with 0<δ<1 fixed. Impose on L(s), in addition to the conditions of 441, L(1/2)=1/ζ(1/2) and L(1/3)=1/ζ(1/3). These two values of ζ are finite and nonzero (by the alternating eta series). For this use m+4 blocks of the same kind as in 441. After the normalization J^(s−1), the two additional limit rows are the integrals of exp(u/2) and exp(2u/3) over the unit intervals. A real combination of a polynomial of degree ≤m+1 and these two exponentials cannot have m+4 distinct zeros unless it is identically zero: differentiate m+2 times and apply Rolle, since two exponentials of distinct rates have at most one zero in a nontrivial combination. Hence a combination with zero integral on each of the m+4 intervals is zero, whence invertibility. For s=1/2,1/3, Abel with M(t)≪t^(Θ+ε) bounds the partial sums of μ(d)/d^s by O(J^(Θ−s+ε)), also at the edge s=Θ. So all the correction coefficients are O_{m,ε}(J^(Θ−1+ε)), and the results on support, transfer, moments at 1 and visibility are preserved.

Moreover ∫g_J(v)v^(1/k−1)dv=0 for k=2,3. On the whole support one has |g_J(v)|+|v g'_J(v)|≪J^(Θ+ε): apply the Abel argument of 413 both to K(w) and to wK'(w), whose derivatives are bounded on the relevant compact interval by Poisson and smoothness. For each k=2,3 the integral in t of g_J(t^k/x) is zero by the change v=t^k/x. Hence the sum over p of (log p)g_J(p^k/x) is integrated against d(θ(t)−t). The classical bound |θ(t)−t|≪_ε t^(Θ+ε) for t≥1, and |θ(t)−t|=t below 1, give by Abel a cost O(J^(Θ+ε)Y^(Θ/k+ε)): integrate the derivative bounded by O(kJ^(Θ+ε)/t) up to (CY)^(1/k). For k≥4 it suffices to count in total O(Y^(1/4)log²(2Y)) of power mass by Chebyshev and k≤log(CY)/log 2. Since Θ≥1/2, this proves, WITHOUT assuming RH,
    |Σ_{k≥2,p} (log p)g_J(p^k/x)| ≪_{δ,m,ε} x^(Θ(1−δ/2)+ε).
The estimate is on the signed balance: the parts of opposite signs of each exponent k=2,3 cancel in their continuous integral.

The complete one-sided deficit has upper exponent Θ by 413 and 441; the preceding difference has strictly smaller exponent for every possible Θ. It follows that
    limsup log([−Σ_p(log p)g_J(p/x)]_+)/log x = Θ    (log 0=−∞).
So the two positive sums to be compared can be built with simple primes only and exactly equal continuous masses. The floor with error O_ε(x^(1/2+ε)) for the simple primes is equivalent to the global sign even without knowing Θ. If Θ=1/2, the signed total of higher powers is O_ε(x^(1/2−δ/4+ε)), below root; unconditionally what is obtained is the separation of exponents, not that last absolute value. The use of μ in the weight persists and the one-sided bound for the simple primes is still pending.

### Minorant by square-rank blocks using only their two extreme primes

With the weight of 457 define W(t)=(log t)g_J(t/x) for t≥2. For the primes p_i in increasing order set
    Q_inf(x)=Σ_{j≥1}(2j+1) min_{p_{j²}≤t≤p_{(j+1)²}} W(t),
    Q_sup(x)=Σ_{j≥1}(2j+1) max_{p_{j²}≤t≤p_{(j+1)²}} W(t).
The effective sum is finite by support. They are deterministic lower and upper certificates for Σ_p W(p): the block of indices j²,…,(j+1)²−1 contains exactly 2j+1 primes. Including the right endpoint in the optimization is valid. The same bracket encloses the sum of samples (2j+1)W(p_{j²}), or any choice of one point of the interval per block. The two certificates use, in the outer sampling, only the primes of square index; the computation of the weight still contains the Möbius sequence.

Uniformly in x, the width satisfies
    0≤Q_sup−Q_inf≪_ε J^(Θ+ε) √Y log²(2x).
Indeed among the blocks with W not identically zero the largest 2j+1 costs O(1+√π(CY))≪√Y. The oscillations add up to at most the variation of W over [2,CY], by disjoint interiors and support (the last interval may cross CY). From 457, |W'|≪J^(Θ+ε)(1+log t)/t, and the integral costs O(J^(Θ+ε)log²(2x)). So the width has upper exponent at most (1−δ)Θ+δ/2. If there is an exterior zero, this number is strictly smaller than Θ. If Θ=1/2 it equals 1/2. There is no need to estimate the internal distribution of the primes of any block nor their individual gaps.

By 457 the floor Q_inf(x)≥−O_ε(x^(1/2+ε)) for every ε>0 and all large x is equivalent to RH. Sufficiency uses the minorization directly; necessity uses the ceiling of the width and the floor of 457 under RH. Even replacing Q_inf by Q_sup or by the left sample preserves the equivalence: if Θ>1/2, along a sequence with deficit of exponent arbitrarily close to Θ the width is of strictly smaller order. More generally the same argument holds for a fixed partition of the ranks with maximal cardinality O_ε(n^(1/2+ε)) up to rank n, also counting the block that crosses the cutoff. No optimality among the actual partitions of the primes is established. The square construction needs O(√π(CY)+1) endpoints and its root-level minorant has not been proved.

### Simultaneous sampling of the primes and of the two Möbius lists

Take m=0 in 457. An equivalent floor can be formulated using O(√J log(2J)+√Y) selected positions from three ordered lists: the primes and the integers with μ=+1 or with μ=−1. The datum of a position is the integer of that rank. Auxiliary sums over integers without prime or Möbius weights remain allowed. This counts list data, not operations to produce them. The antecedent 195:5572 uses transport between the two lists; here they are grouped separately to preserve a detector of simple primes.

In each Möbius list start at rank r=1 and group h=max(1,⌊r/√J⌋) consecutive ranks; assign the whole weight ±h to the first integer of the block if it is ≤J and move to r+h. Even the last block is used with its full weight. For each t≤J the accumulated error Δ(t) against the true prefix is O(t/√J): completed blocks are exact, a unit block introduces no error and in an incomplete block r≤t. O(√J log(2J)) positions are queried, since once r≥2√J each step increases r by at least r/(2√J). Call ν the resulting coefficients.

Recompute the four constant coefficients of 457 with ν in the prefix. The difference with the true ones is O(J^(−1/2)log^B(2J)) for fixed B: in the polynomial rows of 441 apply Abel with Δ(t) to (log(t/J))^k/t, k=0,1; the boundary term and the integral cost O(J^(−1/2)log^(k+1)(2J)). In the rows s=1/2,1/3 the function is J^(s−1)t^(−s); Abel costs O_s(J^(−1/2)). The inverse of the matrix is bounded by 457. Write the new total coefficients as λ̃. On 0<v<b=C/J set g₀(v)=−Σ λ̃_d K(dv) with K from 413 and set zero for v≥b. The cutoff is explicit: ν does not have the divisor inversion of μ. In the interior, both g₀−g_J and its Euler derivative cost O(√J log^B(2J)) by Abel with Δ(t) and the bounded variation of K(dv) and of dvK'(dv) over the intervals of d; the four corrections cost the same order. The jump at b satisfies that ceiling since g_J(b)=0.

Restore the centring exactly. Let χ be a fixed smooth function of integral one with support in (C/3,2C/3), and set
    g_*(v)=g₀(v)−J χ(Jv) ∫₀^b g₀(w)dw.
Since ∫g_J=0 and b=C/J, this adjustment also costs O(√J log^B(2J)) both in modulus and in Euler derivative. The final weight uses only the selected data and has integral zero. For the difference with g_J its prime sum is an integral against d(θ(t)−t). The derivative on each piece costs O(√J log^B(2J)/t) after the change v=t/x, and the jump at t=CY costs O(√J log^B(2J)); in case of coincidence with a prime apply Abel piecewise with the one-sided limits (the atom costs at most the jump times log(CY)). One obtains the bound O_ε(√J Y^(Θ+ε)log^B(2J)), using the bound for θ of 457. It is essential to centre AFTER cutting, so as to use the error of θ instead of its total mass.

For g_* now use the outer sample with square ranks of 469 (or infima and suprema per block, allowing the jump). Its error against the sum over all primes is O_ε(J^(Θ+ε)√Y log^B(2x)), by the variation including the single jump. For J=⌊√x⌋ the two errors together cost O_ε(x^(Θ/2+1/4+ε)). If Θ>1/2 this limiting exponent is strictly smaller than Θ; if Θ=1/2 it is 1/2. Hence the one-sided root-level floor of the simultaneous sampling is equivalent to RH by 457. O(x^(1/4)log(2x)) list entries are required for each x. The floor is not deduced from that count; in particular the centring of g_* does not impose equality between the two arithmetic masses.

### Sum-of-squares test at the balanced scale: a block of arbitrary signature

Test on the compressed detector, not on the Weil form directly. The support of f can be narrowed to [1,26/25] in 386 by multiplying all the lengths of the uniforms by log(26/25)/Σj^(−2). Smoothness, Mellin nonvanishing in Re s>0 and all the preceding bounds are preserved with other constants. The density is positive in the interior: every interior point admits a neighbourhood in the positive part of a finite convolution after subtracting any point of the support of the tail, taking that tail sufficiently short.

For x=J² a perfect square use u=d/J, v=t/J. The copy kernel without the separable term in 413 is A(u,v)=Σ_{l≥1} f(luv). For every N there are 2N nodes in two FIXED bands inside (0,1) on which its matrix has exactly N positive and N negative eigenvalues. Choose small bands around α=3/5 and β=5/6. The products within each band are close to 9/25 or 25/36 and do not fall in any [1/l,26/(25l)]. Between bands only l=2 can intervene. Take u_i=α exp(iη), v_j=β exp((1/2−j)η) for 1≤i,j≤N with η>0 sufficiently small depending on N. Then 2u_i v_j=exp((i−j+1/2)η), strictly less than 1 if i<j and in the interior of the support if i≥j. The matrix between bands is triangular with positive diagonal; the two own blocks are zero. The symmetric matrix has eigenvalues ± each singular value of the triangular one. The kernel K(uv) subtracts I/(uv), of rank one. Any fixed number of linear moment constraints only reduces by that number the dimension of each sign subspace. Likewise the four-block corrections in 457 are of finite rank on the prefix probes (each coefficient depends linearly on four data, plus an independent constant); the recentring of 482 adds at most a separable product. Taking the symmetric part of a correction of rank r costs at most 2r. So a proof that required a sign for all real probes, even using the SAME probe in both variables and those fixed moments, fails in arbitrary dimension. For fixed N the support inequalities are strict, so by PNT one can even choose the nodes as primes divided by J for all large J (without identifying the free coefficients with their real weights).

In these same bands there is a fixed-sign contribution of the genuine data that can be quantified. Let I,V be compact intervals of positive length in the bands of α,β such that 1<2uv<26/25 on I×V; they are obtained by slightly shifting one of the centres. In the copy term of −Σμ(d)A(d/J,p/J), restricting d to primes in JI and p to primes in JV gives exclusively the positive sign, with mass
    (1+o(1)) J²/(log J) ∫_I∫_V f(2uv)dv du.
By PNT the respective measures (log J)/J per prime and (log p)/J per prime converge to Lebesgue on fixed intervals; the statement for the product follows by approximating the continuous weight by step functions. It is a localized piece of the copy kernel, before the compensation terms. It is not a floor for the complete sum. The signature shows why this favourable piece cannot be extended to the global case by matrix positivity of the arithmetic kernel alone.

### Positive balance after regrouping by the full integer and summing cofactors of both signs

Use the DENSE detector of simple primes of 457, with the narrow test of 496 and x=J². Its sum is exactly Σ_n f(n/x)B_J(n), where
    B_J(n)=1_{n prime}log n−Σ_{r|n, r prime}log r Σ_{d|n/r}λ_d.
This incorporates ALL the copies and factorial corrections of each integer. The continuous centring is zero by construction. The constant coefficients of the correction blocks are o(1): in the normalized Taylor rows of 441 the absolute tails after Abel with M(t)≪t exp(−c√log t) cost o(1), since a fixed polynomial in u is integrated against exp(−c√(log J+u)) for u≥0. In the rows s=1/2,1/3 separate the prefix d≤√J and apply the same Abel argument to the rest. The inverse matrix is bounded.

Fix two disjoint closed intervals U,V of positive length inside (1,1.019). For each integer a≤A take p=Ju/√a and q=Jv/√a primes with u∈U, v∈V and n=apq. For fixed A and large J, p,q are distinct and the only two prime factors of n larger than A. The sum over the different families does not duplicate integers. The weight is f(uv)>0. The coefficient of log p, up to o_A(1), is
    b_a(v)=Σ_{b|a, b≤√a/v}μ(b)−1_{a=1}.
Indeed the divisors of aq are b and bq with b|a; the sum of μ(b) is 1_{a=1}, and μ(bq)=−μ(b). The contribution of every divisor in the corrections is o_A(1). The same formula holds exchanging u,v for log q; the prime divisors of a contribute O_A(1). The direct prime term is zero. Already a=2 gives two positive coefficients and a=12 two negative ones, both of asymptotic magnitude one. By PNT in proportional intervals and step approximation (only finitely many boundaries for fixed A), the COMPLETE balance of this union, divided by J²/log J, converges to the number
    ∫_U∫_V f(uv)[C_A(u)+C_A(v)]dv du,     C_A(v)=Σ_{a≤A} b_a(v)/a.

For A sufficiently large this limit is bounded away from zero from below uniformly in A. Indeed, reordering a=bk one obtains
    C_A(v)+1=Σ_{b≤√A/v} (μ(b)/b)[H_{⌊A/b⌋}−H_{⌈bv²⌉−1}].
Set E(z)=H_{⌈z⌉−1}−log z−γ. The first harmonic number is replaced by log(A/b)+γ at cost O(A^(−1/2)). The sum of (μ(b)/b)log(A/(b²v²)) tends to 2 uniformly in v: Σ μ(b)/b=0 and Σ μ(b)log b/b=−1 by the expansion of 1/ζ at 1, and the tails even multiplied by log A are o(1) by the preceding Mertens bound. One gets
    C_A(v)→1−Σ_{b≥1}(μ(b)/b)E(bv²).
The tail of this last series is absolutely uniform. For 1<v<1.019, 0<E(v²)=1−γ−log(v²)<1/2. For z≥2 one has |E(z)|≤2/(3z): between integers, z(H_n−log z−γ) is decreasing and its one-sided endpoints satisfy the bound by 1/(2n+2)<H_n−log n−γ<1/(2n). So the absolute sum of the corrections is ≤1/2+(2/3)Σ_{b≥2}b^(−2)<0.97, using 1/4+1/9+∫₃^∞t^(−2)dt<0.70. This gives a uniform positive margin and justifies the sign of the union even incorporating complete negative cofactors. The order of limits is J→∞ at fixed A, then A→∞, with no conclusion for arbitrary A depending on J.

The selection uses two large factors balanced with respect to their cofactor and the grouping unit is n, unlike the sector P⁺(n)>√n of 195. The positive balance has order x/log J. It cannot be added as a gain to the criterion without estimating the complement: in fact the smoothed PNT for U(x)−xf̂(1), the transfer of 368 and the bound for the higher powers of 457 with Θ≤1 and δ=1/2 give o(x/log J) for the total prime sum. Hence the complement cancels the positive term to first order for each fixed sufficiently large A. The sign at root scale remains open.

### Does the union of positive blocks approximate all tests? A common witness

Consider a collection of finite sets U of distinct real rates u≥1/2 and their complete spaces V_U=span{exp(−uy):u∈U}. In causal L² the UNION, without adding vectors from different blocks, is dense if and only if sup_U Σ_{u∈U}1/u=∞. Indeed, by the ratio of the Cauchy Gram determinants before and after adding v>0, the distance of exp(−vy) to V_U is (2v)^(−1/2)∏_{u∈U}|(u−v)/(u+v)| (zero if v∈U). With v=1/4 the logarithm of each factor is ≥−1/u using log((1+s)/(1−s))≤4s for s≤1/2. If the reciprocal budget is ≤M, one and the same smooth real cutoff of exp(−y/4) with compact interior support and L² error <√2 exp(−M)/2 stays at distance ≥√2 exp(−M)/2 from the WHOLE union. In the opposite direction, −log| (u−v)/(u+v) |≥2 min(v/u,u/v)≥min(2v,1/(2v))/u. Any finite list of target exponents is approximated simultaneously on a single block of large budget. Their combinations are dense by uniqueness of the Laplace transform.

Application to the complete block of 195:3647: x_j=log u_j>0 and x_{j+1}−x_j≥200/x_j imply x_{j+1}²≥x_j²+400. By comparison of sum with integral,
    Σ_j 1/u_j ≤ e^(−x₁)[1+(x₁+1)/200] ≤ 1.005.
This holds even letting ALL the nodes vary from one block to the next. There is therefore a smooth real test of compact support that none of these combinations approximates in L², with distance at least √2 exp(−1.005)/2. It also stays separated in H_eta for blocks belonging to that space, whose norm dominates L². The step via strong density is excluded for this concrete collection; approximation of the mere number W(f) is not excluded, nor is a negative form attributed to the witness.

For blocks with u≥η+c fixed, η>1/2,c>0, the same budget criterion also characterizes density in the H_eta norm of bridge 014. Necessity was already seen in L². For sufficiency, in L² with weight exp(2ηy) the nodes are r=u−η≥c, with Σ1/r comparable to Σ1/u and the same exponential product bound with constant depending on c and the target exponent. Approximate there the derivative of the datum by a single block. The terminal antiderivative −∫_y^∞ maps each exponential to a multiple of the same exponential and is bounded on that L² with norm ≤1/η by Young. It recovers the H_eta datum from its derivative and controls the H_eta norm of the error. Exact antecedent: 05-meta, entry 10914, uses this antiderivative and a union criterion for confluent nodes; 195 records the failure of Blaschke by sequence. Here the common witness covers the union of distinct blocks with moving nodes. A concrete sufficient target would be to prove PSD of the integer-rate blocks n,…,⌊n log n⌋ for a subsequence n→∞, whose reciprocal sum grows like log log n. Their PSD is an open obligation, not a consequence of the distance formula.

### Positivity of dense integer blocks of fixed ratio by uniform temporal concentration

Let T be a window with complete floor W(f)≥κ||f||₂², κ>0, on support [−T,T]. For each 1<c<exp(T) and every sufficiently large integer n, the COMPLETE space of rates n,n+1,…,⌊cn⌋ satisfies W(f)≥(κ/2)||f||₂² for arbitrary complex coefficients. So, with the certified inputs of 195:5157, it holds even for n,…,16n, without fixed logarithmic separation or dimension bound. It is not a new window. The threshold n depends on the slack T−log c and on κ.

Proof. In the Laplace image of causal L² use the orthonormal basis Φ_j(s)=√(2u_j)/(s+u_j) ∏_{k<j}(s−u_k)/(s+u_k), with u_j running over the block. Orthonormality follows by cancellation of the common inner factors on the axis and the reproducing property of the kernel 1/(s+u_k); distinct simple poles give the same space by partial fractions. For σ=hn, 0<h<1/2 fixed, the factor k on the line −σ+iτ has modulus ≤(u_k+σ)/(u_k−σ). The first factor has normalized L² norm ≤(1−h)^(−1/2) on that line. By Plancherel and Cauchy over the N coefficients,
    ||e^(σy)f||₂ ≤ √N(1−h)^(−1/2) exp[σ(2 log c+O_c(h²)+O_c(1/n))] ||f||₂.
Here one sums log((1+s)/(1−s))=2s+O(s³) with s=σ/u_k and uses Σ1/u_k=log c+O(1/n). For the inverse Laplace transforms φ_j, differentiating gives φ'_j=−u_jφ_j−2√u_j Σ_{k<j}√u_k φ_k by telescoping of the product (and φ_j(0)=√(2u_j)). In particular f' is in the block and ||f'||₂≪_c n²||f||₂.

Fix 2log c<L<L'<2T and a fixed η>1/2. Choose h small keeping a margin between L and the exponent in brackets. On y≥L, estimate e^(ηy) by e^(-(σ−η)L)e^(σy) both for f and for f'; this gives a bound O(exp(−bn))||f||₂ for both weighted tails, b>0 fixed after absorbing polynomials. A smooth plateau χ equal to one up to L and zero from L' gives ||(1−χ)f||_{H_eta}≪exp(−bn)||f||₂ and ||f||_{H_eta}≪_c,η,L n²||f||₂ by partition. By 014 the perturbation of W is o(1)||f||₂² uniformly over the WHOLE block. After translating χf to the window of radius T the floor κ applies (the extension by zero admits the initial jump in the form domain by 014), and ||χf||₂=(1+o(1))||f||₂ uniformly. The claimed floor follows. The Malmquist basis was already used in POWER_ARCH_TRIPLE_004; here the vertical shift of the whole basis and the window floor control the temporal tail of all combinations.

The result covers budgets Σ1/u→log c below T. The criterion of 525 needs UNBOUNDED budgets for the global step; this transfer from a single window does not provide them.

### Antecedent audit and leakage at the edge of the integer block

Attribution correction for 535: the positivity statement for n,…,16n was already a direct consequence of the INITIAL THEOREM of ROUND_195_A, lines 1–8, which identifies the limit of the physical minimum with λ_W(S) if the smallest rate tends to infinity and the reciprocal sum tends to S. In this case S=log c; 535 gives another proof, not an extension of the accumulated sign. The inverse approximation by the inner product and the delay is also in 195. In particular a negative test at radius log c would force eventual negativity in these blocks. Using that limit as a supposed independent route to a larger window is avoided.

A different quantification: at the transition scale itself there are unit combinations of these blocks that leave strictly positive L² mass BEYOND time 2log c, even as n tends to infinity. Let U=⌊cn⌋, m=⌊n^(1/3)⌋, h=n^(−2/3), τ_n=2Σ_{u=n}^{U−1}1/u. Use the functions of 535 with sign chosen so that the factors are (u−s)/(u+s). Let f_n be the sum of the last m functions divided by √m; ||f_n||₂=1 by orthonormality. With direct Fourier transform exp(−itξ) and extension by zero, h^(1/2) f_n(τ_n+ht) has a transform converging pointwise to
    √(2/c) exp(i(1−c^(−2))ξ³/3) ∫₀¹exp(2irξ/c)dr.
Indeed at s=iξ/h the phase of the product up to j−1 is −2Σ arctan(ξ/(hu)). The linear term, after centring, leaves 2ξΣ_{u=j}^{U−1}1/(hu)=2ξ(U−j)/(c n^(1/3))+o(1), uniform over the last m nodes for fixed ξ. The cubic one uses n²Σ_{u=n}^{j−1}u^(−3)→(1−c^(−2))/2, and the remainder is O_ξ(n^(−2/3)). The prefactor of each mode is √(2/(cn))(1+o(1)); the Fourier change costs h^(−1/2) and the normalized sum becomes a Riemann integral. For every real ξ the rescaled transform is bounded uniformly by h^(−1/2)√m·√(2/(U−m)), using modulus one of the factors. Hence the convergence holds against any smooth test compact in time by domination of its transform.

With d=(1−c^(−2))^(1/3), the limiting inverse for t>0 equals √(2/c)∫₀¹ Ai((t+2r/c)/d)dr/d, strictly positive by the classical Airy representation on the positive half-line. Testing against a nonnegative nonzero bump supported in (1,2) and applying Cauchy–Schwarz gives
    liminf ∫_{τ_n+h}^{τ_n+2h} |f_n(y)|²dy >0.
Since τ_n=2log c+O(1/n), the strip lies to the right of 2log c. This rules out cutting exactly at the limiting time assuming that the whole tail disappears uniformly over the vectors of the block. It does not contradict the convergence of the minimum of W nor does it attribute a negative sign to the packet. The tail of 535 after a fixed slack and this leakage in a shrinking strip are compatible.

### Energy of the escaping packet and cost of a consecutive group of modes

The packet of 547 satisfies W(f_n)/log n→2/3 for each fixed c>1, even if the sign of the window of radius log c is not known. The convergence of the rescaled profile there is strong in L²: the Fourier limit is a cubic multiplier of modulus one applied to the transform of √(c/2)·1_[−2/c,0]; its norm by Plancherel coincides with the unit norm of the packet. The proved weak convergence against tests and the equality of norms give the strong one. For the archimedean part use m(τ)=log(1+|τ|)+O(1) from 195:3883. The tail |τ|>n³ costs o(1) even with the logarithm, since the expression in 547 gives |f̂_n(τ)|≤√(2Um)/|τ|. In the rest, on passing to ξ=hτ, the ratio log(1+|ξ|/h)/log n is bounded and tends to 2/3 for ξ≠0 (with the indicator |ξ|≤hn³). Strong and dominated convergence give the limit in the spectral average. The comb and the polar term cost O_c(1) by the bound ||exp(y)f_n||₂=O_c(1) of 195:1–10. So there is leakage with growing positive sign in THIS packet; it does not identify the sign of the rest of the block.

A uniform computation explains the mechanism for any consecutive group of Malmquist modes and not only for the preceding combination. The Blaschke product common to those modes can be suppressed without changing the norm or W: on the imaginary axis it preserves the modulus and hence all the autocorrelations; in the polar term its factors at ±1/2 have product one and are real (rates >1/2). On the remaining group of rates u_j≥2 write S=Σ1/u_j≤1. For every unit vector, Cauchy in the orthonormal basis gives Fourier density |f̂(τ)|²/(2π)≤Σu_j/[π(u_j²+τ²)]≤S/π. This cap already appeared in POWER_ARCH_TRIPLE_004. For the measure of mass one, the deficit of log(1+|τ|) relative to log(1/S) has positive part of integral ≤∫₀^∞min(1,2exp(−t)/π)dt by layers. The archimedean term is therefore ≥log(1/S)−O(1). The shift inequality 085 used in 195 gives ||exp(y)f||₂≤∏(u_j+1)/(u_j−1)≤exp(4S). It controls the polar term and the comb by an absolute constant as above. In total W(f)≥log(1/S)−O(1), with no dependence on the number or the rates of the earlier modes. Hence a combination within any consecutive group of budget S→0 has divergent positive normalized energy. The cap and the isometry explain this effect; the same floor is not deduced when summing combinations of different groups because the cross terms survive.

### The archimedean cross term between two ordered groups costs an absolute constant

In the orthonormal Malmquist basis used in 535, let f,h be two combinations whose indices are strictly ordered: every index of f precedes each index of h. For the sesquilinear archimedean form A one has
    |A(f,h)| ≤ (2+π/2)||f||₂||h||₂.
Neither the number of rates, separation, reciprocal budget, window nor the order chosen to enumerate the poles intervene. The hypothesis concerns modes of this basis, not two disjoint lists of raw exponentials.

Proof. Use Laplace on Re s>0 and boundary measure dτ/(2π). The complete archimedean multiplier is m(τ)=a(iτ)+conj(a(iτ)), with a(s)=[ψ(1/4+s/2)−log π]/2 as in 195:3893. Write F,H for the transforms. H vanishes at the reflected positive poles of all the modes of the prefix (at the rates, with multiplicities if included). Hence aH also vanishes there and is orthogonal to F, by reproduction in Hardy space. Indeed a is holomorphic in the half-plane and of growth O(log(2+|s|)) on its closure; H is rational strictly proper with poles on the left, so aH belongs to H². Therefore ∫ F conj(aH)=0 and on replacing m by a−conj(a) in the cross term exactly the same value is obtained. If b=|τ|/2, the digamma series gives
    |Im ψ(1/4+iτ/2)|=Σ_{k≥0} b/[(k+1/4)²+b²] ≤ 2+π/2.
The first term is at most 2 and the others are dominated by the integral of the decreasing function from zero. Cauchy on the boundary proves the bound. This estimates the logarithmic cross term without paying the norm of the real symbol, which is unbounded.

Consequence for a possible witness in two groups. Write P=W−A (prime comb plus polar term, keeping their signs). If on the two groups W≥d_i||·||² with d_i>0, for a negative combination f+h there must exist normalized vectors of those groups with
    |P(f/||f||,h/||h||)| > √(d₁d₂)−(2+π/2).
It follows from W(f+h)<0 and the arithmetic–geometric mean applied to the two diagonals. In particular for two groups of 559 with small budgets S₁,S₂, the required non-archimedean correlation grows at least like the geometric mean of the floors log(1/S_i)−O(1). The estimate also applies to prefix and suffix of any size. It does not give the necessary ceiling for P nor does it rule out collective interactions of many groups.

### A single cross term with fixed extreme poles and integer intermediaries

Take a=2, b=3. For N≥4 let F(s)=√(2a)/(a+s) and H_N(s)=[(a−s)/(a+s)]·∏_{j=4}^N (j−s)/(j+s)·√(2b)/(b+s). They are two orthonormal Malmquist modes in the ordering a,4,…,N,b. Their diagonal values of W are positive constants independent of N by the invariance of the reflected product of 559. Denote by C_N the real cross term W(F,H_N). If RH holds, |C_N|≤√(W(F)W(H_N)) by global positivity. If there is an exterior zero, both [C_N]_+ and [−C_N]_+ exceed N^σ arbitrarily far out for each 0<σ<2Θ−1. So it would suffice to bound by a constant, even only from ABOVE, this single cross term for all sufficiently large integers N. No limit as the rates approach each other is required either: the poles are all distinct integers and the rates of the two extreme modes are fixed. The second vector changes with N and contains all the intermediate powers; it is not the known positivity of two powers. The antecedent of 195 on the second eigenvalue uses a witness subspace by approximation; here the two vectors are fixed by a formula.

Proof. By the Weil formula over zeros with multiplicity and the symmetry z↦−z, z=ρ−1/2, the entry is exactly
    C_N=Σ_z A(z) Π_{j=4}^N (j+z)/(j−z),    A(z)=2√(ab)/[(a−z)(b−z)].
The cancellation of the factor a+z on evaluating F(z)H_N(−z) is exact. For Re z≤0 the total costs O(1) because the product has modulus ≤1 and Σ|A(z)|<∞. For the other z write d=Re z, t=|Im z| and c(z)=A(z)Γ(4−z)/Γ(4+z). One has uniformly |c(z)|≪(1+t)^(−2−2d) by Stirling. The original product has modulus ≪(1+N/(1+t))^(2d): the logarithm of the modulus of each factor is 2dj/(j²+t²)+O(j^(−2)), uniformly, and the sum of the term j/(j²+t²) differs from its integral by O(1) by bounded variation. So the part t>√N, both in the exact sum and in the approximation Σ c(z)N^(2z), costs O(log(2N)) by local zero counting and 0<2d≤1. For t≤√N the quotient Γ(N+1+z)/Γ(N+1−z) divided by N^(2z) is 1+O((1+t)/N+(1+t)^3/N²), expanding Stirling on both sides of the same centre. After multiplying by |c(z)|N^(2d) and summing the error is O(log²(2N)); N^(2d)≤N suffices. The same cost covers replacing N by x∈[N,N+1] in the approximation: for t≤√N use |(x/N)^(2z)−1|≪(1+t)/N, and above use the tail already bounded. It follows that
    C_{⌊x⌋}=Σ_{Re z>0} c(z)x^(2z)+O(log²(2x)).
The main series is real by conjugation. Its Mellin transform with x^(−s−1) from 1 to infinity continues as Σ c(z)/(s−2z), normally on compacts away from its poles; the initial equality is for Re s>1. Changing the initial endpoint to 4 subtracts an entire function. The error has holomorphic Mellin transform in Re s>0. At each exterior 2z the residue, counting multiplicities, is nonzero; nor are there positive real poles. If one orientation of C_{⌊x⌋} were eventually below O(x^σ) with σ<2Θ−1, Landau's one-sided argument applied to a constant times x^σ minus that orientation would contradict a pole with real part larger than σ. This proves the claim. The computation identifies a concrete cross term whose control would resolve the sign; it does not prove the required bound.

Formula in real data without summing zeros. With l(j)=L(j+1/2) from POWER_CONE_001, if H_N(s)=Σ_j r_j/(s+j), the power kernel gives C_N=√(2a)Σ_j r_j(l(a)+l(j))/(a+j). The term l(a) disappears because H_N(a)=0. The residues of H_N(s)/(a−s) are r_j/(a+j); substituting a=2,b=3 one is left with exactly
    C_N=2√6 Σ_{j=2}^N (−1)^(j−2) (N+j)! l(j) /[(j+3)! (j−2)! (N−j)!].
In particular the ceiling to be proved consists of a single finite alternating sum of the real values already used in POWER, with explicit coefficients. The size of those coefficients prevents inferring a uniform bound from computations at fixed precision.

### Jacobi prime quadrature of the prescribed cross term

Remove the factor 2√6 from the factorial weights of 579 and call w_j the remaining coefficients. For m=N−2 their two generating functions are exactly
    K_N(v)=Σ_j w_j v^j = v²(1−v)P_{m−1}^{(5,1)}(1−2v)/m,
    R_N(s)=Σ_j w_j/(s+j) = Π_{k=4}^N(k−s)/(k+s) /[(s+2)(s+3)].
The second equality is by residues; the first by the finite hypergeometric series: the sum divided by v² is (m+4)!/(5!m!) times ₂F₁(−m,m+5;6;v), and Euler's transformation extracts 1−v. One obtains the following reading without zeros and without the huge individual factorial terms:
    C_N/(2√6) = (8/105)(N+1/2) − Σ_p (log p)/√p ·K_N(1/p) − 1/12 + o(1).
In particular the target is equivalent to the floor (8/105)N−O(1) for this sum over simple primes.

Details. The two polar summands of l(j) give R_N(−1/2)+R_N(1/2); the first is the linear term shown, by telescoping, and the second O(1/N). The digamma-plus-constant summand is the archimedean cross term of 565 divided by 2√6 and tends to zero: in the Fourier integral it depends on N only through the inner product over k=4,…,N, or its conjugate. On each compact interval of τ that product is N^(−2iτ)Γ(4+iτ)/Γ(4−iτ)+o(1) uniformly by Stirling. The other factors are dominated on the whole line by O(log(2+|τ|)/(1+τ²)). Truncate and apply Riemann–Lebesgue. The term ζ'/ζ gives minus the sum over all prime powers with the indicated weight, by absolute convergence at each j.

For q≥2 one has |K_N(1/q)|≪min(N⁴/q², q^(3/4)/N^(3/2)). Indeed apply the Jacobi inequalities 18.14.1 and 18.14.3 (DLMF https://dlmf.nist.gov/18.14) with α=5,β=1; the factor 1−1/q is bounded away from zero. The powers of order ≥3 give o(1): on a block q≍Q they weigh O(Q^(1/3)log(2Q)) before multiplying by K_N(1/q)/√q. Summing dyadically splitting at N² gives O(N^(−1/3)log(2N)). For squares there remains Σ_p(log p)/p K_N(p^(−2)). By PNT and the change p=Nz this sum differs by o(1) from ∫₁^∞ K_N(t^(−2))dt/t. On compacts 0<e≤z≤M the weights K_N(1/(Nz)²) converge uniformly to (1/(120z⁴))·₀F₁(;6;−1/z²), by the preceding finite series and domination by a convergent series independent of N (each pair of numerator factors costs ≤3N² for large N). Outside those compacts the Jacobi bound and Chebyshev give O(e^(3/2)+M^(−4)) in both readings for t≥√2, by dyadic blocks. Between t=1 and √2 in the integral the same Bernstein bound is used: the remaining factor (1−v) has positive exponent 1/4 after applying 18.14.3, giving O(N^(−3/2)) there. The integral is EXACTLY R_N(0)/2=1/12 for all N. This determines the squares correction without RH.

At absolute cost O(1) one can also remove all p≤N^(6/5) and all p≥N^(8/3): by Chebyshev at each end the costs are respectively O(N^(−3/2)(N^(6/5))^(5/4)) and O(N⁴(N^(8/3))^(−3/2)). The remaining primes keep the same one-sided obligation at constant precision; the main term of order N is not certified to that error.

For p≥N² the weight is positive: in the alternating series of K_N(v)/v² the successive absolute ratios are bounded by m(m+5)v/6<1 for v≤1/N². Hence K_N(1/p)≤(m+4)!/[120m!p²]. By PNT and Abel this sector contributes at most (1/180+o(1))N, since Σ_{p≥N²}(log p)p^(−5/2)∼(2/3)N^(−3). It can supply at most the fraction 7/96+o(1) of the main term 8N/105. The part where the oscillation of the weight is used must therefore already contribute at linear order.

### Exact convex mixture: removal of the Jacobi oscillation

In cross term 593 include N=3 with empty intermediate product, so that K₃(v)=v²(1−v). For k≥1 and 0≤l≤k let
    b_{k,l}=(2l+7)(l+6) binom(k,l) B(l+6,k+2).
All are strictly positive and one has the polynomial identity
    Σ_{l=0}^k b_{k,l} K_{l+3}(v)=v²(1−v)^(k+1).
Indeed project (1−v)^k onto P_l^(5,1)(1−2v) with measure v⁵(1−v)dv. Rodrigues and integration by parts l times give binom(k,l)B(l+6,k+2); the squared norm is (l+1)/[(2l+7)(l+6)]. Multiply by v²(1−v) and use 593. Moreover ∫₀¹ K_N(v)dv/v=R_N(0)=1/6, which gives Σ b_{k,l}=6/[(k+2)(k+3)]. So on multiplying by (k+2)(k+3)/6 one obtains a genuinely convex average, with final weight positive on ALL of 0<v<1. Without the mode N=3 it is impossible to obtain a nonzero nonnegative polynomial by a finite combination of these modes: ∫₀¹v³K_N(v)dv=0 for all N≥4 by Jacobi orthogonality. The small mass at N=3 restores that moment.

In the normalization k² (total coefficient mass tending to 6), the prime part of the mixture is exactly
    S_k=k²Σ_p (log p)p^(−5/2)(1−1/p)^(k+1).
The continuous part corresponding to R_N(−1/2) is k² B(3/2,k+2)=Γ(3/2)√k+O(k^(−1/2)). The uniform error O(1) in 593, included for N=3 by adjusting the constant, remains O(1) after mixing. The mixture by itself gives only one implication. To prove that it retains ALL exterior zeros, set U(x)=x²Σ_p(log p)p^(−5/2) exp(−x/p). For x∈[k,k+1], U(x)−S_k=O(k^(−1/2)). Indeed for p≥2 the error of the exponential factors is ≪exp(−k/p)(1/p+k/p²), using −log(1−1/p)=1/p+O(1/p²); changing the prefactor costs O(k) before summing. On each block p≍P apply Chebyshev and sum dyadically using exp(−k/(2P)). The continuous constant is the same.

The two-sided Mellin transform of U with x^(−s−1)dx in 1/2<Re s<2 is Γ(2−s)Σ_p(log p)p^(−s−1/2). Restricting to x≥1 subtracts a term holomorphic in Re s<2. Centring by Γ(3/2)√x removes the real pole at 1/2. In Re s>0 the powers of order ≥2 of −ζ'/ζ(s+1/2) give a holomorphic function by local absolute convergence. Hence each exterior zero ρ leaves a nonreal pole at ρ−1/2 with nonzero residue; Landau rules out a bounded eventual deficit, since the centred transform is regular on the whole positive real axis (use the original restricted integral for s>1/2). Under RH the preceding ceiling for C_N gives the floor by convex mixture. It is proved that
    S_k ≥ (√π/2)√k − O(1)   for all large k
is equivalent to the global sign. This route uses the classical one-sided argument; its contribution here is the FINITE mixture of known weights with exact positive coefficients and the check that it keeps the power of the detector. The floor for S_k is not yet proved.

### From modular survival to an inequality between two tails of the same primes

Survival of k+1 independent samples modulo p has probability (1−1/p)^(k+1). A single consecutive run of integer length u with uniform origin has probability (1−u/p)_+. For real u the same ramp is obtained by randomly interpolating between consecutive lengths: its breaks p are integers. Define
    A(u)=Σ_p (log p)p^(−5/2)(1−u/p)_+ .
For Z with beta(2,k) law, integrating twice the second derivative of (1−t)^(k+1) gives E[(1−t/Z)_+]=(1−t)^(k+1) on 0≤t≤1. So S_k=k² E[A(1/Z)] exactly. In particular it suffices that
    A(u) ≥ (4/15)u^(−3/2)−O(u^(−2))    (u≥1).
Indeed (4/15)k² E Z^(3/2)=Γ(3/2)√k+O(k^(−1/2)) and k² E Z²=O(1), by the beta moments. The main term would be insufficient if only the run of length k were used; the mixture of lengths restores the full constant.

This floor is also necessary under RH. With Λ instead of primes, the Mellin inversion is the integral of (−ζ'/ζ)(5/2−s) u^(−s)/[s(s+1)] on Re s=1. Under RH shifting to Re s=9/4 gives the main term (4/15)u^(−3/2), the sum over zeros with absolute cost O(u^(−2)Σ_ρ 1/(1+|ρ|²)) and O(u^(−9/4)) on the new line. There ζ'/ζ has growth O(log²(2+|Im s|)) by horizontal distance to the critical line and the local partial-fraction formula; on horizontals choose the usual sequence of heights separated by ≫1/log T from ordinates. The orders ≥2 cost O(u^(−2)) by Chebyshev and dyadic summation with higher-power mass O(√Q) on q≤Q. This argument uses the ramp to keep constant precision in 610.

The convexity of A allows eliminating the scale parameter in favour of two tail sums. Write B_p=Σ_{r>p, prime}(log r)r^(−5/2) and D_p=Σ_{r>p, prime}(log r)r^(−7/2). The global sign is equivalent to the existence of C with
    B_p ≥ (2/3)(5/2)^(3/5) D_p^(3/5) − C D_p^(4/5)   for every sufficiently large prime p.
Proof. On the line supporting A from the right at p one has A(p)=B_p−pD_p and for ALL u, A(u)≥B_p−uD_p by the positive part term by term. By PNT, D_p∼(2/5)p^(−5/2). The minimum over t>0 of (4/15)t^(−3/2)+st is g(s)=(2/3)(5/2)^(3/5)s^(3/5). Applying the floor of A at p gives B_p≥g(D_p)−O(D_p^(4/5)). Conversely, fix s=(2/5)u^(−5/2) and choose two consecutive tails enclosing it. By PNT both primes are asymptotic to u, and the jump in D is O(u^(−7/2)log u) by its definition. One of the tails D_p can be taken. Then |g(D_p)−g(s)|+u|D_p−s|=O(u^(−5/2)log u)=O(u^(−2)) because g'(s)=u. Substitute into the lower line and use g(s)−us=(4/15)u^(−3/2). This proves the equivalence. The PNT supplies the main term of both tails; their coupling at the precision of the term D_p^(4/5) remains open.

### Squares bias and sign criterion without unknown constant

Let a(u)=(4/15)u^(−3/2) and g(s)=(2/3)(5/2)^(3/5)s^(3/5). Under RH one has
    u²[A(u)−a(u)] = −1/12 − Σ_ρ u^(i Imρ)/[(2−i Imρ)(3−i Imρ)] + o(1).
The formula of 626 for all powers gives the zero term with minus sign (shifting the Mellin line to the right subtracts residues). For the squares, after t=√u z the PNT gives u²Σ_{p≥√u}(log p)p^(−5)(1−u/p²)→∫₁^∞ z^(−5)(1−z^(−2))dz=1/12. The tail is uniform by Chebyshev. For orders ≥3 the mass up to Q costs O(Q^(1/3)log Q), so its reading after multiplying by u² costs O(u^(−1/6)log u). The computation of the squares bias is unconditional.

The modulus of the whole critical series is ≤Σ_ρ 1/(1/4+γ²)=2+γ_E−log(4π)<0.047 under RH. The identity is obtained with the symmetric Hadamard product of ξ and ξ'/ξ(1)=(2+γ_E−log(4π))/2. Without RH the sum over the zeros ON the line also has that upper bound, because Σ_ρ 2 Re(1/ρ)=2+γ_E−log(4π) and all terms are positive. In particular under RH, for all large u,
    a(u)−0.131 u^(−2) < A(u) < a(u)−0.036 u^(−2).
Domination by the squares is a classical bias mechanism; it is computed here for this ramp and its two concrete tails.

For p prime, B_p=min_{t>0}(A(t)+tD_p). The minimum is attained at p. By PNT the minimizer t₀=(2/(5D_p))^(2/5) of the continuum is ∼p. Evaluating at t₀ for the upper bound and at p for the lower one gives under RH, for all large p,
    g(D_p)−0.131 p^(−2) < B_p < g(D_p)−0.035 p^(−2).
This gives another exact equivalence WITHOUT a fitting constant: RH if and only if B_p≤g(D_p) for every sufficiently large prime. Indeed for u≥2 take the last prime p≤u, so that A(u)=B_p−uD_p. If the proposed inequality holds, A(u)≤sup_s(g(s)−us)=a(u) eventually. If there were an exterior zero, the Mellin transform of A−a on [1,∞), with the convention ∫u^(s−1)du, would have a nonreal singularity at s=5/2−ρ with Re s<2 and no real one in (3/2,2), continuing through 3/2 by centring. The higher powers give a holomorphic function for Re s<2. Landau for integrals of fixed sign (or changing s to −s) contradicts an eventual sign: the real abscissa of the first singularity would have to lie between 3/2 and Re(5/2−ρ), where the centred term is regular. This does not prove the required sign of B_p.

It also follows unconditionally that B_p<g(D_p) for infinitely many primes. Under RH the preceding stronger inequality holds. If RH failed, for any exterior zero ρ and any α with 5/2−Reρ<α<2, Landau in both orientations, adding a constant times u^(−α), forces A(u)<a(u)−u^(−α) arbitrarily far out. Choose D_p enclosing the slope s=(2/5)u^(−5/2) as in 626. Then B_p≤A(u)+uD_p and g(D_p)−uD_p=a(u)+O(u^(−5/2)log u), so B_p<g(D_p). Analogously A(u)>a(u) forces B_p>g(D_p) for the last prime p≤u. So under failure of RH both signs occur arbitrarily far out. Globally what remains is to exclude the upper sign, not to find more examples of the lower one.

### Unconditional finite sign of the tails for all primes p≤10¹²

Precisely, ALL primes p≤10¹² are proved (the endpoint bounds the value of p, not its index). Use the unconditional verification up to H=3·10¹² of Platt–Trudgian, already incorporated in the corpus. The idea to avoid estimating separately the PNT error of the squares is to subtract ALL even powers before applying the explicit formula.

Let A_Λ and E be the sums of 626 with Λ, and with terms Λ(n)n^(−5)(1−u/n²)_+, respectively. A≤A_Λ−E because besides the primes only the odd powers of order ≥3 survive. The Mellin transform of E in the same convention uses (−ζ'/ζ)(5−2s)/[s(s+1)]. For u>1 shifting to the right to infinity gives the main term u^(−2)/12; each zero ρ contributes
    −u^(ρ/2−5/2)/[2(5/2−ρ/2)(7/2−ρ/2)].
Analogously A_Λ has the main term and coefficients of 640 with ALL the zeros. The expansion is justified first on rectangles at heights avoiding ordinates by ≫1/log T; the series of residues is absolutely convergent. The new abscissa can be sent to infinity along lines at fixed distance from the trivial zeros, using the functional equation (ζ'/ζ grows O(log(2+|s|)) there), the factor u^(−Re s) and the quadratic denominator. The trivial zeros of A_Λ have favourable sign in the upper bound; those of E after multiplying by u² cost at most u^(−3/2)/(1−1/u).

The sum over the critical line, even limited to |Imρ|≤H, costs in the two scaled terms at most 0.047(1+2u^(−1/4)) by 640. For the remaining ordinates, in absolute value (√u+2)Σ_{|γ|>H}γ^(−2) suffices. If h=H+1, one has γ^(−2)≤(3/H)Re(1/(h−ρ)) in that tail. The complete sum of the real parts is ξ'/ξ(h) by Hadamard, and is ≤log H by the formula for the completed function, ψ(h/2)≤log(h/2) and ζ'/ζ(h)<0. Consequently
    u²(A(u)−a(u)) ≤ −1/12+0.047(1+2u^(−1/4))+(√u+2)3log(H)/H+u^(−3/2)/(1−1/u).
On 256≤u≤10¹⁷ the three positive corrections add up to less than 0.081, so A(u)<a(u) uniformly.

For 2000≤p≤10¹² one has D_p≤p^(−5/2)[(2/5)log p+4/25] by comparison with all integers and a decreasing integral, and D_p≥log p/(2p)^(7/2) by Bertrand. So the continuous optimum t₀=(2/(5D_p))^(2/5) satisfies 256<t₀<10¹⁷ (for the lower bound p/(log p+2/5)^(2/5) is increasing). By B_p≤A(t₀)+D_pt₀<g(D_p), this proves the sign simultaneously in that range. For the 303 primes p≤2000 the verifier verify_prime_tail_bias_2000.py sums backwards with Arb at 96 bits up to three million, bounds the upper tail of B by the integral over all integers, and discards the positive tail of D in its lower bound. Run PASS for p²(g(D_p)−B_p)>0.03 in the 303 cases. This is an unconditional interval for the NEW tail inequality, deduced from a pre-existing spectral certificate; there is no new zero-free region, window T or eventual proof.

### An isolated inversion could not explain an exterior zero: runs in prime rank

Let p_j be the prime of rank j, F_j=B_{p_j}−g(D_{p_j}), Θ=sup Reρ. The variation of the defect from one rank to the next satisfies, for each ε>0,
    |F_j−F_{j−1}| ≪_ε p_j^(Θ−7/2+ε).
Indeed with q=p_j one adds w=(log q)q^(−7/2) to the tail D and qw to B when going back one rank. By the mean value theorem F_{j−1}−F_j=w(q−g'(s)), D_q≤s≤D_q+w. By Abel and θ(t)−t≪_ε t^(Θ+ε), D_q=(2/5)q^(−5/2)+O_ε(q^(Θ−7/2+ε)) for Θ<1 and ε small. The PNT gives comparability with the main term, also at Θ=1. Since g'(s)=(2/(5s))^(2/5), one gets g'(s)=q+O_ε(q^(Θ+ε)); for Θ=1 comparability suffices. Absorb log q into ε. This saving comes from subtracting exactly the two increments of the tails at the SAME prime.

If Θ>1/2, for EACH 0<κ<1 there are arbitrarily large ranks j with
    F_i>0 for EVERY integer i with |i−j|≤j^κ.
To see this take 1/2<β<Θ so close to Θ that κ+Θ−7/2<β−5/2. By the oscillation of 640 (Landau applied with a zero of abscissa larger than β), A(u)−a(u)>u^(β−5/2) arbitrarily far out. For p_j≤u<p_{j+1} one has F_j≥A(u)−a(u) by duality. The PNT gives p_j∼u and p_i∼p_j uniformly in the rank window. Summing the increments with ε sufficiently small costs o(p_j^(β−5/2)) and keeps the strict sign on the whole interval. The same reasoning produces runs of length j^κ of the sign F_i<0: for a lower excursion choose D_{p_j} close to the optimal slope as in 640, whose error O(u^(−5/2)log u) is negligible. No length exponent κ=1 is claimed.

Hence it suffices to prove that every sufficiently late rank window [j,j+⌊j^κ⌋] contains at least one F_i≤0 for SOME fixed κ<1. In particular, having fixed in advance any integer m≥2, the eventual sign only at the primes p_{j^m} is equivalent to RH: their jumps in rank are O(r^(1−1/m)) near rank r, so every preceding run with κ>1−1/m intersects them. Round 195 already has runs for other observables; here the result uses the two coupled tails, holds even when Θ is not attained and does not presuppose a sign at neighbouring ranks.

An adverse local maximum F_j>0 with F_j≥F_{j±1} moreover satisfies a filter independent of Θ. By strict concavity of g, for p=p_j and q=p_{j+1},
    p < g'(D_p) < q .
More precisely g'(D_p)−p≥(1/2+o(1))log p and q−g'(D_p)≥(1/2+o(1))log q. Indeed the maximum imposes p≤[g(D_p+w_p)−g(D_p)]/w_p and q≥[g(D_p)−g(D_p−w_q)]/w_q. Expand the two quotients by integrating the derivative: −g''(s)=(2/5)g'(s)/s∼p^(7/2) uniformly on those intervals, by PNT and q∼p. So a local maximum needs a gap q−p≥(1+o(1))log p. The case of primorial minima in 195 uses another functional and neighbours under another orientation. This filter does not exclude such maxima.

Numerical check: the sweep with approximate continuous tail up to 100000 was exploratory and is not used as a certificate. The established unconditional range remains that of 656. A favourable sign per arbitrarily distant window remains to be proved.

### Local sign criterion on two dyadic bands, without prime tails

Let T(u)=A(u)−(3/2)A(2u)+(1/2)A(4u), exclusively in this subsection (it is not the support radius). One has exactly
    T(u)=Σ_{u<p≤2u} (log p)p^(−5/2)(1−u/p)
         + (1/2)Σ_{2u<p≤4u} (log p)p^(−5/2)(4u/p−1).
All summands are nonnegative. The continuous main term is a_T(u)=(4/15)(1−2^(−3/2))(1−2^(−5/2))u^(−3/2). RH holds if and only if T(u)≤a_T(u) for all sufficiently large u. The detector does not require estimating any prime tail beyond 4u. The use of tents is earlier in the phase; the point here is the quantitative competition between squares and phases at log 2 that permits a PURE SIGN criterion for this concrete tent.

Indeed the dilation factor is P(s)=(1−2^(−s))(1−2^(−s−1)). Under RH, by 640,
    u²[T(u)−a_T(u)]=−7/128−Σ_ρ u^(iγ)P(2−iγ)/[(2−iγ)(3−iγ)]+o(1).
The bound by moduli of each dilation separately gives an insufficient budget. For the joint series use M=45/32 and the ceiling M/(1/4+γ²) per zero. The sum of this majorant is M(2+γ_E−log(4π)). Replacing it by the true modulus on the first 600 pairs of ordinates gives an upper bound <0.05442, while 7/128=0.0546875. The program verify_dyadic_ramp_bias.py uses 80-bit Arb intervals and checks that bound; the remaining zeros are covered by the majorant, without truncating them. Hence under RH one has T(u)<a_T(u)−0.00026 u^(−2) eventually. The numerical certificate is for the budget under RH; it does not by itself verify the global sign.

If there were an exterior zero, the centred Mellin transform on [1,∞) would have at s=5/2−ρ the pole of 640 multiplied by P(s)≠0, since Re s>0. The changes of lower endpoints by dilation produce entire functions. Regularity on the real line up to s<2 persists. By Landau, both signs would occur arbitrarily far out, even with excursions larger than u^(−α) in modulus for each 5/2−Reρ<α<2. So proving the continuous ceiling eventually, despite being local in p, remains the global obligation. The previous proofs of positivity of parts and the ordinary PNT do not establish that ceiling.

### Exact audit of the tent against 195:7559

With centre x=2u, let w_x be the earlier tent of 195:7559 and dν(t)=dπ(t)/t−dt/(t log t). The deficit of 688, with the convention sum minus continuum, is EXACTLY
    T(u)−a_T(u)=(3/2)∫w_x(t) log(t)t^(−5/2) dν(t).
On each half-band it suffices to substitute the linear expression of w_x. In particular the three vertices and the ratio of slopes coincide; the difference is the reweighting of the SAME signed measure and the set of centres. The dyadic localization must not be counted as an independent mechanism.

The sufficient condition that would allow transporting the sign from the earlier weight by monotonicity is the ceiling of all prefixes H_x(y)=∫_[u,y] w_x dν≤0, u≤y≤4u. Indeed, with h(t)=log(t)t^(−5/2) decreasing for u≥2, the new integral is (3/2)[h(4u)H_x(4u)+∫_u^(4u)(−h'(y))H_x(y)dy]. But that prefix ceiling is UNCONDITIONALLY FALSE for arbitrarily large real centres. For a prime q take u=q−ε with ε→0 positive: H_x(q) has atomic contribution 2ε/(3q), while the integral up to q costs O(ε²/(q log q)); for ε<1 there is no other integer in (u,q). Hence transporting the sign by imposing each prefix separately is discarded. The example does not decide the sign of the weighted average nor the case of prime centres of 195.

### The edge counterexample can be suppressed while keeping the global equivalence

Take H=u^(3/4)/log u and restrict BOTH sides of 688 to [u+H,4u−H]. Then RH is equivalent to the eventual continuous ceiling also for this trimmed tent. The reason is unconditional: on the two removed pieces, the function integrated against dθ−dt has modulus ≤H u^(−7/2), by the linear formulas of 688. Each piece contains at most H+1 integers, each prime mass is ≤log(4u) and the continuous mass is H. Hence the signed error of the trimming has modulus ≤2Hu^(−7/2)((H+1)log(4u)+H)=O(u^(−2)/log u). The same bound holds for any partial trimming within those two edges, even chosen after observing the primes.

Under RH the strict margin of 688 absorbs this cost eventually. Without RH its positive excursions of order larger than u^(−α), for some α<2, survive. The contribution of both edges, even their worst prefixes, can therefore be excluded uniformly before seeking compensation in the interior. This is a delimitation of the obstacle for the same criterion; the interior inequality is still missing.

### The two halves do not admit individual budgets, not even under RH

Let L,R be the errors prime sum minus integral on (u,2u] and (2u,4u] of 688. Under RH,
    L=K(2u)(θ(2u)−2u)+O(u^(−2)),   R=−K(2u)(θ(2u)−2u)+O(u^(−2)),
where K is the tent including the power and K(2u)=(2u)^(−5/2)/2. Indeed write E(t)=θ(t)−t, J(x)=∫₁ˣ E(t)dt. Under RH, J(x)=O(x^(3/2)): the integrated explicit formula for ψ has series −Σρ x^(1+ρ)/(ρ(ρ+1)) absolutely summable after normalizing, and ψ−θ=O(√x) by Chebyshev and the higher powers. On each half, Stieltjes integration gives the endpoint terms KE and a remainder −∫K'E. A second integration by parts uses J, |K'|≪u^(−7/2), |K''|≪u^(−9/2) on each side separately, giving the indicated error, including the one-sided endpoints.

By Littlewood, E(x)=Ω±(√x log log log x), since replacing ψ by θ costs O(√x). So under RH both halves multiplied by u² are unbounded in both directions, with simultaneous opposite excursions, while their sum is bounded. No split into two constant individual ceilings at scale u^(−2) can be paid for with the bias 7/128. The exact cancellation of the boundary term must precede any bound by moduli. Littlewood's mechanism is classical; its consequence for the cut of 688 is made explicit here.

### Quantitative localization of a tail witness in its own dyadic sequence

Write F=A−a and G=T−a_T, with G=(1−S)(1−S/2)F and Sf(u)=f(2u). For any J≥1 and v=2^J u one has
    F(u)=Σ_{j=0}^{J−1}(2−2^(−j))G(2^j u)+(2−2^(−J))F(v)−(1−2^(−J))F(2v).
It follows by multiplying the truncated polynomial by (1−S)(1−S/2). All interior coefficients are positive. By PNT, F(v)=o(v^(−3/2)); in particular for the minimal J with v≥u^(4/3), the residue is o(u^(−2)) uniformly as u grows.

Hence if u²F(u)≥δ>0, for u sufficiently large SOME dyadic scale y=2^j u<u^(4/3) satisfies
    y²G(y) ≥ (21/32)(δ−o(1)).
Indeed, after normalizing, the sum of weights is ≤Σ(2−2^(−j))4^(−j)=32/21. The constant 21/32 coincides with P(2). The localization retains the exact initial scale u, the sign and a fixed fraction of the normalized amplitude; it requires no information on the ordinates of the zeros. In particular under an exterior zero there are unbounded normalized excesses of F and each sufficiently large one translates into a concrete band with this guarantee. Dyadic inversion as a technique already appeared in 195 (index 36182); here the transfer between these two witnesses is quantified. The ceiling on the selected bands remains pending.

### Semiprime obstruction for a majorant by small divisors

Let X=2√u, H=10 X^(2/3)(log X)^(1/3). Consider a nonnegative majorant summing K(n)log n b_u(n) over (u,4u], where K is the weight of 715. Suppose b_u≥1 on the primes and on the integers without a prime divisor ≤D, with D≤X−H. This includes squares of divisor sums truncated at D normalized by λ₁=1. This majorant exceeds the continuous integral for arbitrarily large u. Under RH it exceeds it for ALL sufficiently large u.

Indeed, under RH the error O(√X log² X) of θ gives (1+o(1))H/(2 log X) primes in (X−H,X−H/2]. Each distinct pair produces a unique semiprime there, sieved with weight b_u≥1. These products n fall in (2u,4u), with X²−n≥XH/2, so u²K(n)=u²(X²−n)/(2n^(7/2))≥H/(64X²). Moreover log n≥log X. The pairs contribute, after normalizing, at least (1+o(1))H³/(512 X² log X)=1000/512+o(1). They are added to the genuine primes, whose normalized deficit under RH is at most 7/128+0.05442+o(1) in modulus by 696. Without RH, the upper excursions of the primes themselves in 688 already prevent the eventual ceiling of any pointwise majorant.

Hence, even with arbitrary weights on the detectable composites, this pointwise sieve with weight log n fails if at ALL scales it stops at that distance before the root of the upper endpoint. The obstruction uses distinct pairs; removing perfect squares does not repair it. The index already records the sieve parity barrier: here the loss is quantified for this tent and a cutoff approaching the root with ratio tending to one. The argument does not apply to signed corrections of semiprimes.

### Unconditional mass of all composites surviving the cutoff

Fix c>0, X=2√u, H=c X^(2/3)(log X)^(1/3), D=X−H. Denote by S_D the sum of K(n)log n over (u,4u] restricted to integers without a prime factor ≤D. Then, UNCONDITIONALLY,
    u²[S_D−T(u)] → c³/24.
Indeed, D³>X² eventually, so the surviving composites are exactly pq≤X² with D<p≤q, necessarily q≤X²/D=X+H+O(H²/X) and pq>2u. In coordinates s=(p−X)/H, t=(q−X)/H, the prime measure (log X)/H per point converges weakly on compacts to Lebesgue. The unconditional PNT on intervals of length ≥X^(7/12+ε) with fixed small ε suffices, applied to each fixed rescaled subinterval. See [Guth, application to short intervals](https://maths-people.anu.edu.au/~plyung/MATRIXworkshop24/MATRIX2024_Guth.pdf). By convergence of product measures one can integrate over the triangle s,t≥−1, s≤t, s+t≤0: the boundaries have measure zero and the condition pq≤X² differs only in a shrinking neighbourhood of the last boundary. This correctly includes the squares, of limiting mass zero. Uniformly over the real domain,
    u²K(pq)log(pq) = H log X/(16 X²) [−s−t+o(1)].
The integral of −s−t over the triangle is 2/3. Multiplying by (H/log X)² gives the claimed constant.

For c=1/6 the normalized cost is 1/5184, strictly less than 7/128−0.05442. By 688, RH is equivalent to the eventual ceiling S_D≤a_T(u) even admitting ALL the semiprimes of this cutoff. This time the complete cost at precision o(u⁻²) is known without RH; what is pending is a ceiling for the sieved set. The short-interval exponent is used in a prime variable close to X, not to approximate the original sum at root precision.

### Signed bracketing by two divisor polynomials with exact cancellation of squares

For u>6, u<n≤4u, omit the n with (n,6)>1 (none is prime). For the others set
    z_n=Σ_{d|n,d≤u} μ(d)log(n/d)/log n.
Every proper divisor is ≤n/5≤u and the term d=n would have logarithm zero. By the classical identity μ*log=Λ one has z_n=Λ(n)/log n: it equals 0 at numbers with two distinct prime factors and 1/k at p^k. The classical antecedent is the von Mangoldt identity, not a new formula for the primes.

With P(z)=z(2z−1), Q(z)=z(2z−1)², one has pointwise P(z_n)≤1_prime(n)≤Q(z_n). The three coincide at each prime, at each prime square and at every n with at least two distinct prime factors. For the remaining powers p^k, k≥3, the difference is O(1). Multiplying by K(n)log n and summing, the width of the bracket is O(u^(−13/6)log² u)=o(u^(−2)) UNCONDITIONALLY: there are O(u^(1/3)log u) powers with k≥3 in the band, and K≪u^(−5/2). This precision holds at each scale. In particular the eventual ceiling for the lower polynomial sum is already equivalent to RH by the excursions of 688; so is the one for the upper sum by the margin of 696.

The cubic majorant has minimal degree among real polynomials taking the values 0,0,1 at 0,1/2,1 and nonnegative at every 1/k: the only quadratic interpolant is P, negative at 1/3. This distinguishes an algebraic cost from an analytic one: the corrections by two and three divisor sums remove the distinct products and the squares exactly. To close one must estimate with sign the sum over integers of those products of divisor sums; the bracketing alone does not bound it by the integral.

### Exact coefficient by least common multiple and cost of breaking the cancellation

For any polynomial B with B(0)=0 and B(1)=1, on grouping B(z_n) by the least common multiple r of its divisors one gets exactly
    C_B(r;n)=μ(r)[1−Σ_{p|r} B(log p/log n)],    B(z_n)=Σ_{r|n} C_B(r;n).
Indeed, fix L=log n and freeze that denominator in all factors. For m|n the partial sum of the elementary coefficients μ(d)(1−log d/L) over d|m equals 1 if m=1 and Λ(m)/L otherwise. d=n may be included since it is zero. The sum of grouped coefficients over r|m is therefore B of that partial value. Only squarefree r intervene as lcm of squarefree divisors; for such r the inversion over their subdivisors only receives terms from m=1 and m prime, giving the formula. It covers simultaneously the lower and upper bracketing of 751.

With B=P or Q, the bracketed factor tends to 1 uniformly at r=n=pq for distinct primes p,q in [√(2u),√(3u)]: both logarithmic ratios are 1/2+O(1/log u) and B(1/2)=0. Since μ(pq)=1, the diagonal r=n by itself receives at these n a positive mass ≫u^(−3/2)/log u, by PNT and K(n)≫u^(−5/2) on [2u,3u]. By contrast the sum over ALL r|n there is exactly zero. In particular an error estimated by the sum of moduli of these diagonal terms would exceed the scale u^(−2) by a divergent factor. The formula removes the multiple sum but cancellation with the proper divisors is still necessary; the diagonal cannot be discarded as a quadrature remainder by size.

### Rigidity of the diagonal at the scale of the sign: two and three primes suffice

Among ALL fixed polynomials B normalized as in 761, only B(t)=t allows the ABSOLUTE mass of C_B(n;n)log n K(n) over products of two or three distinct primes in [2u,3u] to be O(u⁻²). For B(t)=t that diagonal is exactly zero at every squarefree n>1.

Proof. For k=2,3 and any partition 1=Σα_i with α_i>0, let the prime factors p_i come from intervals [a_i u^(α_i),b_i u^(α_i)] with 2<∏a_i<∏b_i<3. The intervals can be chosen disjoint even if exponents repeat, choosing distinct constants around a tuple of product 5/2. There are ≫u/(log u)^k distinct products by PNT and unique factorization. Uniformly, log p_i/log n→α_i and K(n)≫u^(−5/2). If 1−ΣB(α_i)≠0, the absolute contribution is ≫u^(−3/2)/(log u)^(k−1), which exceeds u⁻². Hence the bound forces B(x)+B(1−x)=1 and B(x)+B(y)+B(1−x−y)=1 for all x,y>0 with x+y<1. Subtracting yields additivity; by continuity and B(1)=1 one concludes B(t)=t. The converse follows from Σ_{p|n} log p=log n on squarefree numbers. The same rigidity holds for any continuous profile with the coefficient formula of 761.

The only choice allowing that discard by moduli returns exactly Λ(n), with the original squares. This closes the search for a fixed scalar polynomial that simultaneously suppresses the squares and makes the isolated diagonal negligible by size. It does not exclude signed cancellation between classes or between divisors.

### Moving profiles: necessary slope cost and exact construction

Let B_u be normalized, B_u(1/2)=0, with Lipschitz constant M_u. If the absolute diagonal mass of 769 over semiprimes is O(u⁻²) uniformly, then M_u≫√u. Proof by contradiction along a subsequence with M_u/√u→0. Split [√(2u),√(3u)] into intervals of length h=min(u^(1/4), √(√u/(1+M_u)) log u). Then h/log u→∞ and M_u h/(√u log u)→0. For two distinct primes in the same cell, both logarithmic fractions differ from 1/2 by O(h/(√u log u)); hence C_B(pq;pq)=1+o(1) uniformly. There are N≍√u/log u primes by PNT. By Cauchy there are at least (N²/J−N)/2 pairs where J≪√u/h is the number of cells, and hence ≫√u h/log² u distinct products. Multiplying by K(pq)log(pq)≫u^(−5/2)log u gives ≫u^(−2) h/log u, a contradiction. Only the global count of primes in the band was used; no small individual gaps were assumed.

Constructively, slope O(√u log u) suffices to kill EXACTLY the whole squarefree diagonal while keeping B_u(1/2)=0. Take δ=1/(8√u log(4u)), B_u(t)=t min(1,|t−1/2|/δ). For p|n, m=n/p distinct from p, if p/m lies outside [1/2,2], |log(p/m)|≥log 2. Inside, max(p,m)≤√(8u) and |log(p/m)|≥1/max(p,m) by integer separation. So |log p/log n−1/2|>δ in both cases for large u. In the formula of 761 (valid by inversion also for this profile) some coefficient changes only when reaching n=p². Summing over r, log(n)B_u(z_n) is exactly Λ(n) with the powers of exponent TWO removed; those of exponent ≥3 remain. This identifies an effective escape from the fixed rigidity, but the resulting functional is exactly the subtraction of squares already recorded in 640. Do not reopen it as a source of an arithmetic ceiling.

### The sieve with c=1 crosses its ceiling in both directions unconditionally

In the exact cutoff of 741, c=1 contributes 1/24, LESS than the bias 7/128. Even so limsup u²(S_D−a_T)>0.0057 and liminf u²(S_D−a_T)<−0.0317. If RH fails, 688 and 741 give unbounded excursions in both directions. Under RH, in v=log u the real and absolutely convergent trigonometric series of 696, centred at its mean, has limsup ≥E and liminf ≤−E, with E=(4/3)Σ_{j≤6}|a_j|>0.0187709; a_j is the coefficient of positive frequency γ_j, including its sign. The mean of the normalized sieved sum is 1/24−7/128.

Proof of the excursions under RH. Average against the product of six nonnegative factors F(γ_j v+φ_j), with F(t)=|1+exp(it)+exp(2it)|²/3, of constant term 1 and ±1 coefficients equal to 2/3. For each integer vector k of six entries with |k_j|≤2 and Σ|k_j|≥2 it is certified that |Σk_jγ_j| is nonzero and different from every zero ordinate. The program verify_dyadic_ramp_excursion.py uses Arb at 90 bits and the first 220 ordered zeros, the last of which already exceeds 2Σγ_j. By orthogonality in mean, the product has mean one and in the weighted mean of the series only the six individual pairs intervene. Choosing each phase so that its contribution is positive gives +E; shifting each phase by π gives −E. The nonnegativity of the product bounds those means between the lower and upper limits of the series. Absolute convergence allows averaging term by term. Neither linear independence of the ordinates nor estimation by truncation of high zeros is used.

The mean cost of the semiprimes below the squares bias does not suffice to budget their incorporation. At cutoff c=1 the sieved majorant itself changes sign relative to the continuum; no excess of the primes alone is deduced. The cutoff c=1/6 of 741 keeps the earlier equivalence.

### An average over one octave recovers the criterion at the cutoff that fails pointwise

Use exactly the sieve of 783 with c=1. Let Z(v)=v²(S_D(v)−a_T(v)), with the cutoff D evaluated at v, and M(u)=∫_[u,2u] Z(v)dv/v divided by log 2. Then RH is equivalent to M(u)≤0 eventually. Under RH one even has M(u)<−0.0017 eventually, although Z(v)>0.0057 arbitrarily far out.

Indeed the constant contribution is 1/24−7/128 by 741. Each critical frequency of 696 is multiplied by (2^(iγ)−1)/(iγ log 2), of modulus ≤2/(14 log 2) since the first ordinate exceeds 14. By the certificate of 697 the averaged fluctuation has modulus ≤0.05442·2/(14 log 2)<0.01122. The o(1) remains uniform over each late octave. In the other direction first apply the average to the PRIME error G of 723: the Mellin transform of M_prime(u)/u² is the Mellin transform of G multiplied by (2^(2−s)−1)/((2−s)log 2), plus an entire term from the lower endpoints. No exterior pole s=5/2−ρ with Re ρ>1/2 is cancelled. Regularity on the real segment of 688 is preserved. Landau in both orientations as there gives unbounded normalized excursions; adding the sieved correction 1/24+o(1) preserves them. This treats the correction without attributing a meromorphic continuation to it.

The criterion is a single finite arithmetic sum. For each integer u<n≤8u integrate v K_v(n) over v∈[u,2u]∩[n/4,n] where D(v)<p_min(n), and multiply by log n. D eventually increases, so it suffices to trim the right endpoint. The primitives before and after n/2 are respectively n^(−5/2)(2v³/(3n)−v²/4) and n^(−5/2)(v²/2−v³/(3n)); use differences on each piece. By finite interchange the required ceiling for that sum is exactly 2C(√2−1)√u, with C=(4/15)(1−2^(−3/2))(1−2^(−5/2)). What is open is to prove this ceiling arithmetically. Multiplicative averages already exist in the phase; the verifiable distinction here is that for the same sieved set the pointwise ceiling is false and the one-octave ceiling is equivalent.

### Six integer readings by quadratic index suffice at cutoff c=1

For Z of 791, RH is equivalent to the eventual inequality over integers m
    Σ_{j=0}^5 Z(13^(5−j)14^j m²) ≤ 0.
Each reading uses only the indicator of absence of small prime factors of 741. There is no integral over scales. Under RH the average of the six readings is <−0.0017 eventually. No claim of minimality of the number six.

First take u real, r=14/13 and average Z(r^j u). The multiplier in 696 is (1/6)Σ_{j=0}^5 exp(ijγ log r). The program verify_rough_scale_average.py certifies with 80-bit Arb the joint moduli on the first 600 pairs, and uses the majorant of 697 for all the others (the average has modulus ≤1). The bias 7/128 minus 1/24 minus the certified budget exceeds 0.0017296. Without RH, for the prime part the Mellin multiplier of 791 is replaced by (1/6)Σr^(j(2−s)). Its roots require |r^(2−s)|=1, impossible at the exterior pole with Re s<2. Landau again gives unbounded excursions in both directions for the normalized average. The additional mass of the composites is 1/24+o(1).

To pass to u_m=13^5 m² asymptotic continuity at distances O(√u) suffices. In the prime part write G=∫K_u(t)d(θ(t)−t). One has G=o(u^(−3/2)) and G'=o(u^(−5/2)) away from the breaks: ∂_u K_u is −t^(−7/2) on (u,2u) and 2t^(−7/2) on (2u,4u), zero outside. Its total variation including the jumps is O(u^(−7/2)), so integration by parts and sup_[u,4u]|θ(t)−t|=o(u) give the bound for G'. Continuity of the original weight guarantees that G is locally absolutely continuous across the breaks. So (u²G)'=o(u^(−1/2)) almost everywhere, uniformly in the tails. The average of the six prime readings keeps this modulus of variation, and adding the sieved part, convergent after normalizing, only alters the differences by o(1). Rounding to the nearest u_m costs o(1), even along the excursions if there were an exterior zero. It remains to prove the discrete inequality uniformly for large m; the discretization does not enlarge the Weil window.

### Positive average of integer shifts uniform modulo each small d

For each scale reading v of 799 fix the SAME cutoff D=D(v) before shifting. Let P be the product of the primes ≤D and W(n)=K_v(n)log n extended by zero. There is an explicit symmetric law of integer shifts H such that H is exactly uniform modulo each d≤Y=⌊√v/log v⌋, while
    v² |Σ_n W(n) E 1_{(n+H,P)=1} − S_D(v)| = O(1/log² v).
This allows removing simultaneously from the arithmetic problem all nonconstant periodic frequencies of reduced denominator ≤Y without losing the critical scale. Joint uniformity modulo the primorial is not assumed.

Construction: for each integer j between 1 and Y take two independent U_j,V_j uniform on {0,…,j−1}, all variables independent, and sum H=Σ(U_j−V_j). The factor j=d guarantees uniformity modulo d. The variance is Σ(j²−1)/6=O(Y³). For an integer step h, the sum over n of the moduli of W(n+h)+W(n−h)−2W(n) is at most h²||Δ²W||₁ by the triangular telescoping identity for differences. In turn ||Δ²W||₁≤TV(W')≪v^(−7/2)log v by the two formulas of 688, counting the slope jumps at the three vertices. Symmetry, the change of variable and 0≤1_coprime≤1 give the claimed error. The arithmetic of the shifts uses neither RH nor the short PNT.

The finite Fourier expansion modulo P of the indicator leaves, after this convolution, only the constant φ(P)/P and characters of reduced denominator >Y. The multiplier at each character is real nonnegative by the construction as a difference of independent copies. The constant term alone contributes (2exp(−γ_E)+o(1))a_T(v): by Mertens φ(P)/P∼exp(−γ_E)/log D, log D∼(log v)/2 and Σ W(n)∼log(v)a_T(v) by Riemann sum. Since 2exp(−γ_E)>1 and S_D(v)∼a_T(v) by PNT and 741, the remaining frequencies already have a first-order NEGATIVE balance (1−2exp(−γ_E)+o(1))a_T(v). The global step requires certifying the balance relative to the EXACT constant term at precision v⁻² and with sign for the six readings. Replacing that term by its Mertens equivalent loses that precision. Averaging all translations modulo P would also destroy the necessary balance.

### Removal of ALL the characters of a single prime, with negligible critical cost

In 809 one can take Y=⌊v^(3/4)/log v⌋ and suppress exactly EVERY character of frequency θ with distance to the nearest integer ≥1/Y, in addition to those of reduced denominator between 2 and Y. In particular Y>D(v) eventually: all nonconstant characters of each prime factor of the primorial disappear, even those of p close to the cutoff. The normalized error is O(1/log v), without RH.

Construction on the circle of length 1. Let f(t)=cos(πYt) for |t|≤1/(2Y), zero outside, extended periodically. Give the integer shift h probability |f̂(h)|²/||f||₂², with character exp(2πiht). By Parseval the mass is one, and by symmetry the mean is zero. The multiplier is the normalized autocorrelation of f, so it is nonnegative and vanishes for ||θ||≥1/Y by disjointness of the supports except at endpoints. The function is continuous at the edges and its weak derivative is in L². By differentiated Parseval the exact variance is ||f'||₂²/(4π²||f||₂²)=Y²/4. The triangular sum of second differences of 809 gives the cost O(v^(−3/2)log(v)Y²). The shifts may be unbounded: the indicator is periodic over ALL integers and the triangular bound holds for any step. There remains therefore a band of frequencies of width 2/Y and, apart from the constant, only denominators composed of at least two distinct primes.
