Let W_T(y)=y^(−s−1)(1+y/T)^(−2), I_T=∫₁^∞ E W_T, finite already by E≤y². For G(y)=E(y)−cE(cy)≥0 the substitution u=cy and (1+u/(cT))^(−2)≥c²(1+u/T)^(−2) give ∫G W_T≤(1−c^(s+3))I_T. Moreover ∫Σ_d G(y/d)W_T(y)dy≤ζ(s)∫G W_T by positivity and change of variable. In the divisor sum of P use the hypothesis with 0<ε<s−1; the majorant of D+constant, integrated after dilations, costs O_s(1) uniformly in T. Consequently I_T≤O_s(1)+4ζ(s)(1−c^(s+3))I_T. Choose c so that the factor is less than one and pass to T→∞ by monotone convergence. Since yE(y) is increasing, integrating on [X,2X] gives E(X)≪_s X^s. This holds for all 1<s<2, which implies RH by 1144. The converse follows from D≤P. 

The control of the diagonal is substantial: with independent unrestricted changes, two primes p<q<y and changes +a,−a give curvature −2(1−q/y)a², so that the infimum would be −∞. The new minimum still lacks the required one-sided bound.

### Dimension of the spaces of admissible adjustments and explicit minimum

For h=y/log y, exclude p=y (its integrated weight is zero), and call d(y) the largest dimension of a linear subspace of adjustments in (y−h,y) whose combinations satisfy ∫H²≥2∫V, as in 1216. One has √h/log y ≪ d(y) ≪ √h. In particular d(y)=y^(1/2+o(1)). This measures entire subspaces contained in the admissible set, without claiming that this set is a space.

Upper bound. In each band of distances y−p∈[a,2a) with a a power of two ≥1, subdivide into intervals of length ℓ≤√a/2, using O(1+√a) intervals. Impose that the sum of b over each interval be zero and separately annihilate the coordinates at distance <1. These are O(√h) equations in total. For a vector in the kernel, the prefix H starts and ends at zero in each cell. By Cauchy and integer spacing, ∫cell H²≤ℓ(ℓ+1)Σcell b²<2aΣcell b², while ∫₀ʸ V=Σ_p(y−p)b_p². Summing, every nonzero kernel vector violates admission. An admissible space then injects into the value space of the equations.

Lower construction. Use successive blocks of 2k primes with k=⌈4√h⌉. The pulse is +1 on the first k and −1 on the rest. In the k gaps after the first k primes the prefix takes the values 1 to k and each gap has length at least one. Therefore ∫H²≥Σ_{j≤k}j²≥k³/3≥4kh, while ∫V≤2kh. The profiles H of distinct blocks have disjoint supports and their vectors use distinct coordinates; admission holds for every real combination. The classical zero-free region gives π(y)−π(y−h)∼h/log y (the remainder O(y/log³ y) in the li approximation suffices). One gets ≫√h/log y complete blocks.

In this space the optimal choice is explicit. If H_j,V_j are the individual profiles, the minimum is P−Σ_j (avg H_j(L+1))²/avg(H_j²−V_j). Every denominator is positive by construction. A root upper bound in the sense of 1216 for this residual would be sufficient and under RH is necessary; this follows by inclusion between the minimum of 1216 and P. The identity eliminates numerical optimization within this space. The arithmetic bound on the residual remains open.

### Scales where every terminal adjustment loses relative efficacy, under an outer zero

If there exists a zero of ζ with real part β>1/2, then for each 1<a<2β there is a sequence y→∞ with P(y)>y^a and D(y)/P(y)→1, where D is the infimum over ALL adjustments of 1216. This is stronger than the mere absence of a uniform bound for D. At those same scales no admissible selection can remove even a fixed positive fraction of the excess.

Proof. Choose a<s<2β. For W_T and I_T of 1216 one has I_T→∞: if ∫E(y)y^(−s−1)dy were finite, the monotonicity of yE(y) would give E(y)≪y^s. The Cauchy and Mellin proof of 1144 would continue ζ(2z)/ζ(z) to the half-plane Re z>s/2, contradicting the zero. By E(y)=Σ_d P(y/d) and the change of scale for positive functions of 1216, ∫ P₊ W_T ≥ I_T/ζ(s), with P₊=max(0,P).

Let F(y)=2 avg_terminal(L+1)², and zero where D=P by the convention of 1216. For each c<1 close to one, F≤4[E(y)−cE(cy)]+O_c(1) at all scales, and 0≤P−D≤F. Upon integrating, the quotient ∫F W_T/I_T has upper limit ≤4(1−c^(s+3)); letting c→1 proves that the quotient tends to zero. For fixed η>0, the scales with P≤y^a contribute to ∫P₊W_T at most 1/(s−a), and those with P>0 and F≥ηP contribute at most ∫F W_T/η=o(I_T). Also any compact contributes O(1). In the complement there therefore remain arbitrarily large scales with P>y^a and (P−D)/P<η. Successively take η→0. The argument covers the block space of 1226 by inclusion. Conditional result on the efficacy of the adjustment; it does not decide whether an outer zero exists.

### Prefixed sparse sampling of the coprime criterion, even after minimizing

Let 1≤x₁<x₂<…→∞ with x_{j+1}/x_j→1. The one-sided bound D(x_j)≤Kε x_j^(1+ε) for all j and all ε>0 suffices; under RH it holds even for P(x_j). For example x_j=⌊exp(√j)⌋, omitting repetitions, requires only O(log² X) integer scales up to X. The result concerns the signed coprime sum and the minimum of 1216, which lack the monotonicity of yE(y).

Indeed, by the inversion of 1144, yP(y)=Σ_d μ(d)∫₀ʸ L(t/d)²dt. Its derivative in absolute value is dominated almost everywhere by U(y)=Σ_d L(y/d)². Let x be the sample immediately preceding y and fix 1<s<2. For large y and prescribed δ>0, y≤(1+δ)x. Then
    P(y)≤(x/y)D(x)+(x/y)F(x)+y⁻¹∫ₓʸ U(t)dt
with F of 1236. For c<1 close to 1, choosing δ sufficiently small and y sufficiently large, the terminal band of x is contained in [cy,y]. Thus (x/y)F(x)≤4[E(y)−cE(cy)]+4. On the initial compact a constant is enlarged.

Integrate the last error against W_T of 1216. By Fubini, each t of the large cells only contributes for t≤y≤(1+δ)t. The monotonicity of W_T gives cost ≤δ∫U W_T≤δζ(s)∫L²W_T. Since (yE)'=L², integrating by parts and using −yW_T′≤(s+3)W_T gives ∫L²W_T≤(s+3)I_T (boundary term zero by E≤y²). In the sum over dilations for E the positive majorant of each term is used, whose integral cost is multiplied by at most ζ(s). With 0<ε<s−1 one obtains
    I_T≤O_s(1)+[4ζ(s)(1−c^(s+3))+δζ(s)²(s+3)]I_T.
Choose first c and then δ so that the coefficient is less than one. The passage to the limit of 1216 concludes the proof. A finite table of samples does not verify the asymptotic hypothesis.

### A bounded-gcd subtotal at each power of two for a prescribed line

Fix 1<s<2 and an integer K with θ=2^(s+1)ζ(s)Σ_{d>K}d^(−s)<1. Let S_K(x)=Σ_{d≤K}P(x/d), exactly the contribution of the pairs with gcd≤K to the complete triangular Liouville energy. If S_K(2^j)≤C_s 2^(js) for all j≥0, then E(x)≪_s x^s and there are no zeros with Re ρ>s/2. K is fixed for each line and all cancellation among its classes is allowed. Unlike 986, the Liouville energy, complete multiplicativity and a contraction from dyadic samples are used.

Proof. Let M_J be the supremum of E(u)/u^s on [1,2^J]. By inversion, |P(v)|≤M_J ζ(s)v^s there, and P(v)=0 below one. For dyadic x≤2^J, the remainder of the gcd sum satisfies E(x)−S_K(x)≤M_J ζ(s)x^s Σ_{d>K}d^(−s). For u in [1,2^J], take the smallest power of two x≥u. The monotonicity of uE(u) implies E(u)/u^s≤2^(s+1) E(x)/x^s. Taking the supremum one obtains (1−θ)M_J≤2^(s+1) C_s, uniformly in J. Apply 1144 for the exclusion of zeros. Under RH the bound on the subtotal holds for all s>1 by the bound on P; thus obtaining the inequalities for s arbitrarily close to one would suffice for the global sign.

Strictly conditional example: s=3/2 and K=1600 work, since ζ(3/2)≤3 and the tail is ≤2/√1600. Proving S_1600(2^j)≪2^(3j/2) would exclude Re ρ>3/4. That inequality has not been proved. For a sequence s↓1 this estimate of the contraction requires growing K.

### Which products can already be paid in the subtotal of 1600 classes

Let q be the squarefree kernel of mn (product of the primes with odd exponent). In S_K(x) the contribution of ALL pairs with q≤R, even after taking moduli term by term, is ≤2 H_K x √R (1+log R) for R≥1, with H_K=Σ_{d≤K}1/d. Indeed extract d=gcd(m,n); the coprime primitive parts are a u² and b v², with a,b squarefree and ab=q. For each a,b,d there are at most x/(d√(ab)) possibilities and the weight is ≤1. Summing first over b≤R/a using Σ_{b≤z}1/√b≤2√z and then over a proves the bound. For K=1600 and R=x/(log x)² at large scales, these pairs already satisfy O(x^(3/2)) unconditionally. In the criterion for the line 3/4 it only remains to obtain the one-sided bound over q>R. The sign of each remaining term is λ(q).

Independent finite verification of that estimate. In verify_dyadic_gcd_subtotal.cpp the exact subtotal was evaluated for x=2^j, 0≤j≤22. Using c(n)=Σ_{d|n,d≤K}μ(n/d), accumulated L and area A(q)=Σ_{i<q}L(i)²; the integer numerator xS_K is Σ_n c(n)[nA(⌊x/n⌋)+(x mod n)L(⌊x/n⌋)²] by inversion. In all these samples 0≤S_K/x<0.267; at x=4194304 the numerator is 532074504340. The independent enumeration by pairs gave 1023268 and 1367736 for x=2048 and 4096 respectively, coinciding. No empirical fit of a constant intervenes in the bound over q≤R.

### Signed cancellation up to q=x(log x)^B for every fixed B

In 1264 absolute values were taken before summing the kernels. Keeping the sign of the larger of the two squarefree parts, for fixed K and fixed B,D≥0 the whole subtotal q≤R=x(log x)^B is O_{K,B,D}(x^(3/2)/log^D x) in modulus. This allows enlarging the already paid region beyond x by any fixed power of the logarithm.

Necessary uniform detail. For k≤x², x^(1/5)≤t≤x and every fixed A, M_k(t)=Σ_{a≤t,(a,k)=1}μ(a)≪_A t/log^A x. Indeed M_k(t)=Σ_{e≤t, p|e⇒p|k} M(t/e) by convolution. Up to e≤√t use the classical bound with A+2 logarithmic powers and Σ1/e≤∏_{p|k}(1−1/p)^(−1)≤ω(k)+1≪log x (the i-th prime divisor is ≥i+1). For the tail, Σ_{e>√t}1/e≤t^(−1/8)∏_{p|k}(1−p^(−3/4))^(−1)≤t^(−1/8)exp(O((log x)^(1/4))). This absorbs every logarithmic power.

Extract the gcd d and write the primitive pair (au²,bv²) as in 1264. Split according to which kernel is larger, resolving ties once. If both are <x^(1/5), q≤x^(2/5) and 1264 gives O_K(x^(6/5)log x). In the sector a larger, fix d,b,u,v, keeping b squarefree and (u,bv)=1. The sign is μ(a)μ(b) with (a,bv)=1. The allowed interval for a has upper endpoint Z=min(R/b,x/(du²)), lower ≥x^(1/5); the triangular weight is monotone between 0 and 1. By partial summation and the preceding uniform bound, the signed cost is O_A(Z/log^A x) for each nonempty choice (an integer endpoint costs at most 1). To sum Z, use Z≤2⌊Z⌋ and count all a≤Z, removing the coprimality and order restrictions. The same count of 1264 costs O_K(x√R(1+log R)); the proof of that majorant already allowed all integers a,b. Swap the coordinates in the other sector. Choose A>D+B/2+2. The estimate uses only classical Mertens and does not provide a fixed exponent gain on the boundary q≈x.

By contrast, the absolute cost for R=x is already ≫x^(3/2)log x: use d=1 and boxes a∈[A,2A), b∈[x/(8A),x/(4A)), with A dyadic between x^0.3 and x^0.6, u between √(x/A)/8 and √(x/A)/4 and v between √A/8 and √A/4. Each box has volume of order x^(3/2), weight ≥1/2, and a uniform positive proportion with a,b squarefree and (au,bv)=1. For this impose conditions at primes ≤P by Chinese remainders; the local density is positive and ≥1−6/p² (two forbidden squares and four cross divisibilities), so the product has a positive limit. The relative cost of primes >P is O(Σ_{n>P}1/n²): in an interval [M,2M] the number of multiples of k is O(M/k) for k≤2M and zero for k>2M. All sides grow uniformly and P is fixed before letting x grow. Valid representations are unique and the boxes are disjoint in a. Thus genuine cancellation is needed already for q≤x.

### Localization on two kernels almost of the size of the scale

Let Θ be the supremum of the real parts of the nontrivial zeros, and suppose Θ>1/2. In the primitive coordinates (au²,bv²) of 1264, for fixed K the rectangular signed subtotal a≤A, b≤B, with 1≤A,B≤x, has modulus
    ≪_{K,ε} x^(1+ε)(AB)^(Θ−1/2).
Therefore, for any fixed 0<δ<1, the subtotal with min(a,b)≤x^(1−δ) is O_{K,ε}(x^(2Θ−δ(Θ−1/2)+ε)), by inclusion and exclusion of three rectangles. It is a power gain relative to the exponent of the hypothetical witness, without fixing the position of Θ in advance.

Proof. Integrate the indicator m,n≤t over 0≤t≤x with dt/x to recover the triangular weight. Fix d≤K and (u,v)=1. The restrictions are (a,v)=(b,u)=(a,b)=1 with signs μ(a)μ(b). Remove the last coprimality via Σ_{e|a,b}μ(e). Only squarefree e coprime to uv contribute. Substitute a=e a', b=e b': two independent Möbius sums appear with coprimalities ev and eu. For θ=Θ+η with small η>0, the classical bound M(z)≪η z^θ and the convolution of 1270 give |M_k(z)|≪ x^η z^θ uniformly for 1≤z≤x, k≤x²: the product ∏_{p|k}(1−p^(−θ))^(−1) is x^o(1) upon ordering the O(log x) prime divisors (also if Θ=1 by the trivial bound). Each sum thus costs at most x^η e^(−θ) min(A,x/u²)^θ or the analogue with B,v. The sum over e converges since 2θ>1. Splitting u at √(x/A) one obtains Σ_u min(A,x/u²)^θ ≪_θ √x A^(θ−1/2). Multiply the two bounds and choose η small relative to ε.

Concrete consequence: to exclude Re ρ>3/4 it suffices to prove the ceiling O(x^(3/2)) at the scales x=2^j for the subtotal with d≤1600 and BOTH a,b>x^0.99. Indeed if Θ>3/4, take 3/2<s<2Θ with s larger than 2Θ−0.01(Θ−1/2). The other sectors are O(x^s), choosing ε sufficiently small. The proposed ceiling would give S_1600(2^j)≪x^s. The contraction of 1256 remains valid for that s, because each factor 2^(s+1)d^(−s) of the tail decreases with s and so does ζ(s). One would get Θ≤s/2, a contradiction. For the global exclusion the same argument applies to each line σ>1/2 with K chosen in 1256 for s=2σ<2. The exponent 0.99 can be replaced by any fixed constant less than one and greater than zero. The bound for the remaining sector is not proved.

### Criterion via comparable, separated squarefree pairs of bounded gcd

For each 1<s<2 there exists a finite K(s) with the following property. At x=2^j take I=[x/4,x] and the covariance kernel W_x of 986 with l=x/4. The upper bound O_s(x^s) for
    Σ μ(m)μ(n)W_x(m,n),   m,n∈I, (m,n)≤K(s), |m−n|>x^(s−1),
suffices to exclude the zeros with Re ρ>s/2. The bound is required only at the dyadic scales. The variance idea and the gcd inversion are in 986; here the overlap and a uniform absorption allow fixing K and the ratio of the band endpoints. K(3/2) is not identified with 1600, which corresponded to another kernel.

Let E_M(u)=u⁻¹∫₀ᵘM(t)²dt and N_J=sup_{1≤u≤2^J} E_M(u)/u^s. Write V_j and b_j for the variance and the mean on I_j=[2^j/4,2^j]. If V_j≤B 2^(js) up to J, Cauchy on I_{j−1}∩I_j gives |b_j−b_{j−1}|≤√3(√V_j+√V_{j−1}), since the lengths of both intervals are at most three times that of the overlap. Summing geometrically from j=0 gives |b_j|≪_s 1+√B 2^(js/2). Each [2^(j−1),2^j] lies in I_j; summing the integrals and passing to the right dyadic endpoint one obtains N_J≤C_s(1+B), with C_s independent of J.

The variance V_j has the expansion of 986. Removing |m−n|≤x^(s−1) in the small-gcd sector costs O(x^s) by counting. For the other remainder, the inversion of 986 gives coefficients c_d vanishing up to K, |c_d|≤τ(d), on variances of the partial sums over multiples of d. For d squarefree such sums are μ(d)Σ_e M(t/(de)) with e supported on the primes of d. In normalized quadratic norm on I_j, each function M(t/(de)) has norm ≤√(4N_J/3)(x/(de))^(s/2) by change of scale; for d with a square factor the original sum is zero. By the triangle inequality in norm and variance≤second moment, the remainder in absolute value is ≤N_J x^s T_K, with
    T_K=(4/3)Σ_{d>K}τ(d)d^(−s)∏_{p|d}(1−p^(−s/2))^(−2).
The tail tends to zero: both τ(d) and the product are O_{ε,s}(d^ε), the latter by the prime-by-prime comparison of 986. Thus B≤O_s(1)+T_K N_J under the requested ceiling. Choose K so that C_s T_K<1/2. One gets E_M(u)≪_s u^s, and the Mellin argument of 1144, now with the series 1/ζ, excludes Re ρ>s/2. Conversely under RH the second moment satisfies the bound for all s>1 and the same estimates bound the subtotal. The arithmetic estimate of the indicated sum remains open.

### Counterexample to replacing the variance by finitely many dyadic linear means

For 1/2<β<1 and any r fixed real weights w_i in L¹([1/4,1]), there exist coefficients a_n with |a_n|=μ(n)² such that all means ∫ A(xu)w_i(u)du are O_i(x^(β−1/2)) for x=2^j, where A(t)=Σ_{n≤t}a_n. However the variance of A on [x/4,x] is asymptotic to c x^(2β) with c>0 at those same scales and the Dirichlet series of a_n has a pole of real part β. This sampling counterexample does not impose Möbius multiplicativity.

Choose a nonzero real trigonometric polynomial F of period one, from a space of dimension >r, in the kernel of the r linear equations ∫u^β F(log₂u)w_i(u)du=0. Put f(t)=t^β F(log₂ t), with derivative O(t^(β−1)). Let Q(t)=Σ_{n≤t}μ(n)²=ct+O(√t), c=1/ζ(2), by the identity μ²(n)=Σ_{d²|n}μ(d). For k sufficiently large round f(k/c) to the nearest integer B_k with the parity of k. Rounding changes the value by ≤1 and, since the increment of f(k/c) has modulus <1, B_k−B_{k−1} is odd and of modulus <3, hence ±1. One can start at B_0=0 and join with any B_K of the tail through K steps ±1 since |B_K|≤K. Assign those steps in order to the squarefree integers and zero to the rest. Thus A(t)=B_{Q(t)}=f(t)+O(1+t^(β−1/2)) by the mean value theorem.

At x=2^j one has f(xu)=x^βu^β F(log₂u), which proves the linear bounds. The variance normalized by x^(2β) converges to the variance of u^βF(log₂u), which is positive: if that profile were constant, comparing u and 2u in the band would force the constant zero and F identically zero. Finally s∫₁^∞A(t)t^(−s−1)dt is the original series for Re s>1. The error defines a holomorphic function for Re s>β−1/2 and each nonzero mode of F produces a distinct pole at β+2πik/log 2. This rules out as a general argument even several means per scale with bounds at root level and with exact squarefree support; the step would need additional information about the Möbius signs.

### Which prime rules preserve the dyadic means counterexample

Let S be a set of primes with Σ_{p∈S}p^(−1/4)<∞. It can include all primes up to any fixed threshold and in addition infinitely many primes (for example adding one larger than 2^(8j) for each j, in increasing order). The model of 1302 can be constructed with a₁=1 and the exact rule a_{pn}=−a_n for ALL p∈S and p∤n, in addition to |a_n|=μ(n)². Its means are still O(x^γ) with γ=max(1/4,β−1/2)<1/2 and the pole of real part β persists.

Indeed the squarefree integers without factors in S have counter Q_S(t)=c_S t+O_S(√t), c_S=ζ(2)^(−1)∏_{p∈S}(1+1/p)^(−1)>0. Convolve the counter of μ² with the coefficients of ∏_{p∈S}(1+p^(−s))^(−1); its absolute series converges at 1/2, which controls both the remainder and the continuation of the main sum to infinity. Construct on those integers by parity rounding a sequence of signs with sum f(t)+O(1+t^(β−1/2)) as in 1302, for f(t)=t^β G(log₂t). The initial joining can start with +1 since |B_K|=o(K). Extend to a squarefree integer n=dm by multiplying its sign at m by μ(d), with d formed by all the factors in S. For the other integers put zero. The extended sum is Σ_d μ(d) A_base(t/d) over squarefree d supported on S. The error is O_S(t^γ) by convergence at γ, including the tail d>t of the main profile (use (t/d)^β≤(t/d)^γ there). At each frequency τ=2πk/log 2 the main profile acquires the multiplier ∏_{p∈S}(1−p^(−β−iτ)), convergent and nonzero. Dividing beforehand the coefficients of the desired profile F of 1302 by those multipliers preserves the conjugate symmetry and restores exactly F at the end. Apply the means, variance and Mellin argument of 1302.

At the other extreme there is a positive implication. If the same rules are imposed on S and the EXEMPTED primes satisfy Σ_{p∉S}p^(−1/2)<∞, every outer pole of the model requires a real or complex zero of ζ at that same point. In Re s>1 the series of the model is C(s)∏_{p∈S}(1−p^(−s)), where C sums the coefficients of squarefree integers supported on the complement. Then ζ(s) times the series of the model continues as C(s)∏_{p∉S}(1−p^(−s))^(−1), holomorphic in Re s>1/2 by absolute convergence. No multiplicativity among the exempted primes is presupposed. The two regimes are sufficient; the interval between them is not classified.

### How many dyadic linear means suffice to detect an outer zero

At x=2^j let m_k(x)=∫_{1/2}¹ M(xu)(−log u/log 2)^k du. Fix any c>0 and, for large x, N(x)=⌊c log x/log log x⌋. The bound |m_k(x)|≤Cε x^(1/2+ε) uniformly in 0≤k≤N(x) and on the dyadic scales suffices to conclude RH. More generally the right side can be multiplied by B^k k! for any fixed positive B. The converse of the requirement without that factor follows directly from the classical bound for M under RH. It is a two-sided condition on signed moments.

Proof. Let Θ be the supremum of the real parts of the nontrivial zeros; |M(t)|≪η t^(Θ+η) (for Θ=1 the trivial bound suffices). In the block of the Mellin integral with complex variable z appears x^(−z)∫_{1/2}¹M(xu)u^(−z−1)du. Write u^(−z−1)=exp((z+1)log(2)v), v=−log u/log 2∈[0,1]. On every compact set of z, the Taylor remainder after degree N has modulus ≤e^R R^(N+1)/(N+1)! with R fixed, hence is ≤x^(−c+o(1)) by Stirling. The integrated polynomial part costs Oε(x^(1/2+ε)) under the simple hypothesis. Even with B^k k! the additional sum costs ≤(N+1)max(1,BR)^N=x^o(1). Therefore the blocks form a locally uniformly convergent series in Re z>max(1/2,Θ−c) (choose ε,η smaller than the margins of the compact). Each block is an entire function of z. Multiplying the sum by z continues 1/ζ(z), initially in Re z>1, to that half-plane. If Θ>1/2, by definition there is a zero strictly inside, a contradiction. It is not necessary that the supremum Θ be attained.

This gives an alternative to the variance of 1290 and quantifies a sufficient regime against the fixed-degree countermodel of 1302. It proves neither the optimality of N nor the bounds on the actual moments of M.

### One-sided moments and a single lower anchor at much more widely spaced scales

An asymmetric version of 1318 uses N=⌊c log x/log log x⌋ at x=2^j and the means b_{k,j}=∫_{1/2}¹ M(xu) binom(N,k)v^k(1−v)^(N−k)du, v=−log₂u. It suffices that (i) b_{k,j}≤Cε x^(1/2+ε) uniformly in k,j and (ii) ∫₁^X M(t)dt ≥−Cε X^(3/2+ε) at X=2^(l²). Both conditions are necessary under RH. The second uses only one unweighted integral at the end of each group of octaves.

To prove it, put m_j=Σ_k b_{k,j}=∫_{1/2}¹M(2^ju)du by the Bernstein partition. From (i) follows m_i≤(N_i+1)Cε 2^(i(1/2+ε)). Choose X=2^(⌈√j⌉²). In ∫₁^X M(t)dt=Σ_{1≤i≤log₂ X}2^i m_i, set aside the term i=j and bound all the others from above. The geometric progression and (ii) give 2^j m_j≥−Oε(X^(3/2+2ε)); since X=(2^j)^(1+O(1/√j)), one gets m_j≥−Oη(x^(1/2+η)) for all η>0, adjusting ε. Then Σ_k |b_{k,j}|=2Σ_k(b_{k,j})₊−m_j≪η x^(1/2+η). The argument is likewise valid with endpoints 2^(j_l) such that j_{l+1}/j_l→1.

In the Bernstein basis of degree N, the monomial v^r has coefficients binom(k,r)/binom(N,r) (zero for k<r), as follows from the binomial identity. The Taylor polynomial of exp(Cv) up to N thus has coefficients of modulus ≤exp(|C|), uniformly in N. Its integral is controlled by the preceding absolute sum. The tail and the passage to Mellin are exactly those of 1318. This criterion concentrates all the lower control at the anchors; both the upper inequalities and the arithmetic lower control at those endpoints are still missing.

### Without a lower anchor: criterion of polynomial moments from above only

Fix c>0. For b=2^j≥2 define I_k(b)=∫_{1/2}¹ M(bu)[2(1−u)]^k du and N_b=⌊c log b⌋. It suffices that I_k(b)≤Cε b^(1/2+ε) for each ε>0, uniformly in 0≤k≤N_b and b. Under RH it holds even with moduli. Compared with 1326 the family of weights is changed and the number of degrees is raised up to O(log b), eliminating the lower condition. The one-sided step uses the real regularity of ζ, specific to the arithmetic and absent in the general countermodels.

Proof. Let F(X)=X⁻¹∫₀^∞ M(t)exp(−t/X)dt. For t=bu in an octave write A=b/X, v=2(1−u)∈[0,1]. The weight is exp(−A)Σ_{k≥0}(Av/2)^k/k!. The positive coefficients allow bounding the part up to N_b from above using only the hypothesis; before the factor b/X, it costs ≤Cε b^(1/2+ε)exp(−A/2). The remainder of the weight in uniform norm is ≤(3/2)^(−N_b)exp(−A/4), upon multiplying the k-th power by (3/2)^k. With |M(t)|≪η t^(Θ+η), the contribution of that part before the factor b/X costs Oη(b^(Θ+η−c log(3/2))exp(−A/4)). Let h=max(1/2,Θ−c log(3/2)). Summing b/X times both majorants over dyadic b one obtains F(X)≤C'ε X^(h+ε) for X≥1: both powers of b can be bounded by b^(h+ε), and the sum of (b/X)^(1+h+ε)exp(−b/(4X)) is uniformly bounded.

For Re s>1, the Mellin transform ∫₀^∞ F(X)X^(−s−1)dX is Γ(s+1)/(sζ(s)), by Fubini. The integral over X<1 is entire by the exponential decay at the origin. If Θ>1/2 choose h<q<Θ. With a suitable constant G(X)=C X^q−F(X)≥0 on X≥1. Its integral transform, initially to the right of 1, has a meromorphic continuation regular at each real point >q: ζ has no positive real zeros, and its pole at 1 is a zero of the reciprocal. By Landau's theorem for transforms of nonnegative functions, the abscissa of convergence of G cannot be larger than q (if it were finite and larger it would be a real singularity). Its integral therefore gives a holomorphic function in Re s>q, as does that of F over X≥1 upon subtracting from C/(s−q). The Mellin formula excludes any zero in that half-plane since Γ does not vanish. This contradicts the definition of Θ. The uniform upper bound for the I_k has not been proved.

### First contact: the block of outer zeros must be strictly negative on the kernel

Audit of the attempt to contradict 195:3794 using the true zeros on the line. Assuming a first contact, let V be its complex kernel of dimension D. If there are only r distinct outer pairs under ρ↔1−conj(ρ) (count the positive and negative ordinates separately), then D≤r, without counting multiplicities in r. More precisely the complete contribution of those pairs is negative definite on V. It is a conditional rank restriction, with no advance on the sign.

In the Weil formula for smooth tests, write A_ρ(f) for the evaluation of the two-sided transform at ρ−1/2, with the same convention for its reflection B_ρ. The term of each pair is 2m_ρ Re(A_ρ conj B_ρ) and that of each zero on the line is a square multiplied by its multiplicity. For f∈V approximate in form norm by smooth tests in the window, as in 195:3732. The individual evaluations converge by Cauchy on compact support; the outer block passes to the limit by finiteness. For any finite collection of zeros on the line, the sum of their squares in the limit is therefore ≤ minus the outer contribution of f. For f≠0 at least one of those squares is positive: its entire transform of exponential type has O_f(T) zeros in the disk of radius T by Jensen, while the known density of simple zeros on the line gives ≫T log T distinct evaluations (dependence already audited in P122_003). Strict negativity follows. In particular the map f↦(A_ρ(f)−B_ρ(f)) into C^r is injective on V: on its kernel the outer block is a sum of positive or zero squares. This proves D≤r. The argument uses the finiteness of the outer block for the passage to the limit; with r finite but ≥D it gives no contradiction, and the contact does not come with an upper bound for r. The incorrect identification of the poles of the comparator with the true zeros is not used.

### Two copies of the same profile: the complete critical contribution can tend to zero

Audit of 1342 against 195:33–65. The global index of a finite outer divisor, its saturation and the rates of the eigenvalues were already proved there. For the attempt to dominate the outer pairs by the critical contribution, a more localized obstruction is obtained: having fixed ANY width ℓ>0, there exist two intervals of that width, with growing separation, and tests of fixed norm on them on which the contribution of ALL critical zeros tends to zero. If there are only r distinct outer pairs, r>0, those same intervals admit r directions in which the outer contribution is simultaneously negative definite and of growing magnitude. The profiles can have both polar moments zero.

With the convention F(z)=∫f(t)e^(zt)dt, choose smooth complex ψ_i in (0,ℓ), whose transforms equal one at both points w_i=b_i+iγ_i, −conj(w_i) of pair i, zero at the other pairs and at ±1/2. The interpolation is surjective: a functional annihilating all those evaluations would give a combination of distinct exponentials vanishing on the interval and hence identically zero. Without outer zeros a nonzero profile with both polar moments zero suffices. Take f_{i,T}=ψ_i(·−T)−ψ_i(·+T). Its physical Gram is twice the fixed Gram of the ψ_i for 2T>ℓ, positive definite in the interpolating construction.

There are integers T_n→∞ with exp(iγ T_n)→1 for every zero ordinate at once (recurrence on each finite torus and diagonal choice over the countable set). On the critical axis the translation factor is 2i sin(γT_n); the weighted sum of squares tends to zero by dominated convergence, since the smooth profiles have rapidly decreasing transform and the zero count includes multiplicities. All crossings of the critical Gram in this fixed coefficient space also vanish in the limit. In the outer block only the diagonal remains: its entry i is −2m_i Re[(exp(w_iT_n)−exp(−w_iT_n))²]∼−2m_i exp(2b_iT_n). Here the common positive factor of the normalization is suppressed. Thus not even an arbitrarily small local width, the two vanishing polar moments and the full density of the critical zeros give a floor uniform in the separation. The statement about the critical Gram alone is unconditional. No new arithmetic bound nor contradiction for the actual divisor.

### Two narrow intervals when the outer supremum is attained without a gap

Suppose Δ=sup(Re ρ−1/2)>0. For any width ℓ>0, if there is at least one zero with Re ρ−1/2=Δ, there exist a single smooth profile ψ with support of width <ℓ and integers T_n→∞ such that for f_T=ψ(·−T)−ψ(·+T) the critical contribution tends to zero and exp(−2ΔT_n)W(f_{T_n}) tends to a strictly negative constant. There is no hypothesis of finiteness of the outer divisor nor of separation from the extreme line. If the supremum is not attained, on the other hand, exp(−2ΔT)W(f_T)→0 for EVERY fixed smooth compactly supported profile, with no restriction on T→∞. This second result concerns only the exact normalization. Detection by limsup of exponent for two triangles already appears in 195; here the subject is the normalized amplitude and simultaneously switching off the critical block.

To prevent different pairs on the extreme line from cancelling, start with ψ₀(t)=exp(−ct) on (0,l), zero outside, 0<l<ℓ. For w=b+iγ, 0≤b≤1/2, the product z_w=Φ₀(w)conj Φ₀(−conj w) has positive real part if c is sufficiently large depending on l. Indeed the two factors of the numerator are of the form 1+O(exp(−(c−1/2)l)), and the denominator has real part c²−b²+γ² and imaginary part of modulus 2b|γ|. The ratio of the latter to the real part is ≤b/√(c²−b²). One obtains uniformly Re z_w≥|z_w|/2 and |z_w|≪(1+γ²)⁻¹. Thus the sum of m_w Re z_w over the extreme line converges and is positive. Convolving ψ₀ with a smooth compact approximate identity of sufficiently small support keeps the sum positive by dominated convergence (the multipliers are uniformly bounded in the strip). This gives the smooth ψ of the requested width; the polar moments of 1348 are not imposed here.

Take the simultaneous recurrence of 1348 for all ordinates. The term of an outer pair, up to a common positive normalization, is −2m_w Re[z_w (exp(wT)−exp(−wT))²], now with z_w of the smoothed profile. After normalizing, its limit is zero if b<Δ and −2m_w Re z_w if b=Δ. The majorant 8m_w|z_w| is summable by the zero count and the decay of the transform. This justifies the interchange even if there are b approaching Δ. In the non-attained case, the same majorant works for any T and each term tends to zero in modulus; the critical block is bounded before normalization. Neither of the two conclusions determines whether outer zeros exist.

### Detector with vanishing polar moments and mean: what the comb really contributes

In 1356 the profile ψ can be taken real with Φ(0)=Φ(1/2)=Φ(−1/2)=0, keeping the constant strictly negative if Δ>0 is attained. For this choose the exponential box χ of 1356 with width <ℓ/10 and replace its transform by z(z²−1/4)Φχ(z)^8 (eightfold convolution and a differential operator of order three, with the sign corresponding to the Laplace convention). In the product of reflected evaluations the factor −w²(w²−1/4)² appears. For |Im w|≥14, as happens at the nontrivial zeros of ζ, the modulus of its principal argument is ≤3 arctan(2b|γ|/(γ²−b²))<0.22. Choose c in the box so that the argument of the original product has modulus <1/20 uniformly; after the eighth power it stays <0.4. The real part of the final product is strictly positive and its modulus is O((1+|γ|)^(−10)). Real smoothing by convolution preserves the three zeros and, as in 1356, the positivity of the sum on the extreme line. The passage to smooth ψ may depend on the divisor.

Write C(v)=∫ψ(t+v)ψ(t)dt. In the difference of two copies separated by s=2T the polar part is exactly zero. The archimedean crossing tends to zero by Riemann–Lebesgue applied to the digamma multiplier of logarithmic growth times |ψ̂|². The comb crossing enters with positive sign and a fixed positive normalization factor in front of Σ_{p,k} (log p)p^(−k/2) C(k log p−s); the other shift has no support for s>ℓ. The term k=2 converges to ½∫C=0 by the prime number theorem and partial summation at p≈exp(s/2). For k≥3, each weight p^(−k/2)≪_ℓ exp(−s/2), there are O(s) exponents and for each at most O_ℓ(exp(s/3)) bases with log p=O(s). The total is O_ℓ,ψ(s² exp(−s/6)). Thus the contribution between copies, up to that positive factor, is
    Σ_p (log p)/√p C(log p−s) + o(1).
The self-energy part 2W(ψ) is fixed. This separation of powers uses the classical PNT. What is specific here is keeping at the same time the detector of 1356 and the three vanishings. If Δ>0 is attained, the exponential deficit along the subsequence comes entirely from the primes at the first power. The prime reading remains signed: C(0)>0 and ∫C=(∫ψ)²=0 imply exactly equal positive and negative masses. Eliminating the powers does not prove the lower bound for that reading.

### Precision needed when replacing the prime comb by a subset of genuine primes

Let S(s) be the prime reading of 1364 and let X=exp(s). If a set E with θ_E(Y)=Σ_{p∈E,p≤Y}log p=O(√Y) is omitted, the change of S is uniformly bounded: the support of C only sees [Xe^(−ℓ),Xe^ℓ], where p^(−1/2)≪_ℓ X^(−1/2). For θ_E=o(√Y) the change tends to zero. The order threshold for this absolute guarantee is sharp. For any increasing function g(Y)→∞, g(Y)≤log Y for large Y, there exists E with θ_E(Y)≪√Y g(Y) and a sequence s_j→∞ where the reading of the retained primes tends to −∞. All primes below a prefixed threshold can be kept intact.

First there are arbitrarily large scales with S(s)≤1. The Laplace transform in s≥0, initially for Re z>1/2, is H(z)D(1/2+z) plus an entire correction of compact support in the variable s, where H(z)=∫C(v)e^(zv)dv and D(w)=Σ_p(log p)p^(−w). To continue near the real axis w≥1/2 use D(w)=−ζ′(w)/ζ(w)+ζ′(2w)/ζ(2w) plus a function holomorphic in Re w>1/3 (by the tails of powers ≥3 in the first series and ≥4 in the second). The only possible real poles on that stretch are 1 and 1/2; H(1/2)=H(0)=0 by 1364. If S>1 eventually, its positive tail would have abscissa of convergence between 0 and 1/2 and by Landau a real singularity there, a contradiction. At the endpoint of abscissa zero, the transform of the tail of S remains bounded as z↓0 by the cancellation in H(0), while S>1 on the tail would require an integral ≥exp(−z s₀)/z. This does not use RH.

Choose a fixed interval (a,b) interior to the support with C≥κ>0. At the preceding scales X_j=exp(s_j) sufficiently separated (for example X_{j+1}>10e^(4ℓ)X_j), omit ⌊√X_j g(X_j e^(−ℓ))/log X_j⌋ primes of (X_j e^a,X_j e^b). By the PNT there are enough. Inside the block Y≥X_j e^(−ℓ), and the earlier masses sum in geometric progression using the monotonicity of g: the announced global bound results. No other block affects S(s_j), while block j subtracts ≫g(X_j e^(−ℓ))→∞. Even suppressing all powers of those primes, the reading of squares remains O(1) by the absolute majorant of the PNT and the rest of the powers is o(1). Keeping the original archimedean and polar terms, the energy of the two copies in the modified comb is thus unbounded below.

At the same time the factor ∏_{p∈E}(1−p^(−w)) converges locally absolutely and does not vanish for Re w>1/2, by partial summation with the bound on θ_E. Removing those Euler factors neither creates nor eliminates zeros in that half-plane. Keeping the original archimedean part after changing the product does NOT provide the functional equation of ζ for the modified product. The counterexample measures the sensitivity of the arithmetic reading, without implying a failure of the sign for the complete primes.

### For a fixed real profile, a single two-copy deficit implies unbounded deficits

Let ψ be real smooth compact, F its two-sided transform and V(t)=W(ψ−ψ(·−t)), t≥0, with the complete form. There is an exact dichotomy per profile. If F(w)F(−w)=0 at all centered zeros w of positive real part, V(t)≥0 always and liminf_{t→∞}V(t)=0. Otherwise, if δ is the supremum of the real parts of the zeros with nonzero product, for all 0≤a<δ one has liminf e^(−at)V(t)=−∞. In particular, any negative value, even at small separation, is amplified without changing the original profile. Global detection by two triangles and Landau is already in 195; the detail here is the exact threshold of the prime reading and the treatment of zeros invisible to a given profile.

Proof. In the Weil formula, up to a common positive factor, the summand of w is m_w F(w)F(−w)[2−exp(wt)−exp(−wt)]. One uses that ψ is real and the symmetry of the divisor. If only critical zeros survive the summands are nonnegative and the recurrence of 1348 gives the liminf. In the other case the Laplace transform in t is meromorphic: the series of simple fractions converges locally normally away from the poles by the decay of F in the strip and the zero count. At w of positive real part it has residue −2m_w F(w)F(−w) and it has no poles on the positive real axis. If V(t)≥−C exp(at) eventually, after adding C exp(at) and discarding a compact set one is left with a nonnegative function whose transform is regular at each real >a. Landau implies convergence in Re z>a, incompatible with the visible pole of real part larger than a. The argument holds even if δ is not attained.

For the prime application one can prefix a smooth real ψ of arbitrarily small width with the three moments of 1364 vanishing and with no zeros of F in 0<|Re w|<1/2. Take the infinite convolution of uniform densities centered on intervals of half-widths h_j=h·2^(−j), j≥1. It is a compact probability measure (law of an absolutely convergent sum); its transform is ∏ sinh(h_j w)/(h_j w), with local convergence and no zeros off the imaginary axis since Σh_j²<∞. On the imaginary axis the first n factors give O_n((1+|Im w|)^(−n)) for each n; by inversion the density is smooth. Multiplying the transform by w(w²−1/4) preserves support and smoothness. With this profile, if one writes V(t)=2W(ψ)+k S(t)+o(1) as in 1364, k>0, then the liminf of the prime reading S is exactly −2W(ψ)/k under RH and is −∞ if RH fails. Thus for the genuine primes every finite liminf other than that threshold is unconditionally excluded. The proof does not determine which alternative occurs.

### Quadrature over consecutive primes for the residual reading of two copies

Correction of antecedents of 1382: N_BUILD_015_XI_LANDAU §4 (phase 188) already recorded visible pole ⇒ amplification by Landau, and L2_BUILD_024_SINGLE_PROFILE_BOCHNER already constructed a detector profile without zeros in the open strip. 1382 is kept as a refinement per profile and per exponent.

Let C be the real autocorrelation of 1364 and X=e^s. Denote by p₋,p₊ the primes immediately before and after p. At every large scale X one has, unconditionally,
    S(s)=Σ_p [log p−(p₊−p₋)/2] C(log(p/X))/√p + O_{C,ε}(X^(−251/360+ε)).
The term p=2 does not intervene at large scales. The error quantifies only the replacement of the integral by geometric weights at the exact prime positions.

Indeed f_X(t)=C(log(t/X))/√t has support in [Xe^(−ℓ),Xe^ℓ], second-derivative norm O_C(X^(−5/2)) and integral √X H(1/2)=0. Integrate by linear trapezoids between each pair of consecutive primes p,p₊; each error costs ≤||f_X''||∞(p₊−p)³/12 and each interior node receives the weight (p₊−p₋)/2. Also include the cells crossing the endpoints of the support. Their endpoints are at scale X by Bertrand. In them and in the interior cells, max g≪_C X^(21/40) by Baker–Harman–Pintz and Σg²≪_{C,ε}X^(23/18+ε) by Heath-Brown (both inputs already used in 195:5540 and 6909). Thus Σg³≤max g Σg² and 21/40+23/18−5/2=−251/360.

The majorant by moduli of the remaining subtotal diverges at least as √X/log X at EACH scale. On a fixed interval of v with |C(v)|≥κ>0, there is a subinterval of fixed positive length, uniform in s, where dist(s+v,Z)≥η_C>0 (remove small neighborhoods of the integers and keep the longest component). For large primes (p₊−p₋)/2 is an integer; on that subinterval the modulus of the weight defect is ≥η_C. The PNT on intervals of fixed ratio, uniform for endpoints in a compact set of positive ratios, gives ≫_C X/log X primes. Therefore the compensation between signed defects is missing. The procedure is an application of classical quadrature and of known gap estimates, not an improvement of those estimates nor a bound on the complete subtotal.

### Effective cancellation of the alternating contrast in the prime-gap defects

With the notation of 1390 put g_j=p_{j+1}−p_j and a_j=log p_j−(g_{j−1}+g_j)/2. At every scale X→∞, for real ξ, v_X(t)=t^(−1/2) C(log(t/X))exp(iξ log(t/X)) satisfies
    |Σ_j (−1)^j a_j v_X(p_j)| ≪_{C,ε} (1+|ξ|)X^(−2/9+ε).
In particular the contrast between the two index classes is o(1) for the original detector. Each class contributes half of the signed subtotal plus O(X^(−2/9+ε)). The cost by moduli of the complete subtotal is ≫√X/log X by 1390; the estimate here uses the signs. It does not bound the common component of the two classes.

For the logarithmic term, the partial sums of (−1)^j have modulus ≤1. The total variation of (log t)v_X(t) costs O_C((1+|ξ|)X^(−1/2)log X) upon integrating the derivative over the support. For the geometric term, reassigning each gap to its two endpoints gives exactly Σ_j(−1)^j g_j[v_X(p_j)−v_X(p_{j+1})] before the factor 1/2. The derivative has norm O_C((1+|ξ|)X^(−3/2)); the relevant intervals have Σg_j²≪_{C,ε}X^(23/18+ε) as in 1390. The same geometric bound holds retaining ANY subset of gaps provided both contributions of each gap are retained, uniformly even if the selection depends on X and on the actual sizes. The power saving is not extrapolated to the non-alternating component, where the sum of the two values of the weight appears instead of their difference.

Audit of the Vaughan route: the cancellation of type I by two moments and the pure bilinear residual are already in 920–930. That decomposition is not counted as a new route.

### Absolute contribution of primes with few neighbors on both sides

For 0<η<1/9 let E_η be the fixed set of primes p with fewer than ⌊p^η⌋ other primes in at least one of the intervals [p−√p,p) and (p,p+√p]. Then
    #(E_η∩[X,2X]) ≪_{η,ε} X^(5/18+2η+ε).
Therefore the part of the reading S(log X) of 1364 over E_η, even taking moduli, costs O_{C,η,ε}(X^(−2/9+2η+ε)). In particular for η=1/18 the cost is O(X^(−1/9+ε)). The result uses the square-gap estimate already employed in 1390; here it is applied directly to the genuine prime weight, without attributing to it the cost of its geometric cell.

Proof. Take k=⌈(2X)^η⌉. For p_j in the octave, its k neighbors on both sides lie in [X/2,3X] for large X by the PNT. If p_j is exceptional, one of the two stretches up to the k-th neighbor exceeds √X. In one orientation, by Cauchy and by the multiplicity of each gap in the sum over j,
    Σ_{X≤p_j≤2X}(p_{j+k}−p_j)² ≤ k² Σ_{X/2≤p_i≤3X}(p_{i+1}−p_i)².
The same holds on the left. Divide by X and apply Heath-Brown's 23/18 bound. On the support of the detector there is only a bounded number of octaves comparable to X and each prime weighs O_C(log X/√X), which gives the signed and absolute estimate after adjusting ε.

Moreover Σ_{p∈E_η}(log p)p^(−σ)<∞ for all σ>5/18+2η by dyadic summation. Consequently removing those Euler factors multiplies ζ by a holomorphic factor without zeros up to that half-plane, which reaches strictly to the left of the critical line. This contrasts with the adversarial erasure of 1372. A specific class is eliminated by its genuine mass; the contribution of the primes with enough neighbors on both sides remains uncontrolled.

### A single omission per short window can still accumulate an unbounded deficit

Fix 0<α≤1 and 0<η<1/9. There exists a subset of genuine primes that can be omitted satisfying simultaneously: (i) every interval [u,u+√u (log u)^(1−α)] for large u loses at most ONE prime; (ii) the omitted primes had ≥⌊p^η⌋ original neighbors on each side within distance √p; (iii) the prime reading of 1364 over the retained ones drops below −c(log X)^α along a sequence X→∞. By contrast, with α=0 condition (i) guarantees a change O(1) at any scale. This is the stability threshold under erasure, not the sign of the genuine comb.

Use the well-separated scales X with S(log X)≤1 and the interval [aX,bX] where C(log(p/X))≥κ of 1372, here a,b positive constants. Divide it into cells of length H=√X(log X)^(1−α). There are ≍X/H complete cells. Each empty cell lies in a prime gap g≥H; such a gap contains at most g/H complete cells. Therefore there are O(Σg²/H²)=O(X^(5/18+ε)) empty cells, using the square-gap bound on a fixed enlargement. The cells with primes but only exceptional ones of 1412 are at most O(X^(5/18+2η+ε)). Both costs are o(X/H). Disjoint interiors with unique assignment of endpoints are used, or the mesh is shifted to avoid primes on the edges.

Among the remaining cells, one index class modulo a fixed integer M contains ≫X/H of them. Remove one non-exceptional prime from each cell of that class. The separation exceeds (M−1)H except for possibly equal endpoints, and M can be fixed sufficiently large depending on a,b so that no interval of (i) reaches two omitted primes. Indeed, if the interval reaches a prime of the block, u≪_b X and its length is ≤C_b H; the blocks can be separated by arbitrarily large ratios. The cost in the reading is ≥const·(X/H)log X/√X ≍(log X)^α. The higher powers of the eliminated primes still contribute at most O(1) to the crossing by 1372. For α=0 cover the support of the reading by O(√X/log X) intervals of length ≤min_{u in support}√u log u, each with at most one omission. The total cost is O(1). It is not required to assume RH nor the existence of outer zeros in either direction.
